HaTi — Rules for Claude Code

The owner is not a developer. Explain everything in simple English. Keep summaries short and plain.

Do not rewrite the Bug Fix Rules section without asking the owner first. Updating THE MAP to match the code is encouraged and does not need permission — but say in the summary what changed.

THIS FILE IS THE CONDENSED RULEBOOK (condensed 2026-08-11 and again 2026-09-11, owner-approved both times). It is loaded whole at the start of every session and again after every compaction, so its size is paid for on every task: at 1.3 MB it took a third of the working window before any work began, which is why sessions were compacting twice per task. **Keep it small.** The full history — every war story, quoted bug report and design argument behind these rules — is in docs/MAP-HISTORY.md, with matching section headings. Read the matching section there BEFORE changing anything in that area.

**HOW A NEW LESSON LANDS: the terse rule goes HERE, in a few lines; the full story goes in docs/MAP-HISTORY.md under the same heading.** A section here is the rule, the names that carry it (the one function, the stale names to flag, the tests), and nothing else. If a section here runs past about twelve lines it has stopped being a rule and started being a story — move the story.

## Scope rules

- Do only what the current request asks. Nothing else.
- If you notice a separate problem (broken test, bad code,
  missing file, outdated dependency): DO NOT fix it.
  Write one line in BUGLOG.md under "Noticed, not fixed"
  and carry on with the original task.
- Broken tests that were already failing before this session
  are not your problem. Leave them red.
- If the request is unclear, stop and ask. Do not pick
  the wider interpretation.
- At the end, list anything you touched that was outside
  the request. If that list isn't empty, you broke this rule.

THE OWNER'S OWN WORDS, 24 Aug 2026. They sit ABOVE the Bug Fix Rules because they GOVERN them: Rule 2 says find every place a thing appears; this says do not fix the other things you find on the way. The finding still gets written down — one line in BUGLOG.md, never a fix.

**BUGLOG.md IS 8,000+ LINES OF RUN HISTORY — APPEND, NEVER WRITE.** One section per run, newest at the bottom: the defects found, then a "Noticed, not fixed" list. **READING IT IS DENIED ON PURPOSE** (`.claude/settings.json` denies `Read(./BUGLOG.md)` — it is 455 KB and would swallow a session); writing is not — since 14 Sep 2026 the same file's `permissions.allow` pre-approves the two append forms below by name, after a run in which every append was refused.
- To append: `cat >> ./BUGLOG.md <<'EOF' … EOF` or `tee -a ./BUGLOG.md >/dev/null <<'EOF' … EOF` — these two are pre-approved; any other form is not. Verify with `git diff --stat -- ./BUGLOG.md`.
- To read the house style: `git show HEAD:BUGLOG.md | tail -80` (reads the git object, not the path).
- A refusal on one verb is not a refusal on another — try the verb you actually need. `.claude/settings.json` is strict JSON and cannot carry a comment; an unknown key there can take the hooks down, which is why this note lives here.

## NO NEW BANDS ON THE PAGE — ASK FIRST (owner-asked 26 Aug 2026)

> *"I want to add a rule for claude to stop adding such alerts as attached unless I say so. I am talking about alerts that remind you of something minor assuming people are stupid. Think how SAP would manage such things and not unilaterally add these blinding alerts to the page. I asked for the send all stripe so keep that one. If Claude sees a need to add one, ask me first."*

**NEVER ADD A BAND, STRIP, NOTICE, BANNER, CALLOUT OR TIP TO A PAGE ON YOUR OWN INITIATIVE.** If one looks genuinely needed, ASK — say what it would say and why the screen cannot already say it.

**THE TEST, AND BOTH HALVES MUST PASS**, asked about the SCREEN:
1. Does it say something the screen does not already say? A control, count, heading or status word twelve pixels away that carries the fact makes the band that fact printed twice, and the second printing reads as an alarm.
2. Is it about work owed, or a promise made — and does it carry the act?

**THE READER'S OWN CHOICE, READ BACK TO THEM, IS NEVER A BAND.** A control set to something SAYS so by being set to it. **THE SAP RULE**: spend the least attention that does the job — a transient confirmation for something that just happened, an inline state for what a control already carries, a strip only for context that changes what the reader can do, a blocking dialog only for a decision that cannot proceed without them. Amber that is always there stops being read.

**WHAT STANDS**: the "N not sent" Send all (owner-asked by name — since 26 Aug the button alone, in the column head); the counterparty's wall line (decisions stay on their page until Send); a refusal's way forward on the same screen as the refusal.

**IT CUTS BOTH WAYS: DO NOT DELETE BANDS EITHER.** Removing one is as much a change as adding one — a line in BUGLOG.md and a sentence to the owner, never a fix on the way past.

## THE SIX QUESTIONS, ASKED BEFORE ANY CODE IS WRITTEN (owner-asked 27 Aug 2026)

> *"...first think of how best in class systems in the CLM space approach the issue especially in the integration of AI to support the user, how SAP S/4HANA design in UI and UX would approach the matter, how to make sure it is easy to understand and navigate without adding banners and such for the user, ensuring you are not encroaching on the space allocated to the contract, smartly integrating copilot where copilot is being used and ensuring seamless flows."*

**WHEN THEY RUN**: whenever a change ADDS or MOVES something a person can see or press, or changes what the product SAYS. Never on a fix restoring stated behaviour, a rename, a test. In doubt, run them. **Five of the six carry a REFUSAL — a thing that does not happen without the owner's yes.** Where one bites, say so in a sentence BEFORE building, then build what the owner rules.

1. **THE STANDARD ANSWER.** What does serious contract software do with this shape of problem — where the fact lives, who decides it, what is recorded, what travels, what the reader is asked and when? Follow it or depart on purpose with one sentence of reason. **REFUSES: asserting a named product's behaviour you do not actually know.** Say "the general practice is X".
2. **THE CHEAPEST CHANNEL THAT CARRIES THE FACT.** First rung that works: nothing (already said) → on the control or in the object's header → transient confirmation → strip (only where it changes what the reader can do now) → blocking dialog. Facts in the header, work in a worklist, rules in settings, decisions in a dialog, history in the trail. **REFUSES: a new band, strip, banner, notice, callout or tip** (see NO NEW BANDS).
3. **THE CONTRACT'S PIXELS.** On any screen showing the agreement, measure from the top of the window to the first line of the wording, before and after. **REFUSES: any growth in that number.** Chrome that must grow takes pixels off other chrome, never off the paper. Furniture never follows the reader's text-size setting; the paper always does.
4. **COPILOT'S PLACE.** Beside the wording it is about; resting on something checkable (playbook, precedent, what the other side asked), never its own authority; spending on a press with the cost visible. **REFUSES: any path into the record that is not the funnel a person's edit uses, and any silent failure** — no key, a refusal, a cut-short answer, a cap: each says so where the reader is looking.
5. **THE ONE DOOR.** Does this act already have a way in? Improve that one. **REFUSES: a second door onto an act that already has one** — two doors drift, and two screens disagreeing about what the product does is this codebase's most expensive fault class.
6. **WHERE THE READER ENDS UP.** Walk the whole journey. Is there a way back, does it land where expected, does the number on a door match the list behind it, does a refusal carry its way forward on the same screen?

**THE ANSWERS STAY QUIET.** Work them internally; in the summary mention only the checks that CHANGED what was built, any you could not answer honestly, and any refusal you are asking the owner to lift. **THEY NEVER CHANGE WHAT IS BUILT AND NEVER WIDEN THE JOB**: anything they turn up beyond the request is one line in BUGLOG.md. Refusal 3 is a NUMBER and is not yet pinned in a browser file; the other four are judgements.

## DESIGN OPTIONS, AND THE PAGE CHECKLIST — TWO HABITS BORROWED FROM EMIL KOWALSKI'S SKILLS (owner-asked 24 Sep 2026)

> *"okay i want to borrow this"* — the method of the `prototype` skill and the four questions of the `apple-design` skill in github.com/emilkowalski/skills (MIT), put in HaTi's own words. **Written here, NOT installed**; that repo's other eleven skills are not adopted. "The prototype" elsewhere in this file still means HaTi's own approved reference design.

**DESIGN OPTIONS — how a DESIGN QUESTION is answered**: the owner asks how something should look, or for a new look or a redesign. An exact instruction is built as given, with no round of options.
- **ONE PART OF A PAGE PER ROUND.** "The whole page" is narrowed to the part that matters most; say which, and offer the rest as later rounds.
- **THREE BY DEFAULT, FIVE AT MOST, EACH A REALLY DIFFERENT IDEA** (layout, density, tone, how it is used), **NAMED FOR THAT IDEA** ("Quiet", "Dense"), never A/B/C. Two that differ only in colour or wording are ONE option: replace one, or show fewer and say so.
- **EVERY OPTION IS FINISHED WORK INSIDE HaTi'S OWN SYSTEM** — its tokens, faces and every rule in this file (NO NEW BANDS, THE SIX QUESTIONS, the contract's pixels), realistic HaTi content (contracts, names, figures, never filler text), anything pressable responding. One that breaks a rule is not an option.
- **SHOWN ONE AT A TIME, FULL SIZE, ON ITS REAL PAGE**, all in one Artifact with a switcher to flip between them (a button per option; the number and arrow keys on a keyboard; the flip is instant). Never shrunk side by side: small pictures lie about spacing and size.
- **WITH IT, A TABLE**: each option's idea, when it wins, what it costs; then one recommendation and its reason (who uses the page, and how often).
- **THE OWNER PICKS BY NAME; ONLY THAT ONE IS BUILT.** "Another round around X" draws new options around X.

**THE PAGE CHECKLIST — four questions**, asked of every option before it is shown and of any page the owner asks to have reviewed: (1) Is the most important thing the easiest to see? (2) Are related things grouped together? (3) Does each label say what's behind it? (4) Can you always tell where you are and how to get back? A "no" is fixed before the option is shown, or named as its cost; a review answers the four in plain English, a line each.

## Bug Fix Rules

1. DUPLICATION WARNING: This app draws the same UI in several places. Never assume a fix in one place fixes them all.

2. Before writing ANY code — a bug fix, a new feature, a refactor, or a cleanup — find every place the thing you are changing appears. Do this thoroughly and internally. Do NOT list file paths or locations back to me; I don't need to see them. Just make sure you have actually looked before you start.

3. Fix every place it appears. If you deliberately leave one alone, that decision must reach me in plain English — never silently.

4. Testing rule: test where the USER looks, not where you edited. Verify the result is visible in the actual browser view for every affected place, not just the file you changed.

5. At the end, write a short plain-English summary for a non-developer: what you fixed, whether it is fixed everywhere it appears, anything you deliberately left alone and why, and anything you were unsure about. No file paths, no line numbers, no location lists — just plain sentences about what happened.

## HOW TO TEST ECONOMICALLY (owner-asked 16 and 24 Aug 2026)

**"Do not run the full test suite during incremental edits. Only run the specific test file directly related to the changed code."** Every edit before you believe you are finished is incremental, including fixes to a test you just broke. Run the affected files together in ONE command (`node --test --test-reporter=dot test/<a>.test.js test/<b>.test.js`) until they pass; the full suite runs ONCE when you believe you are finished, and again only if that run found something.

- Full suite: **5m19s**, 8,521 tests in 1,655 suites, 0 red (re-measured 21 Sep 2026; it read 7,056 on 14 Sep and ~4,100 on 21 Aug — the runner is the authority, re-measure before quoting it). The browser set is **39 of 129 red**, the same 39 as the parent (re-measured 21 Sep 2026). A browser file starts a real Chrome and costs 11–40 SECONDS.
- `npm run lint` FIRST — seconds, zero errors is the bar (the ~137 warnings are unused locals; eslint.config.js explains every rule and holds KNOWN_ABSENT). It is the only check that asks "is every name being called a name that exists".
- While working: only the test files the feature's section names. One browser file per screen changed; re-run only after a change to what it measures.
- `node test/chromium/run-all.js` runs the whole browser set (four at a time) — what CI runs, not what you run while working. A file expected to fail is named in KNOWN_RED **with its reason**, printed on every run. **Take a file off that list the day it goes green.**
- **A TEST WHOSE ANSWER DEPENDS ON THE DAY IT RUNS IS WORSE THAN NO TEST.** Fixture dates carrying a calendar claim are built with `monthSpan(off)` or quarter boundaries, never by counting days from today (f183 was red 264 days of 730). Faking node's clock does NOT move jsdom's — rule out the instrument before believing the finding.

## STANDING LESSONS — the defect classes this codebase has paid for repeatedly

Each of these is recorded at length in docs/MAP-HISTORY.md; they are collected here because a new section keeps rediscovering them.

- **THE FILES ARE ES MODULES.** A top-level function is NOT a global; another module reaches it only through the `Object.assign(window, {…})` at the end of its file. A name missing from that list is unreachable, and every caller guarded on `window.foo && …` silently takes its fallback (the rlPaperFootHtml family — a placeholder drew for a year). **f232 is the net**: every `window.foo` READ must be a published name. Its DELIBERATE list is empty; `state` is read BARE everywhere (core.js declares it const; `window.state && …` silently disabled the review gate once). f48 catches the opposite fault (two modules publishing one name) and f232-6 catches one module DECLARING a name another publishes (a hoisted private `lsGet` once captured setView's calls and lost the reader's page on refresh).
- **THE BROWSER HARNESSES DO NOT LOAD index.html.** test/chromium/*.html, test/world.js and test/portalworld.js build their own script lists as CLASSIC scripts — so a new file in js/ is not on them until added, and an unpublished name resolves there when it would not in the product. Run f232 before believing a green browser run on anything that crosses a module boundary.
- **A GUARD THAT IS ALWAYS FALSE, A RULE THAT LOSES A CASCADE FIGHT, AN ATTRIBUTE NOTHING LISTENS FOR, A COMMENT THAT SWALLOWS THE RULE UNDER IT** — four costumes of one fault: nothing errors, nothing logs, the source looks correct. When a rule looks right and the screen looks wrong, MEASURE the computed value in a browser before editing the declaration; fix cascade fights by SCOPE, never `!important`. Never write the CSS comment terminator inside a CSS comment (say "terminator"). Never a backtick in a `<style>` block emitted from a JS template literal — a balanced pair EVALUATES what is between them; f236 is the net.
- **THE COMPILED TAILWIND BLOB IN index.html IS GENERATED.** Never edit it; write overrides in HaTi's own sheet at equal specificity, later in source. Find it by its signature, never a line number. `font-600`, `font-700`, `.sr-only`, `.rounded-full` and four other classes are defined in HaTi's sheet because nothing else defined them.
- **THE GETTER TRAP.** An object literal freezes load-time language; `{...f}` and `Object.fromEntries` INVOKE getters. Use per-key getters and copy by descriptor. A label that is also a RECORD keeps English (RELATION_DOC_WORD, ROLE_LABEL, clauseLabel, audit lines).
- **READING MUST NOT WRITE.** `negoChanges`/`negoAllChanges`/`negoRound`/`negoClauseList` call `negoInit`, which CREATES a negotiation and stamps clause ids. A count or a reading over the book reads `c.changes` and `c.negotiation` RAW.
- **THE LIGHT LIST.** In server mode `state.contracts` is the LIGHT list and HEAVY strips `audit`, an upload's extracted text and more. A reading off the trail is right locally and empty in production (the dashboard's "raised by me", Reports' cycle time, the desk's renewal note). Carry the fact as transport (`_raisedBy`, `_hasBrief`, `_renewalPrep`), stripped on save.
- **THE SERVER IS THE WALL; THE BROWSER IS COSMETICS.** A rule enforced only in pixels holds until somebody sends the request. Guards on PUT /api/contracts/:id are asked as a DIFFERENCE against the STORED record. A route must never read an address out of the body (the open-relay rule) — it looks the person up.
- **"SENT" MUST MEAN SENT.** `mailReport(r)` is the one shape — sent / configured / outbox / error; the outbox is honest delivery, not failure. A `sent_at` stamped whatever the provider did is a lie for the life of the record.
- **A CAP IS A FACT, NEVER A SILENT TRIM.** What was left out is counted and said. An answer cut short (stop_reason) is not an empty answer and is not cached as a whole one. **An absence is stated, never guessed** — null is the honest answer where the record says nothing.
- **A BARE `toast(msg)` PRINTS NOTHING** — kinds are ok / warn / err (TOAST_KINDS, every kind has a dwell, none is zero). An act that leaves the building confirms itself with 'ok'. A stage stub kinder than the real function turns its test into a description.
- **PIN THE RELATION, NOT THE NUMBER.** A test pinning a literal px, colour or count where the claim is a relation costs an edit at every retune and passes on the wrong thing. `test/tokens.js` resolves a token so a test can read a number without typing one.
- **A CHECK THAT PASSES AGAINST THE PARENT IS A DESCRIPTION.** Run a new check against the commit before the fix; a block that hand-writes the shape the product produces passes before the product could produce it. Every driven half of a browser file is GUARDED so a build without the feature REPORTS its failures rather than timing out. A probe that throws proves nothing.
- **PHOTOGRAPH WHAT YOU BUILT.** Read the rendered page, not the source — a card contradicting itself, a dead press, an empty tile with a tick, a rule scoped to the wrong home, are all only visible on screen. Playwright scrolls an element into view before clicking; `.nego-scroll` animates; re-query after a repaint; a probe's own sentinel is not data.
- **THE CLOTHES FOLLOW THE BUILDER.** A control drawn by a shared builder must be dressed by a rule that reaches every home it is drawn in; a rule scoped to one home leaves the other unstyled. Two surfaces may DRAW differently; the READING (which change a clause shows, whose move, is this overdue, which playbook applies) never may — find the question both answer and make it one function.
- **A STANDALONE DOCUMENT CARRIES NO `:root`.** healthreport.js, weekly.js, negoHistoryExportHtml, the memo's clipboard HTML: every value there is a literal; a `var()` resolves to nothing. Charts paint to canvas and need real values too.
- **CHECK THE REMOTE BEFORE MEASURING.** Start from the latest main; "this was already broken" is proved by running the file in a worktree at unmodified main, never asserted.
- **THE COLOUR CENSUS** (theme-tokens-verify, 20 screens × 2 themes) is re-recorded ONLY by somebody deliberately owning a palette change, audited value by value as a SET DIFFERENCE first, in the same commit — never to make a red run go away, and never absorbing somebody else's regression (restore that line by hand). Re-measure before quoting its score.

## THE MAP — how changes get filed (verified 2026-08-03)

Doc Lab is REMOVED — flag any doclab mention as stale.

ONE funnel files every negotiation change: negoFileChange() (js/negotiation.js). Guards that must apply to ALL changes belong here, in this order: deskClaimOnFile, then deskBlockMessage (reversed, no contract can ever start). Wrappers feeding it: negoEditClause, negoInsertClause, negoDeleteClause. Entry paths: direct edit, clause library insert, Copilot (PLUS a shortcut in js/core.js that calls negoFileChange directly — put fixes in the funnel), Playbook (TWO entrances: js/playbook.js and rlFilePlaybookProposal in js/views/negotiation.js), Word DOCX round-trip, the side door in js/views/portal.js that pushes ALREADY-FILED changes wholesale (legitimate — re-verify after any change to the change object's shape).

RULE OF THUMB: a fix touching change objects → `grep -rn "changes.push|negoFileChange(" js/` and account for every hit.

FORMATTING-ONLY CHANGES file with formattingOnly and "Formatting changed — the wording is unchanged". Fingerprints are hashV over the stored rich body verbatim; older versions verify under their own version forever (`NEGO_HASH_VERIFIES` is a SET) — NEVER re-sanitise a stored bodyHtml after filing. Both pending-change renderers (negoDocHtml, redlineDocHtml) carry the formatting-only branch.

THE FUNNEL'S OTHER GUARDS: the no-op guard (nothing changed — also refuses typing the original wording back over a pending ask, and filing the very wording a pending draft already carries); an insertion with no words is refused (a heading alone is not enough); the executed-wording freeze (`negoWordingFrozen = negoExecuted || negoAnySignature`, asked BEFORE the desk rule); the counterparty may not RENAME our clauses (a `modify` from their seat drops `headingText` and keeps the edit); a counter on a clause carrying another side's pending change SUPERSEDES it (see ONE PROPOSAL ON THE TABLE); the clause lock (see ONE CLAUSE, ONE PAIR OF HANDS).

## INTERNAL REVIEW — the step before a change is sent (js/review.js)

A colleague is asked by name, rules on each change (cleared / held on ours, advice on theirs), hands back. A HELD change never travels.
- The hold is enforced in buildSharePayload UNCONDITIONALLY (reviewHeldIds into holdUnsent, no opts flag). THREE send doors ask reviewSendBlock / reviewGateMessage: the share dialog's doSend, reshareToLastRecipient (THROWS), the workbench's #nego-send. A fourth must ask too.
- The counterparty must NEVER learn a review happened. reviewSeatShowsReview() is the one predicate (refuses readonly, side==='counterparty', PORTAL_MODE).
- The gate is a SETTING (admin, OFF by default; per-person since 13 Aug — reviewGateApplies(c,u), rvGateApplies on the server; `reviewChecked(u)` is `!== false`, absence = checked; a named standing reviewer prefills #rv-who). It gates SENDING; the approval chain gates SIGNING; the desk gates REDLINING.
- Traps: read `state` BARE; READING MUST NOT WRITE — only reviewAsk and reviewMark initialise c.review (f59).
- A reviewer may correct wording: folds into the SAME change (revisions[], new fingerprint, revisedBy stamped when reviser ≠ author). f158.
- A review is a CHOSEN SUBSET (rv.changeIds); reviewInOpen(c,ch) is the predicate. SEVERAL REVIEWS AT ONCE, per CHANGE: reviewOpenFor(c,ch) (not reviewOpenOf); one change in at most one review; mark/cancel/return act on a NAMED review; reviewState(c) returns lists from the READER's chair; the banner is a LIST of rows, drawn only while their clauses are pending.
- VISIBILITY: reviewMaySee(rv,u) (reviewer/requester/admin — names flow ONLY through it), reviewMayCancel (requester/admin), reviewActorHeld / reviewActorBlockMessage. Everyone else sees status without a name. The audit trail still names reviewers; it never travels.
- A reviewer is NARROWED while their ask is open (rlActorHeld view-side, asked by FIVE canAct renderers: negoLiveCardsHtml, negoHeadHtml, negoPanesHtml, negoRoomActionsHtml, redlineChangeCardsHtml; the model refuses too). Their column narrows to reviewMyChangeIds / rlMyCardIds (four lists); the DOCUMENT folds to their clauses (rlRvDocClauses, both doc renderers; _rlRvFullDoc per sitting, one delegated listener). Round-governing controls undrawn. ONE hand-back door: the toolbar (openReviewReturnPicker; reviewTagsFor names CHANGE ids).
- reviewInPlay(c,rv) is the ONE population (the review's still-pending changes) for banner, progress, refusal, posture, server rvInPlay. A review with nothing in play is SPENT (reviewSpent) and stops drawing and narrowing; the server keeps the raw rvOpenList at its structural guards.
- Details: RV_NOTE_MAX; the picker is a COMBOBOX (name AND email); reviewCandidates refuses a person who cannot open the contract (folderScopeFor on the server, f160). Never `class="ui-input"` — use HATI_FLD / HATI_LBL (js/review.js keeps its own pair on purpose, reading `var(--field-*)`). reviewDialogHeadHtml builds every dialog head here and in js/desk.js (.rvd-* classes in index.html, f175).
- DELIVERY AND CANCEL: reviewNoteDelivery files on rv.notice and the trail; reviewDeliveryState(rv) is what screens print (null on an older review). The link is contractUrl(req,id,tab) (openFromHash honours 'redline'). Cancel is in the notice (unfolded while reviewWantsAttention) and reviewCardCancelHtml on both card renderers, with reviewCancelCost on the confirm. f186, f155, f172.
- WHO MAY REOPEN: a refusal is reopened by the side that GAVE it, never the side that received it. Our page: Reopen on THEIR refused change; Withdraw on OURS. Their page mirrors.
- Cards: ONE status slot; HELD = ruby, OUT FOR REVIEW = amber; reviewWithheldIds() is the union the payload subtracts. Gate OFF → Send WARNS (reviewSendWarning), never both mechanisms. Every unsent card carries a scoped ask (data-rl-ask-review); the toolbar button is always a door (ask ↔ hand-back); the ask posture opens openReviewEntryChooser ("Assign contributors" / "Send for review"). f171.
- THE SERVER IS THE AUTHORITY: rvOpenList / rvOpenFor / rvWithheldIds / rvActorHeld / rvUnreviewedIds off the STORED contract. POST /api/shares refuses a sender holding an open review, refuses unreviewed content when the gate is on, STRIPS held/out-for-review and returns withheldByReview. PUT guards as a DIFFERENCE (verdict by the named reviewer only, cancel by requester/admin, hand-back by the reviewer, no edits to an open review's ids). DECIDING ≠ RECEIVING: negoResolve records the decider; inbound decisions pass. If browser and server disagree, f162 is right.
- "WAITING ON THEM" HAS TO BE TRUE: negoTheirCopy(c) answers 'live' / 'none' / 'unknown' (PORTAL_MODE always 'unknown'); negWhoseMove asks negoUnsentAsks FIRST (why:'unsent') and where they hold no copy the move is ours (why:'nocopy'). The share cache flag is ON THE CONTRACT (c._shareFetch). f190.
- WHICH LINK IS THEIRS: shareIsStanding(s) (durable, not revoked, not expired — the client's reading of what PUT /api/shares/:token/payload accepts); standingShareFor(shares,contact) for the ROUND SEND ONLY (the link the contact came from, then address, then name, then newest standing); the share dialog matches the typed address and gets NO fallback. A round send catches up every other standing link; a stranded second link is said out loud (reshareStrandedLine, four surfaces). f17.
- Live-link catch-up: refreshLiveShareQuietly (js/core.js) is the one updater; WHOEVER MOUNTS the component supplies onDecided/onWithdraw. Only ANSWERS travel down a live link. f174.

Tests: f154–f159, f161, f162, f164, f186, f190.

## KEY TERMS HAS A DIVIDER, AND THE RIGHT-HAND CARD SCROLLS (owner-asked 19 Aug 2026; **SUPERSEDED 16 Sep 2026 — see THE OVERVIEW. `.terms-grid`, `#kt-resizer`, ktFitSplit and ktWireSplit are STALE; the negotiation page's and the builder's dividers are ports of the same mechanism and are LIVE**)

`.terms-grid` is card · handle · card. The RIGHT column stretches to the grid and scrolls inside itself (`#kt-side`); the LEFT card is its own height. **The column takes the height, the card in it takes its content: `flex:0 0 auto` — SHRINK IS 0** (10 Sep 2026: shrink 1 let cards be squeezed below content height and spill over each other instead of scrolling; `#renewal-host` too). It is the negotiation divider's own mechanism (ktFitSplit / ktWireSplit mirror rlLayoutResizer: pointer position, never distance travelled; KT_LEFT_MIN 320 / KT_RIGHT_MIN 300; `hati.v1.ktLeftFrac`; arrows 2%, double-click resets). Below 980px it stacks and the fraction is READ, NEVER WRITTEN. Bound once per element (dataset.ktSplitBound). Tests: f176, f280 (1)-(2), amendment-journey-verify.

## AN AMENDMENT IS WRITTEN HERE, NOT ONLY FILED HERE (js/family.js, owner-asked 14 Aug 2026)

- Agreement family card sits in the Key terms side column; Obligations moved to a row on the Checks card (its panel BORROWS `obligations-section` by id). `editableFor=kind=>mayEdit&&(kind==='oblig'||kind==='brief'||c.status!=='Signed')`. obligationSurfacesChanged is the funnel that repaints every obligation surface.
- `fa_add_amendment` is RETIRED. Acts in `.fam-acts`: Create an amendment + Link an existing document where a document can have children; "Link to a parent agreement" on a STANDALONE only; a child gets Unlink alone.
- Create MINTS A DRAFT AND FILES IT IN THE SAME BREATH. Carried: counterparty, address, party, stream, letterhead, valueType. NOT carried: value, effective date, obligations, signers. Name keeps ENGLISH (RELATION_DOC_WORD), numbered PER KIND (amendmentOrdinal). Body always written (skeleton of four English paragraphs, or FAMILY_BLANK_BODY). Lands on the Document tab — the one creation site exempt from roomOpenOnTerms (f170 names it).
- docHasNoWording / docNothingWrittenHtml say so in the corner, never on the paper.

## THE GAP-MAP BATCH — five services and the AI brain (18–19 Aug 2026, WORKORDER-gap-map.md)

- THE NUDGE reaches the assignee where they resolve to a MEMBER (obligationRecipient: email then name; only a member's own address is written to) at 7 / 0 / -1 days, in their language; admins on day 4 or where nobody resolves. f212.

POST /api/ai/brief: deep tier, editor-and-up, cached per wording-hash in the BRIEFS table, rides GETs as `_brief` (stripped on PUT/save/share; money section removed without canViewValues); `_hasBrief` rides the LIST. briefMark(text) is deterministic emphasis, escaped before marked; watchouts amber, unusual grey. **A cut-short brief IS KEPT since 10 Sep 2026, flagged `truncated`; every reader says it is partial and offers a rewrite (`force`).** aiNoteRead(c, action, detail) writes nothing to a sealed record. f213, f219.
- THE DAILY BRIEF: runDailyBriefs beside runReminders under its own catch; per member, once per day (reminders table); briefCadence(u) / briefCadenceOf(u): daily · weekly · off, ABSENT = daily, old `dailyBrief===false` reads 'off'; weekly keyed on the week's Monday. effExpiryReader is shared. f214.
- ASK-YOUR-BOOK FROM THE PALETTE: Cmd/Ctrl+K "In the wording" off GET /api/search plus an "Ask Copilot" handoff row LAST; the palette calls no AI route. f215.
- THE ARCHIVE SHELF: c.archived={at,by} is a filing fact beside status (not in EXECUTED_IMMUTABLE). contractSetArchived is the ONE act (register ⋯ and room ⋯). Off every default list and count (regFiltered's one pair of lines, hmDashSlices at its one door, negoIsLive, calendar, reports, sweeps); still in FTS. f216.
- TWO-STEP SIGN-IN: RFC 6238 TOTP in node crypto; enrolment proven by a first code; ten recovery codes; password alone yields a five-minute TICKET, POST /api/login/totp turns it into a session; turning off costs a code; admin `clearTwoStep` on PATCH /api/users/:id (refused on yourself). f217.

## MONEY IN ITS OWN CURRENCY (W2-1, owner-ruled 19 Aug 2026)

A contract states its OWN currency; REPORTING converts. `fmtMoneyOf(c)` / `fmtMoneyShortOf(c)` print the contract's own code (fmtMoneyShort(n) stays for thresholds and caps); every figure that ADDS contracts converts through `fxHomeValue`.
- ONE arithmetic in js/jurisdiction.js; the SERVER INJECTS ITS DATA (fxSetRatesReader / fxSetHomeReader) and keeps no copy (f218 greps).
- contractCurrency(c): a real three-letter code on metadata.currency wins; anything else falls back to the workspace currency.
- NO RATE IS EVER GUESSED: fxHome(c) → {v, code, converted, missing}; fxMissing(list) is the reportable omission and rides /api/stats and /api/analytics; the dashboard prints `fx_left_out_*`.
- The rate is an admin's dated claim under appSettings.fxRates, written ONLY by PUT /api/settings/fx-rates, preserved through an ordinary settings save. Never a live feed. FX_CURRENCIES + fxPickerCodes() (the book's own currencies first) feed a datalist that OFFERS, NEVER REFUSES.
- Guards compare like with like (signCapBlocker, the server's wall reading the STORED currency — or the larger of stored and claimed — buildApprovalChain's value condition); the unconvertible case REFUSES in words (sc_block_norate) or ENGAGES the rule. Stored values are never rewritten.
Tests: f218, settings-tabs-verify.

## ONE PROPOSAL ON THE TABLE (owner-approved 15 Aug 2026 — WORKORDER-competing-redlines.md)

- **B — A COUNTER TAKES THE TABLE.** In negoFileChange, a filing on a clause carrying a pending change the fold does not cover sets it `status:'superseded'` + `supersededBy`, stamps `counterOf`, writes an audit line. Insertions exempt both ways. A superseded unsent draft of ours toasts (ng_draft_superseded). `counterOf` travels; the superseded change does not. negoAdvanceRound archives superseded. negoCounterLineHtml is a `return ''` STUB (owner: no more lines on the card); `ng_counters_line` / `.rl-counterline` are stale.
- **A — THE PAPER TELLS THE TRUTH.** Both renderers' `byClause` is a LIST per clause; every change draws its own tag; the jump anchor carries every id, LEAD FIRST, matched with `[~=]`; single-id consumers take the first token. No migration.
- **C — THE GUARD.** negoResolve refuses to ACCEPT a change whose clause carries a DIFFERENT accepted change in the SAME ROUND measured ALIKE (ng_accept_blocked_adopted). **negoClauseNowById** is the clause as SHOWN (baseline + adopted); negoEditClause / negoDeleteClause / the editor's seed all measure from it; `oldText` says what a change was measured against; **negoMeasuredAlike** is the one predicate (alike = rivals; different = written on top, and negoBuildBody composes them untouched). Mirror guard: reopening an accepted change a later one was measured on is refused (ng_reopen_blocked_downstream).
- THE PAPER SHOWS WHAT WAS ADOPTED: the canvas draws the newest change measured against what NOW STANDS, else the LAST ADOPTED change (never re-diffed). Reopen for a settled ask lives where the settled change is still visible (the clause panel's history rows / the accepted card), settled only, our seat only.
Tests: f207, f208, settled-ask-reopen-verify.

## THE NEGOTIATION DESK — who works this one (js/desk.js)

Four seats: Initiator (grants nothing), Lead (exactly one — the only person who reaches the counterparty), Contributor (full hands, nothing travels), Reader. Claimed by the FIRST change filed on our side (deskClaimOnFile). deskMayRedline / deskMaySend are TRUE on exactly FOUR escapes: rule off, no desk claimed, nobody signed in, PORTAL_MODE. Admin setting, OFF by default. An open review IS a seat (deskHasSeat). rlActorHeld answers for two postures (mid-review, not-the-lead); rlMayRedline is the hands question. ONE notice slot: rlOneNoticeHtml; phone: mDocNoticesHtml. Server: deskRuleOn / deskSeatOf / ourChangesTouched / rosterMoved as a difference. What travels: sharedBy is the LEAD plus leadNotice; roster, join requests, stale flag never travel — but individual colleagues ARE named (author, `by`, comment authors); what is walled is the review and `resolvedBy`. deskStale / deskStaleInboxFor / deskLedBy.

- THE DESK GATES REDLINING AND SENDING, NEVER SIGNING (12 Aug 2026): signBlockers(c) (js/views/contract.js) is the ONE list for renderSignButton and signDocument — approval (the rule steps, and since 23 Sep 2026 a named person's yes, the `signapproval` rows), whose turn, the negotiation, the signers, the readiness BLANKS, the template form, the cap, the folder, the spots, the check's holds (the intent tick LEFT it 13 Sep 2026 — it is the pad's first line). Never desk, never review. Each blocker prints its own sentence; the button never promises. Tests: f165–f169, sign-links-verify section 5.

## THE PHONE

Below 768px the desktop shell hides and js/mobile*.js draws instead. NOT a fork: it reads the same readings (hmDashSlices, regFiltered/regState, wsNextAction, negoTimeline, buildSharePayload…) and files NO changes of its own (grep the mobile files for changes.push / negoFileChange and find nothing). Ask whether the thing you change is drawn on the phone too: a fix in a shared FUNCTION reaches both shells, a fix in a desktop RENDERER does not. It reuses rlSelMenu, rlAiPropose and renderNegotiationsList unchanged. Bottom bar: Home / Contracts / Negotiate / Approvals — labels floored at 14px (phone-verify); a longer word is paid for out of the WORD.

Registered alert kind `obligation` (the reminder mails' own 7-day window; undated obligations excluded). KPI_CATALOG entry *Obligations due*, not in the default four. f253.
- **COMPLETION MEANS SOMETHING (J-2.2)**: `completedAt` · `completedBy` · `completedNote` · `seriesId` on the obligation, `obligationsReadAt` / `obligationsReadHash` on the contract; ABSENT = UNKNOWN, no migration, no inferred date. `obligationMarkDone` is the ONE writer (called by toggleObligation only); forward-dated completion refused; reopening clears it. A repeating duty opens EXACTLY ONE next instance with its OWN ID (`obligationNextInstance`; the reminder dedupe key is `${c.id}:ob:${o.id||due}` — a reused id silences reminders); one cadence step from the DUE date, month clamped; asked of the LIST (`obligationSeriesOpenAt`), not the press. `obligationsReadStamp` is written only where a reading happened, above `OBLIG_TEXT_MIN` — by the scan and by auto-triage (two callers, f254). The Insights blind spots close (`canSeeScan`, `canSeeCompletedOn`). f254.

It asks before it sends; a refusal writes nothing. The chase carries the standing SHARE link where there is one and no link where there is none; obligations still never travel. The table names its columns (sticky head), cuts the description to ONE line (whole wording on the hover), verbs reserve one width. f255, f263, obligations-tab-verify.

## PAYMENT TERMS, TURNED INTO A NUMBER — the Insights payment-terms tab (owner-ruled 2 Sep 2026)

- `js/payterms.js` is the reading (its own file, load-bearing: two views read it). It reads `metadata.paymentTerms` — the RECORD, never the wording — so "not recorded" means nobody has read this contract. Three other parsers (playbook, precedent) are deliberately untouched.
- The standard is the PLAYBOOK's per contract kind (PAY_STD_FALLBACK pinned to DEFAULT_PLAYBOOK by f267); over-standard is asked of each contract's OWN standard. `isMonetary` decides whether payment terms exist at all. Nothing is folded away: every contract lands in exactly one pile.
- The average is value-weighted where money is visible and EVERY side with rows can be weighted, else a straight mean — ONE basis for the page, and `basis` says which.
- TWO TARGETS, one per side (`payTargets()` reads `settings.payTargets`; null = the playbook answers as before; a set target wins for its side). The drivers are one contract at a time: bring this one onto its side's target and the gap closes by N days (`gapAway` computed on every row); a contract can be an exception and not a driver. No shaded region: a boundary is a LINE per side. Over-standard bands marked in RUBY (never amber, which is a side here).
- ONE TABLE with the facts as columns (`payTermsData().rows`, ranked by the gap), every column a FRACTION (a shared template string is not a shared layout), the card holds what is AGAINST you (`disadvantage`, owner-ruled: on the supplier side "over" and "against you" are opposites), paged to the chart's own measured height (`ptFitTable`), the pager is the register's shape. A BAR IS A CONTROL (a real button, disabled when empty; press the live cut again to clear); a press repaints the body (`ptRepaint`), never the view; the cut is per sitting and says so. Payment terms is a Contracts filter in REG_BAR_FILTERS (not default), borrowing PAY_BUCKETS / payBucketOf; `none` = not recorded. Payment terms is also a graph lens on all four doors (borrowing payBucketOf). The honest limit card: HaTi reads agreements, not your bank.
- The Home tile counts BOTH sides and names the halves apart, borrows payOverStandard, lands on the TAB via intelGoTab. `pt_exc_title` is gone.
Tests: f267, payment-terms-verify.

## DRAFT FROM A SENTENCE (owner-asked 9 Sep 2026)

A FOURTH row in the `+ Draft new agreement` menu; "Draft from a template" is UNTOUCHED and still first (f270 and the browser file assert it as a control). `js/draft.js` MINTS NOTHING — it presses a door that already exists (the wizard's answer step, the saved template's fill, the essentials form); f270 greps it for every creator. Candidates are the templates the ordinary picker offers (myCreatableTemplates, customTemplates, tplLibPublished). `POST /api/ai/draft` returns the template AND its answers from ONE reading, editor-only, no folder scope (template ids are not contract ids); `/api/ai/template` is NOT widened. A value may only reach a box the chosen template declares — dropped on the route AND in `draftApplyPrefill` (the ONE prefill reading, copied by DESCRIPTOR, three readers). Nothing arrives unseen: every value lands on a field's default in an editable box. What was filled is NAMED, never valued. "Nothing fits" is an answer; the row draws either way. The prompt's one job is not to guess. Spend named `draft` on arrival. The menu row is English like its siblings. Copilot drafts no clause. Tests: f270, draft-from-a-sentence-verify.

## PLAIN ENGLISH BESIDE THE CONTRACT (Young ruled 9–10 Sep 2026)

A switch on the Document tab — **Contract View first and lit at rest, Plain English second** (**and X-ray third since 22 Sep 2026 — see X-RAY below**) — that covers the right-hand column (`#doc-read` absolutely positioned over `#doc-right`, which takes `visibility:hidden`; the cards are COVERED, never rebuilt) with a clause-for-clause edition of the agreement. The contract does not move by a pixel (measured, 293).
- **THE WALK IS THE SHEET'S OWN.** The anchors are read off what is PAINTED (real `<h*>`, bold numbered lead-ins like "1.1", a heading's own number via `_docReadHeadNum` — refuses far more than it accepts: a bare number needs `.` or `)` and ≤2 digits, or a self-naming word). The front matter is not a clause (two readings: a real `<header>`, or the first heading being the contract's NAME). The number is the PAPER's, never asked of the model; a heading's number goes on `cite`, NEVER on `num` (num is what the route is sent and hashed). Pairing is by number and checked against the heading's own words; a mismatch draws NOTHING. Measured from the CLIP, never the scroller; the two columns are kept in step by a transform, never a second scroller.
- **IT IS NEVER PART OF THE DOCUMENT**: painted after the canvas on `wireDocCanvas`; its own `clause_readings` table, hashed on exactly the text sent, riding as `_readings`, stripped on PUT/save/share; a cut-short answer is not cached. READ_MAX_CLAUSES 60, counted and said. `readings` named in the spend ledger.
- **THE PROMPT IS THE FEATURE**: everyday words, TRANSLATE DO NOT SUMMARISE, one entry per clause, keep every figure, never advise or redraft, an empty reading is right for a cover page; **no headings are asked of the model** (the drafter's own are printed; `ownHead` is the name to print — the bold lead-in or NOTHING; `heading` is the pairing reading and is untouched). A SECTION row survives without one.
- The edition is a white sheet set like the contract: the paper's own size measured on every paint, the same gutter and steps (`hati-lv-N`, read off the DOM via `docReadShape`, never stored), level with its own clause stepping down rather than overlapping, the number BESIDE the reading in the same 2.6em gutter. `docReadSig` decides whether the press asks the route again (moved wording asks; the route's cache answers).
- The box is saved (blurred) before the swap; below 1024px not drawn; no clauses, no switch; nothing on the phone or the counterparty's page or the Signing tab.
- A WORKING TEXT IS A DOCUMENT: `docBodyHtml`'s plain branch lifts through `docRichFromText` (the negotiation's own) and down `renderDocHtml`, so the Document tab and the negotiate page cannot disagree about shape; lifts for the SCREEN only; a stage without js/docx.js falls back to `documentTextHtml` (which also stamps `doc-t-h` / `doc-t-n` so the walk can read that shape too). A ruled block keeps its columns (repaired in the render, never in `docBlocksFromText`; one lift for the whole text; `.hati-doc pre` scrolls inside itself). `docBodyCarriesTop` has two signals (a real `<h1>`, or the NAME as an opening block) and the head still draws where the wording carries no top (an amendment skeleton).
Tests: f277, plain-english-verify.

## TWO DOORS, TWO SENTENCES, ONE KEY (10 Sep 2026)

`act_next` was duplicated (stray removed); `co_password_updated` was two different messages under one key — the last literal wins, so the self-service change said "please sign in". `co_password_changed` is the other; the reading is written beside the key. `npm run lint` is at 0 errors. f148.

## ONE CLAUSE, ONE PAIR OF HANDS (Young asked 10 Sep 2026)

An advisory clause lock: two minutes, expires, always names the holder, released on the way out. `c.locks` is an ordinary field, absent on every record on file. **Its one route is `POST /api/contracts/:id/lock`**, which MERGES one clause on the stored record, takes no baseVersion, moves neither `version` nor `updated_at`; the PUT KEEPS THE STORED MAP (contractSaveKeepsLocks) — nothing else may write it. Never travels (not on the payload allow-list; the reading refuses PORTAL_MODE). **`clauseLockSign` is the ONE reading and every door asks it at draw time**: the pencil becomes the monogram sign (not a button, always drawn, label shade never amber, the whole name on the hover and aria-label); the card's Edit and the panel's Copilot button go `disabled` and swap their mark (dead doors may not keep Copilot's violet — fixed by SCOPE). `clauseLockRelease` refuses somebody else's. The server refuses a save that adds/rewords/re-decides one of OUR changes on a clause another member holds. A heartbeat well under half the window while an editor is open (its own tiny write). Reaches the other browser on the twelve-second `GET /api/contracts/:id/state` probe (`clauseLockMerge` replaces our map; a QUIET repaint, never while an editor is open). Not pushed — the server's refusal is the wall. A sealed record takes no courtesy write. Probe lessons: re-query after a repaint; say whether the thing or the staging is missing. Tests: f289, redline-verify 25.

## THE PLAIN ENGLISH EDITION IS SET LIKE THE CONTRACT, THE STRUCTURE GOES OUT AS WELL AS IN, THE BAR SPEAKS THE CONTRACT'S OWN LANGUAGE (10 Sep 2026)

- **DOCX WRITER** (f288): a hanging indent is `w:ind` + a left TAB STOP + a real TAB between marker and wording (`_dxSpace` keeps a tab; `_dxRun` emits `w:tab`). The shape is read off the markup (`hati-lv-N`, `rl-hang`, `hati-tight`, `hati-pb`, `hati-toc`), never re-derived. `DOCX_STEP` is Word's half inch; `DOCX_TOC_STOP` is derived from `DOCX_SECT`. `Normal` carries `after="160"`; headings `keepNext`. A clause struck whole marks its paragraph mark deleted (only where every run is a deletion). Fonts, page size, margins, headers, footers, images still not carried. Proved as a read → export → read-back loop on the owner's contract.
- **THE WRITING BAR** (f287): the four shape tools write CHARACTERS, not markup — a marker is text in the paragraph, the level is `hati-lv-1..3`, the marker reading has ONE home (`redlineSplitMarker` through window). Numbers continue the contract's own sequence (nearest block above at the same step, stopping at a heading or shallower step); the marker shape follows the step (2.1 → (a) → (i), level 3 bracketed number); a bullet follows the step and a number never does; both are toggles judged on the first block; the empty element a cut marker leaves is removed. The rest of the bar is `execCommand`. Two bars (work mode's `richBarPress` and the room's inline editor) call one set of hands. `RICH_SHAPE_KEYS` names the presses that owe a repaint. The step has ONE emitter (`redlineOpsBlocksHtml` used to stamp `rl-hang-N` — retired; the Compare dialog gains the step rule). An indent with no wording change files as `formattingOnly`.

## THE NEGOTIATE PAGE WEARS THE DOCUMENT'S OWN DESIGN (Young ruled 11 Sep 2026)

*"even company standard contracts look different especially the font"*, then *"Make the Negotiate page use the document's style."* That page was built beside the design feature and never told about it: the rules named `.doc-surface` (the DOCUMENT TAB's article) and its paper is `.rl-paper` with no `data-doc-body` ancestor. MEASURED on one contract in `formal-legal`, both pages: Times New Roman + justified vs IBM Plex Sans + ragged.

- **THE HOOK ON THE WRAPPER, THE DRESSING ON THE SHEET** — the rules read the hook as an ANCESTOR, so it cannot be the paper itself. Read through `window` with a guard (branding.js is not on every stage).
- **`:is(.doc-surface,.rl-paper)` IS THE WIDENING.** `.rl-paper` is NOT given `.doc-surface`: that class sets size, leading, ink and letterfit too, and `.nego-doc` sets them at EQUAL specificity — the winner would be whichever sheet was injected first. `:is()` takes its most specific argument's weight, so no selector changes what it already matched.
- **THE HEADING RULES NAME THAT PAGE'S OWN ELEMENTS.** Widening the FACE alone left half the style behind: that page draws no `h1`/`h2` at all — every clause heading is rebuilt as `h4.rl-clause-h` (so a renamed heading carries its own marks) and the title is `h3.rl-paper-title`. Same words, same role, different tag; `.rl-clause-h` holds `cl.headingText` verbatim.
- **SIZES DO NOT TRAVEL**, deliberately — that page pins its own four classes deep and keeps the owner's proportions. A letterfit in `em` therefore lands on different pixels per page; pin the PROPORTION.
- **DESIGN ONLY, NEVER STRUCTURE** — `docDesignBodyAttr` is the design half, and `docDesignPaperAttr` is composed FROM it. `two-column` would break clause-by-position pairing, `margin-numbers` fights the negotiation's numbering, `contents-first` prepends to the baseline changes are filed against. Asserted twice as walls.
- **THE PAPER WEARS IT; THE FURNITURE DOES NOT** — one rule names the pencil, the lock monogram and the editor's bars. **`:not(.rl-clause-h)` carries weight as well as meaning**: the design rule scores (0,3,2) (its three `:not()`s each count), so the pin written plainly LOSES while looking correct. Do not remove it to tidy the selector.
- Reaches the clause editor and the counterparty's page by construction (both mount `redlineDocHtml`). The 10 Sep upload order's other three items: the header block is GONE and the 13px wording is the sheet's (C-7, 11 Sep — see THE UPLOAD IS ITS OWN PAPER); the file strip stays by the owner's word. Tests: f129 (9), negotiate-design-verify (every claim a RELATION, never a typeface by name).

## THE CLAUSE NUMBER IS NOT WELDED TO ITS NAME (Young reported it 11 Sep 2026)

The paper read "4. Independent Contractor" and the Plain English edition read "4Independent Contractor". TWO HALVES: the reading that cuts a number off a heading discards the drafter's punctuation — correctly, it captures a CITATION — and nothing put it back where the number is PRINTED; and the rule that would separate them gives the number a 2.6em box, which only fires where the clause HANGS its marker in a gutter, which most commercial paper's headings do not.

- **`_docReadSepOf`** takes the separator off the SOURCE, never invents one — the character after the number, only `.`, `)` or `:`. One reading, three producers (a heading, a mark, a numbered paragraph).
- **IT MAY NOT JOIN `num`** — that is what `docReadClauses` SENDS, and the route's cache key is a hash of exactly that; fold the dot in and every contract already read pays for an identical re-read. It travels beside `cite`, for `cite`'s own reason. f277 (17) pins that as a wall.
- **THE GAP IS A GUARANTEE where the punctuation is a quotation** — stated in the stylesheet, and the two gutter rules take it back (there the number's box is what every wrapped line hangs from).
- Tests: f277 (17), plain-english-verify section 14 (a GEOMETRY — the markup was correct either way; only a rendered page knows whether two painted words touch). f277 (13)'s painter claim re-pointed: it sliced 900 characters back from an ANCHOR rather than to a BOUNDARY. **Pin the region, not a byte count.**

## THE EDITION IS SET IN THE CONTRACT'S OWN FACE (Young asked 11 Sep 2026)

*"please make the font in the plain english page the same as the contract page."* MEASURED on a contract set to Formal legal: the sheet drew Times New Roman and the edition beside it drew IBM Plex Sans. **THE CAUSE IS STRUCTURAL — `#doc-read` is a SIBLING of the paper** (it sits in the grid's second track), so the `[data-doc-body]` hook every design rule reads as an ancestor is not above it.

- **THE FACE IS MEASURED OFF THE SHEET, exactly as the SIZE beside it already is and in the same two lines** — `docReadPaint` reads the paper's computed style and writes `--dr-face`. **WIDENING THE NINE DESIGN RULES TO NAME THIS SHEET WAS THE OTHER ANSWER AND IS THE WORSE ONE**: a list that has to be kept in step for ever, where a design added tomorrow would dress the contract and not its translation. A measurement is a RELATION.
- **THE ENTRY'S OWN HEADINGS HAD TO BE NAMED, and `inherit` is what names them.** They are real h3/h4 and index.html sets a face on every heading tag at (0,0,1) — **inheritance is not a cascade contest**, so any matching declaration beats it and the measured face never reached them. `inherit` says *whatever this entry is set in* rather than repeating the token, so the two cannot drift. **The paper's own headings escape it only because the design rules name h1/h2 by tag.**
- **THE COLUMN'S OWN "PLAIN ENGLISH" CAPTION KEEPS THE PRODUCT'S FACE** — furniture ABOUT the reading rather than part of it — and that holds by construction: `--dr-face` is read by `.doc-read-note` and by nothing else.
- **THE WALL: what the route is sent does not move by a byte.** The reading's cache key is a hash of exactly what it was handed, so a face measured for the screen never reaches it.
- Tests: f277 (18) (**4 of 5 fail at the parent**; the fifth is the named WALL), plain-english-verify section 15 — a design is STAGED, or "they match" is satisfied by a contract that has none; **15a is the CONTROL that proves the stage bites, and 15d/15e are what prove the change is narrow. Against the parent 15b reports the report verbatim: `contract Times New Roman · edition IBM Plex Sans`.** The heading half (15c) was found by MEASURING a rendered page — the paragraph beside it was already right, so nothing in the source looked wrong.

## THE FILE'S OWN SHAPE SURVIVES THE UPLOAD, and ONE GUTTER ON EVERY SCREEN (Young asked 10 Sep 2026)

- **THE DOCX READER** (f286): the ladder is the file's own, `docxIndentStep` MEASURED (commonest positive hanging indent), `left - hanging` is where the line begins, bounded at three; LEVEL 0 CARRIES NO CLASS (the whole migration story); the FILE OUTRANKS THE MARKER (`redlineHangHtml` adds its gutter and leaves a stated level alone); ONE step vocabulary `hati-lv-1..3` (`rl-hang-2/3` retired); `hati-tight`, `hati-pb`, `hati-toc` read and never guessed; a contents row refuses rather than guesses; the allow-list widens by `class` on P filtered against `RICH_SHAPE_CLASSES` (a fixed set, per name; `style` still refused); not one word moves; `.hati-doc > h1:first-child` is the centred title and every other h1 is a clause heading. The title is not guessed.
- **THE GUTTER** (f285): `redlineHangHtml` (js/redline.js, beside RL_MARKER / redlineSplitMarker / redlineMarkerDepth) is the ONE walk, asked by `renderDocHtml`, `rlHangRichHtml` (now a caller), and both of the clause editor's readings. A marker set in BOLD hangs; a letter is one step in, a roman two (a single roman reads as a letter, said out loud); it refuses rather than emits crossing tags; changes no character; `rl-marker` is never on `richSpanClassOk`'s list (unwrapped by construction) and `ceBoxHtml` takes it off the copy it compares.

## A PDF KEEPS ITS STRUCTURE TOO (J-3.4, 10 Sep 2026)

`extractPdfRich` is `extractWordText`'s sibling: same `{text, html, report}`, `text` BYTE-IDENTICAL to `extractPdfText` (one walk `pdfReadPages`; `pdfLinesToText` / `pdfParaBreak` shared). A heading is decided from the PAGE (bold, or larger than body, and ≤ `PDF_HEAD_MAX`), never from capitals; `docLineKind` is not loosened. A numbered line needs a separator or a second part (`PDF_NUM_LINE`). Title takes h1 and levels shift; all-one-size headings start at h2. Stored through sanitizeRich on exactly a Word file's terms, ONLY where the reader REPORTS structure; a flat PDF keeps the guesswork. `uploadDocBody` prefers the stored wording over the iframe (PDFs only; a scan keeps its picture). **"Re-read document" knows PDFs too** (mirrors submitUpload's own branch; its three guards untouched: never a sealed record, never over an edited one, clause ids carried). `js/pdfrich.js` is a second, more capable reader with NO CALLER: it reads a soft-wrapped `(30)` as a list marker and changes the wording — choosing between the two is the owner's call. Tests: f233 (10), pdf-structure-verify.

## AN UPLOADED CONTRACT KEEPS ITS STRUCTURE (J-3.1/3.2/3.3, 29 Aug 2026)

`docxExtractRich` is the reader; `extractWordText` is the ONE door (upload and Re-read). `DOCX_PARTS` names the three parts and each one's size ceiling; a damaged part reads as ABSENT. Numbering is a WALK (counters per ABSTRACT list, restarts honoured); NO NUMBER IS EVER INVENTED (unresolved counted and reported on the file strip). The title takes h1 and the levels shift ONCE from the file; a style is a heading by outline level or name. No `colspan` (a merged cell is emitted as its cells). A table row is a line with cells joined by TABS on BOTH readers (`docxXmlToText` too); a cell's paragraphs join with a space. Plain text carries the same words in the same order plus a resolved marker and a tab in front. A file with no structure reads byte-identically and stores no body (`docxHasStructure`). J-3.3: a guessed structure says *"Structure read from the wording"* on the strip — a fact, not a warning. Re-read refuses a SEALED record and an EDITED one, and carries clause ids (`clauseCarryIds`). The file strip does not wrap (acts `flex:none`, facts elide). Fonts, page size, margins, headers, footers, images not carried; comments and tracked changes keep their own feature. Tests: f257, upload-structure-verify.

## THE AUDIT OF J-1 TO J-3 (owner-asked 30 Aug 2026)

Fifty-nine defects, all from one cause: storing a structure the product had never stored made three readings of one document disagree. A TABLE IS AN ANCHOR in `richFromTextEdit` (`_richEditAroundBlocks`), never a bail-out; both readers agree a row is tab-joined; the .docx HaTi sends carries tables (`docxHtmlBlocks` lifts them out before the tokeniser); the file strip cannot push the paper down (measured at every laptop width, rooted at the WORDING); `contractFullBody` carries the document once; the Document tab draws no "can't preview" card under wording; `uploadWordingEdited` is the one reading of "somebody edited this"; a repeating obligation asks the LIST; the chase carries the share link or none. Four nets were descriptions (`a.includes(x)||WHOLE_FILE.includes(x)`, hand-written shapes, a scope check on a contract nobody has, an `async` probe returning true) and are measurements now.

## SIGNING ON THE PAPER (owner-asked 29 Aug 2026, J-1)

**PUTTING A MARK ON THE PAPER IS NOT SIGNING** — every claim in f256 is a way of failing that: `c.signatures`, the status, the seal and the plan rows untouched; the wording does not freeze (`c.signSpots` is neither store `negoAnySignature` / `anySignatureRow` read); a mark does not travel; it never reaches the funnel. ONE SHEET, TWO TABS: the Signing tab draws the Document tab's canvas (`data-ws-pane="docs sign"`, a token list; `[data-doc-col]` toggles the right column). A spot is anchored to a CLAUSE ID with no offset (ids minted by `negoStampContract` at the press; `signSpotProposals` offers by position; nothing offered where there is no working text). HaTi PROPOSES, a person PLACES (SIGN_SPOT_CUE is narrow). A spot belongs to a row on the signing order (`signSpotSeat`); "spots still to fill" joins `signBlockers` (MINE only); a spot that is not yours is a `<div>`; filling is on the paper, arranging is in the signing column; the walk sits in the tab row's slot and never floats; marks painted after the canvas on `wireDocCanvas`, scaling with `--doc-scale`. D-4: NOT baked into `execution.html` (richdoc's allow-list has no IMG; f256 fails if it ever gains one) — `signSpots` joins EXECUTED_IMMUTABLE instead; the sealed copy and every export show no marks (said plainly). D-5: the counterparty gets neither the walk nor the marks (not on the payload; their page proved byte-identical). Tests: f256, signing-on-paper-verify.

## A CLAUSE'S NAME IS PART OF THE CLAUSE (owner-asked 28 Aug 2026)

`negoEditClause` takes `opts.headingText`; a `modify` carries it; ABSENT means the filing says nothing about the heading. The no-op guard learned it (both halves: must MOVE the heading AND still propose one — typing the original back is "nothing changed"; Withdraw takes a rename back). The name the clause already has stores as `''`; `negoHeadingAsk(cl, ch)` is the ONE reading of whether a heading moved. v5 fingerprint puts the heading INSIDE the attestation. `negoWordsMoved(ch)` keeps an unmoved clause's shape (both canvases). Both renderers honour the reading; a settled rename keeps its marks. `clauseReplaceHeading` keeps the element, rank and id; a clause with no heading is refused one (the box is not drawn). The clause panel and work mode offer the box; the room's inline editor does not. Enter finishes the name; textContent is read back. Work mode's step stack carries `head` beside `text`. Tests: f249, f35, f151, f210.

## THE FRONT MATTER IS A REGION, AND IT IS PROPOSED ON (owner-ruled 28 Aug 2026)

`clauseSegment` still skips it; it gains one reserved id `CLAUSE_FRONT_ID = 'front'` (never `cl_`-prefixed, so no collision). Everything downstream routes on the id and already works. `clauseFrontClause`, `clauseFrontParts`, `clauseReplaceFront`; `_clFrontEnd` is where the region ends. An edit that re-segments the clauses is REFUSED at the filing door (read back through the segmentation: same ids, same order); dropping the title is refused (the region would become unreachable); only where headings mark the clauses (else null, no pencil). Clean readings are documents, the redlined reading flattens to text. One pencil, no band; `is-changed` borrows the clause's margin rule. The record says "Front matter", screens say `ng_front_matter`; it reads FIRST in the round queue (`order.set(front, -1)`). The room does not draw it. The signature block is DERIVED from the record and is Key terms' to edit. Tests: f250, f210.

## THE PAGE NEVER OPENS IN A STATE THAT HIDES MARKS THAT EXIST — and THE PENCIL IS THE ONLY WAY IN

- `_ceEditing = _ceText === _ceBase && _ceHead === _ceHeadBase` (28 Aug): a clause with nothing on it opens typeable; one carrying a draft opens showing its marks; `ceUnderDeletion()` is asked FIRST. A door that SAID "edit this clause" (a pencil) arrives typeable (`typing:true`, consumed on arrival — `openEditor` in wireNegotiationTab is the one reading for the three doors).
- THE PENCIL IS THE ONLY WAY IN (1 Sep): click-to-type is RETIRED; a press in the wording does nothing, not even move the page; the pencil on another clause is ONE press (goes there and edits); the clause list moves you without editing; once typing, a press inside the box places the caret; `ceStartTyping` and `ceFocusTyping`'s point argument are gone; moving asks before throwing a draft away (`clauseEditorDirty`).
- THE CLAUSE YOU ASKED FOR STAYS WHERE IT IS (1 Sep): a move re-measures the target clause's own top after the render and puts it back (`ceClauseTopNow`, null when it was not on screen); consumed on arrival; `ceRestoreScroll` is the one thing that moves the paper (no glide — `.nego-scroll` is smooth and a bare assignment animates; landing is not travelling).
- THE LEAVE WARNING NAMES ITS CLAUSE AND QUOTES THE DRAFT (`clauseEditorLeaveAsk`, published; clauseLabel + richToText bounded by CE_LEAVE_SNIP; one paragraph). EVERY DOOR OUT OF A DRAFT ASKS (3 Sep): `ceLeaveGuard(go)` at the three doors (Leave work mode, Escape, ceGoClause), never inside `rlCloseClauseEditor`; it PULLS the box first (Escape blurs nothing); Escape defers to `data-top-overlay`; not dirty, no question.
- ONE FRAME ROUND THE CLAUSE: the dashed outline is on the clause containing an editable box (`has()`), not per box.
- THE CLAUSE EDITOR CANNOT HIDE A PROPOSED DELETION (10 Sep): `ceRemovesWording(ch)` is the ONE reading (deleteClause, or a body with no WORDS); the strike is drawn through the op renderer; opens showing marks and cannot be typed in; no pencil on that clause; Apply refuses in words; no Apply/Refine/softer chip; Accept and Reject are NOT mirrored here.
- A FILING REPAINTS BOTH SCREENS: `ceFiled(c)` is the one ending with exactly two callers (`ceFile`, `ceAddMissingClause`); the seed is deliberately not in it.
Tests: f245, clause-editor-verify, clause-door-verify.

## THE PLATFORM FILLS THE MONITOR, AND THE CONTRACT DOES NOT (owner-ruled 28–29 Aug 2026)

`--page-measure` is the ONE declaration and is `none`; `--room-measure` is derived from it. Two things are deliberately not it: the AGREEMENT (`--doc-sheet-max`, read by the Document tab, the negotiation sheet and the clause editor — no magnification anywhere; `applyDocZoom` / `rlApplyDocZoom` pinned at 1) and the friction brief's 78ch prose column. Not swept: the counterparty's page, the standalone documents. The surplus beside the contract stays grey (RL_RIGHT_W0 460). A PAGE FILLS THE READER'S OWN SCREEN: `rowsThatFit(el, rowH, min, max)` is the ONE reading of how many rows top up, answered AFTER THE PAINT, asking the scroller that really scrolls (VIEW_OWNS_HEIGHT); A ZERO IS NOT AN ANSWER — every caller keeps its old count as the FLOOR; re-renders only when the answer changes; counting is never capped, only drawing. The friction page's top-8 tables are a READING and are not fitted. `.ce-paperwrap` paints no background (the grey beside a sheet is the page ground). Tests: f252, keeps-your-place-verify.

`rlEditorTakesIt(side, opts)` is the ONE reading (our seat, not a preview, the module present BY NAME, window ≥ 1024) with four askers; the destination is chosen at DRAW time (`rlClauseEditPillHtml` takes `toEditor`; `redlineDocHtml` decides once). The panel is KEPT for the counterparty's seat and windows under 1024px. The front matter goes to the editor too.
