## THE LAYERED REDLINE — A COUNTER WRITTEN ON THEIR ASK STACKS (Young ruled 13 Sep 2026, "the Large option"; built overnight 13–14 Sep)

Manual redlining only. The 15 Aug rule (ONE PROPOSAL ON THE TABLE) still governs a RIVAL — a filing measured against the standing text (the playbook pass, Prepare redlines, Copilot's batch, the Word round trip, our own ask from another round) SUPERSEDES. Two other things a filing can meet are not rivals:
- **WRITTEN ON TOP**: `negoEditClause(c, id, html, { onTop })` measures against that ask's `newText` (a pending ask, or one parked under a pending counter of the filer's own side); `negoMeasuredAlike` tells it from a rival; the funnel PARKS their ask — `status:'countered'`, `counteredBy`, `bundle:[{id, was, counteredBy}]` on the counter, `counterOf` naming what it stands on — never superseded, wording and fingerprint kept, and it TRAVELS (`buildSharePayload` carries `counteredBy` / `bundle` / `replacement`; `applyNegoProposals` honours the sender's `oldText` so their counter stacks on arrival; the server's held-reviewer guard lets parking and releasing moves through).
- **THE PAIR IS DECIDED TOGETHER**: `negoResolve` refuses a decision on a parked ask (`ng_countered_decide_counter`); `negoBundleFollow(c, ch, status)` is the ONE follower — accept answers everything under the counter (walked, not one deep; deeper asks keep `counteredBy` for the reopen), refuse puts each back exactly as it stood (under its own counter where that is still on the table, roots first), reopen re-parks. `negoRetractDraft` releases. `negoAdvanceRound` refuses while anything is parked, so a replacement can never cross a round boundary undecided. `negoProgress` counts the question, not the ask beneath it.
- **THE WHOLESALE RULE**: `redlineWholesale(before, after)` — the marks outnumber the words, two lengths compared, never a percentage — makes a counter written on top a REPLACEMENT (`replacement:true`) and a BUNDLE of every live ask on the clause, both sides', however deep. Drawn by `redlineReplacementHtml`: one struck block, one inserted block, `.rl-repl-on` saying what it stands on (`ng_repl_stands`), on the block, never a band.
- **THE PICTURE**: `redlineLayerOps(underOps, overOps, {under, over})` composes the two STORED op arrays (never re-diffed; null unless their new text is our old text); `redlineOpsHtml` wears `who` as `rl-them` / `rl-us` (amber for the other side, the accent for us — RELATIVE TO THE READER, so their page shows the same words with the classes swapped; one stylesheet in negotiation-css.js and index.html). `rlLayeredHtml(c, ch, viewerSide)` is the one reading for both canvases; `rlStackUnder` names the ask a counter stands on; `negoLeadChange` leads with the top of a stack and never a parked ask. A lone ask keeps the classic green/red. A parked ask keeps a row on the column in its counter's pile (`_rlIsParked`, `rlCardBand` — live again on a seat the counter has not reached) and offers no verbs; the badge says `ng_badge_countered`.
- **THE EDITOR** (js/views/clauseeditor.js): seeds from their version; `ceStacksOn()` is the one reading of which ask this page writes on (their pending ask, or the one parked under our own counter) and `ceFile` passes it as `onTop`; `ceMarkedHtml` draws the two layers (or the replacement) for a draft not yet filed; the recorded words win over a stale body (`ceProposedOf`). Copilot: NOTHING changed.
- **RULE 1 — CLICK INTO A CLAUSE AND TYPE** (reverses THE PENCIL IS THE ONLY WAY IN of 1 Sep, for the editor only; the negotiation page's pencil is still the door): the click handler's branch sits after every control on the paper and before the strip's acts; another clause → `ceGoClause(id, {typing:true})`; a drag is left to the mouseup handler; `ceTypingRefusal()` is the ONE reading of "may this clause be typed in now" and SPEAKS the existing sentences (a proposed deletion, a reading that refuses, a colleague's lock, the door's walls) — no caret blinks in a refused clause. **RULE 2 — MARKS NEVER DISAPPEAR** — since 14 Sep 2026 the marks are IN the box the reader types in (see TYPING IN THE MARKS below); the reading under the box (`ceTwinHtml` / `ceTwinPaint`) survives only as the fallback for a box the painter cannot own. **THE PENCIL MEANS DONE**: none at rest; drawn on the clause being typed in only while typing (`ce_pencil_done`); it files where there is something to file and asks for the note, else ends the typing. **THE LOCK SIGN IS DRAWN BEFORE `skip` IS ASKED** in `rlClauseEditPillHtml`, so a held clause is seen to be held at rest.
Defaults taken and reported: wholesale = marks outnumber words, and only for a filing written on top; Copilot's batch and the playbook keep superseding; the default reading stays Redlined; every counter typed in the editor is a stack; our own earlier ask is superseded, never parked.
Tests: f207-D (18 claims), f245 (26), clause-editor-verify 32 (+16–21, 30d re-pointed), clause-door-verify 16d3/16e–16h (reversed in place, a real mouse), redline-verify 27, parity-verify 12; every new claim red at the parent.

## TYPING IN THE MARKS — THE BOX IS THE REDLINE (Young ruled 14 Sep 2026)

*"I want to be able to redline in the redlined section … I would delete whatever I disagree with and enter what I want as the new clause and when I finish I click the pencil and the new redlines appear on top of the previous redlines."* Reverses the 13 Sep twin under the box (kept as the fallback only).
- **THE BOX HOLDS THE DRAFT; THE MARKS ARE PAINTED INTO IT.** `ceMarksPaint` (js/views/clauseeditor.js): the projection is read off the LIVE box by `_lineUnits` (js/richdoc.js, published, each unit carrying its `nodes`); `ceMarksOps(ours)` gives the three answers `ceMarkedHtml` gives at rest (two layers over their ask, a REPLACEMENT under `redlineWholesale`, one layer against what stands); `ceMarksDraw` walks the ops onto DOM characters — an added run is wrapped `<ins data-ce-mark="ins">`, a struck run is an ATOM `<del data-ce-mark="del">` put back where it was taken out; `ceMarksApply` lands the events last-first. A walk that disagrees with the projection by a character draws NOTHING, and the reading is drawn under the box instead (also for a table or a `<pre>`). Repainted `CE_MARKS_MS` (300) after each keystroke with the caret put back by its count of live characters (`ceCaretSave` / `ceCaretRestore`, `ceLiveTextNodes`); `ceMarksMount` is the one caller (the mount, the debounce, the pull).
- **THE PAINT NEVER REACHES THE RECORD**: `ceBoxHtml` takes it off first (`ceMarksClear`; `CE_PAINT_SEL` names `[data-ce-mark]`); the pull, the dirty check and `ceDraftNow` read the clean draft; the pencil files through `negoEditClause` with `onTop` as before.
- **AN ATOM IS CROSSED, NEVER TYPED INTO, AND IT IS NOT `contenteditable="false"`** — a non-editable island is what the browser DRAGS as an object (a highlight begun on one selected nothing) and what a bar press wrote its marker into. Backspace/Delete step over it (`ceAtomSkip`, `ceAtomAt`); a caret inside one steps out on `beforeinput` and the keystroke lands beside it; a struck run a selection swallows is back at the next paint.
- **THE WRITING BAR TAKES THE PAINT OFF FIRST** (`ceMarksClear` before `richBarPress`, both paths); the reader's selection is a LIVE range and is left alone (a rebuilt one landed in a whitespace node between two paragraphs and the bar found no block); a press that moved nothing gets its marks back.
- **A HIGHLIGHT IS READ AGAINST THE LIVE WORDING AND WITHOUT THE STRUCK WORDS** (the two silences the owner reported): `ceSelectionRead` reads the range in `'current'` mode and gives a marker span its gap; `ceLines` reads `ceDraftNow()`, never the draft at the last pull; the three finders (`ceAttachWords`, `ceRangeForText`, `ceMarkHeld`) walk `ceLiveTextNodes`. A highlight made only of struck words is offered nothing (noticed, not ruled).
- **A REMOVED PARAGRAPH IS A REMOVED PARAGRAPH** (Young reported the movement a second time, 15 Sep 2026, at rest and with a cursor in): a struck run is put back where it was taken out, and where a whole sub-paragraph was taken out is the START OF A LINE — whose first text node belongs to the NEXT paragraph's `.rl-marker` span. So the box drew a paragraph of wording inside a 2.6em gutter, one block fewer than the sheet: MEASURED, the block under the caret grew 49 → 122.5px, the one under it dropped 15.5px, and striking the LAST sub-paragraph took it off the screen. A run carrying a `\n` is not inline wording: `atomsAt(node, k, edge)` takes the block path at a line's START or END, `delBlock` builds one `<p class="rl-line rl-clause rl-line-del">` PER LINE in the at-rest renderer's own classes (`redlineSplitMarker` hangs the marker, the ONE reading), and `ceMarksApply` lands them against the BLOCK — nothing there splits a text node, so the per-node walk still means what it recorded. The paint still never reaches the record (`CE_MARK_ATTR` on the block; `ceMarksClear` removes it whole).
Tests: f245 (26, extended — every new claim red at the parent), clause-editor-verify 32i–32k (a real drag over typed words, over their marks, a real Backspace at an atom; 19 and 21 act on live wording) and section 34 (a struck sub-paragraph, every block measured before and after a real press; 3 of 5 red at the parent), clause-door-verify 16d3/16f re-pointed.

## ENTERING A CLAUSE IS NOT AN EVENT (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026)

*"I do not want to have the dotted line or any line around a clause when I go to edit ... I also do not want to click twice ... nothing on the page should move as far as positioning or spacing just because I entered a cursor in the clause ... Just put your cursor wherever you want once and start typing."*
- **NO FRAME.** Reverses 26 Aug ("a very light almost dotted line") and 1 Sep ("one frame round the clause"), both kept in the source because their reasoning is what makes this safe: the note already named THE CARET as the real indicator. The outline is gone from the editor's live clause and from the clause panel's name box. `.ce-twin`'s separator stays (it parts the fallback reading, it is not a frame).
- **NOTHING MOVES.** `.ce-typing` and `.ce-headbox` are re-scoped to `.ce-paperwrap .rl-doc .rl-clause …` — MEASURED: the paper's own `.redline-page .rl-doc .nego-body` (0,3,0) was setting `margin:0`, so the wording's negative margin had NEVER applied and the ink jumped 10px right / 8px down on every press. Both insets are HORIZONTAL ONLY and written as `margin-left`/`margin-right` LONGHANDS — a `margin` shorthand also matches the HEADING and wiped its own 5px bottom gap (the last 5px). Fixed by SCOPE, never `!important`.
- **THE PENCIL'S RESERVE IS ONE TOKEN**, `--rl-pill-reserve` (78px, measured: Done is the widest face at 74px), read by the heading's `padding-right` and the chip's `margin-right`. The Done pencil is `position:absolute;right:0` like every other face and the `:has(.rl-cp-pill-done)` rule that released the reserve is GONE.
- **ONE PRESS, AND THE CARET LANDS WHERE IT WENT.** `ceHoldClickPoint` / `ceClickPointNow` / `ceRangeAtPoint` / `ceFocusTyping(pt)` — the point is held against THE CLAUSE (dx/dy off its own rect, so a rebuild cannot move it), consumed once, expires at `CE_CLICK_PT_MS` (1500); a point outside the box or inside a struck atom places nothing / steps beside it. Hung off `ceScrollToClause`'s landing, NEVER the paint — measured, the paint runs before the page has scrolled.
- **A PRESS ON THE PENCIL MAY NOT BLUR THE BOX** (`preventDefault` on its mousedown): traced with a real mouse — mousedown, blur, the paper rebuilt, mouseup on a different node, no click. The third costume of the 30 Aug / 11 Sep fault.
- **NO BLOCK BEHIND AN ADDED CLAUSE**: `.rl-clause-new`'s wash and its edge are gone (the class STAYS — both renderers stamp it). The marks on the WORDS say it instead. Young: *"not the teal highlight but the green block covering the entire space ... even the empty space"*.
Tests: f245 (15) and its click branch (reversed in place; the colour claim DELETED, not skipped), f210 (the reserve re-pointed to the relation, plus the pinned-Done claim), clause-editor-verify 17d/17h reversed and section 33 (nine checks, driven with a real mouse; 33d measures the INK, 33h the WORD under the caret — six red at the parent), clause-door-verify 14h–14h3.

## FOUR THINGS OFF ONE SCREEN (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026)

- **THE PIN QUOTES WHAT MOVED, NOT THE CLAUSE IT MOVED IN** (*"I only changed clause 1.2 so the reference on the right should only take 1.2 and not the whole clause 1"*): `rlNpChangeQuote` reads the change's OPS through `redlineShownBlocks(ops,{changedOnly:true})` + `redlineBlockShown` — the card's own selection, so pin and card cannot disagree. It read `bodyHtml` and cut 400 characters off the FRONT, so an edit half way down quoted the paragraphs above it. The whole-body reading survives as the FALLBACK for a change with no ops (an insert, an older record), where every word IS what moved.
- **THE LEGEND STATES THE FACT AND STOPS** (*"delete the 'not agreed text' bit"*): `ng_legend_plain` SHORTENED in both books — the first half is still drawn, so the key is shortened, never retired.
- **THE THREE CHECK SYMBOLS ARE IN THE ACTS ROW** (*"move the highlighted buttons up and to the right of the more button"*): `roomChecksHtml` is called from `.room-acts` on `backC` (the workbench flag it always had) and NOT from `roomFactsHtml`; `.room-acts-lead > .room-checks{order:4}` puts them after `#ws-more` (order 3). The clothes: `--ctl-h` (one rung with every other button), the row's own 6px gap, `--btn-edge` for the edge (`.ui-btn`'s own — green on teal, blue on navy from ONE token), the glyph 16 → 18 because the box may not pass the row's rung. **The workbench's `#ws-head .room-acts button` rule pins 11px of side padding** and turned the square into a 46px lozenge — answered by `button.room-check` at that rule's weight plus one, by SCOPE.
- **AND COLLAPSE NOW GIVES THE CONTRACT THE SPACE** (the point of the move): with nothing left in it the fact row folds to 0 and the paper rises 53px (was 25 — a 28px button held it open). MEASURED both ways.
Tests: f313 (39), f304 (8) extended (both red at the parent), nego-redesign-verify 13 (five claims reversed in place; 1b excludes the checks by name — they are not acts), room-head-fold-verify section 6 (a real press; three red at the parent).

## FIVE MORE OFF THE SAME SCREEN (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026, later)

- **THE MARK SITS IN THE MIDDLE OF ITS BOX** (*"the signs are not in the middle of the boxes they sit in"*): MEASURED 1px left / 9px right in a 28px box round an 18px glyph. `.room-check` states `display:grid;place-items:center` and the workbench's own `#ws-head .room-acts button` states `display:inline-flex;align-items:center` with NO `justify-content` at three classes to one — so it won and the mark packed against the left wall. The centring is RESTATED on `button.room-check` at the narrower scope; the row rule's inline-flex is what keeps the rest of the row on one baseline, so it is not touched. By SCOPE, never `!important`.
- **A REDLINED CLAUSE IS SPACED LIKE EVERY OTHER CLAUSE** (*"when you press inside a clause with redlines, the paragraphs slightly separate from each other when they should not move at all"*): the ops renderer's `.rl-line` had been swept in with the CONTAINERS at `margin:0`, so a redlined clause already drew tighter than the wording round it, and the press swapped it for the typing box's ordinary paragraphs at 9px — 9px down for the paragraph under the caret, 18 for the next. `.redline-page .rl-doc .rl-clause .rl-line` takes the body's own 9px with `:last-child` giving it back. **LONGHANDS**: a `margin` shorthand also states a LEFT of 0, which is the sub-bullet's own step — written short once, it flattened a sub-bullet onto its sibling (clause-editor-verify 26j). The same trap this file paid for on the typing box's inset.
- **A CHANGE THAT MOVES NO WORDING QUOTES NO WORDING** (*"this is still not resolved"*, of the 15 Sep pin fix): an edit to one sub-paragraph was already quoting that sub-paragraph. What still read the whole clause is the pair of asks that file ALL-KEEP ops — a formatting-only edit and a heading rename — where nothing is touched and the fallback read the body. `rlNpChangeQuote` asks `redlineDrawnBlocks` + `redlineBlockTouched` DIRECTLY, never `redlineShownBlocks` (whose "draw them all" fallback is right for a canvas and wrong for a quote): a rename quotes `headingText`, a formatting ask quotes its summary, and the body is the answer only where there are NO ops at all.
- **ON OUR SEAT THE CLAUSE PANEL IS THE LADDER, WHATEVER DOOR OPENED IT** (*"still appears here and there but I cannot trace what is making it appear. It should be deleted"*): it was never one door — the pencil, the card's Edit and the empty column's act all ask `rlEditorTakesIt` at the DRAW and send our seat to the clause editor, but the deal board's row press opened the panel in full, and any door added later would have. `rlCpNarrowSeat()` (PORTAL_MODE first, then `rlEditorTakesIt('owner')`) is asked inside `rlCpLadderOnly` and `rlCpSetShown`, so every door lands on the same panel and none can drift. `_rlCpLadder` remembers only the CALLER's own ask. The counterparty's seat and any window under 1024px are untouched — there the panel is the only way wording is proposed, which is why three browser files now stage it at a 1000px window rather than on our seat.
- **A SHORT WORD IS STILL A WORD** (*"sometimes when i highlight a word the 3 options do not appear"*): MEASURED — three characters offered all three verbs, two offered nothing, in silence, while the clause editor beside it answered on two. The floor of three was a guess at an accidental drag and the wrong instrument: a drag too small to select leaves the selection COLLAPSED and never reaches the handler. ONE floor, and it is EMPTINESS — `!words.length` in `rlPaperOfferFromRange`, `!text.length` in `ceSelectionRead`, and no caller keeps a stricter copy.
Tests: f313 (38)(39) and f245 (15) extended, f304 (8) and a new f304 (14) — all red at the parent; nego-redesign-verify 13, clause-editor-verify 8c/33d2 (33's stage now needs a clause of SEVERAL paragraphs, or the gap cannot open), ladder-verify 6e2/18j, round-two-comments-verify J1d–J1d3, clause-door-verify section 3 and redline-verify 12b/22/25q staged at the panel's own seat.

## THE SAVE, THE WHOLE READING, AND THE DOCUMENT'S OWN HEAD (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026, evening)

- **ONE SAVE AT A TIME, OR THE CONTRACT CONFLICTS WITH ITSELF** (*"this pop up appears for no reason at times"*, of "This contract just changed on the server"): a race with nobody else in it. `baseVersion` is read off `c._v` and `c._v` only moves when the PUT COMES BACK; the debounce is 400ms and a long contract's PUT takes longer, so the second PUT left carrying the version the first had already spent and the server answered 409 — correctly. `_flushing` / `_flushAgain` make `flushSaves` single-flight: a second caller marks that there is more to do and AWAITS the run already going, which loops again for whatever was dirtied meanwhile (so `await flushSaves()` still guarantees the record is on the server). **THE LATCH IS A BOOLEAN, NEVER THE PROMISE ITSELF — see the section below.** The server's optimistic lock is UNTOUCHED — it is the wall — and the dialog stays for a real conflict. No other server route can cause one: every other writer updates the stored json without moving `version`, deliberately.
- **THE EDITION READS THE WHOLE CONTRACT** (*"the platform stopped reading the contract. This should never ever happen as it would kill the business proposition"* — 216 clauses, 60 read, "156 further clauses were not read"): the 60 was never a product limit, it is ONE CALL's arithmetic (8,000 tokens ÷ ~110 a clause). `READ_PAGE` 60 stays the page, `READ_MAX_PAGES` 12 is the total (`READ_MAX_CLAUSES` is derived), `READ_AT_ONCE` 3 runs them a few at a time. **A KEY IS THE ROW'S ADDRESS WITHIN ITS PAGE** (R0…R59, a short fresh key space per call) and `base` offsets it back, so a stored item's `i` is the GLOBAL index the browser pairs against — the one place paging could have reintroduced the 11 Sep off-by-one, and the echo guard is asked per page. ~~The first page refusing refuses the press~~ (REVERSED 23 Sep 2026: a failed page is asked again once, and only a press where no page answered is refused — see SEVEN FIXES 6); a page still failing is counted into `over`, said, and **not cached** (the cut-short rule, applied to a page). The hash is over the whole edition; one cache row, one press, one column.
- **THE DOCUMENT TAB IS BUILT LIKE THE NEGOTIATE PAGE** (*"Build the document page to have the same well constructed contract"*): MEASURED on the owner's paper, the same stored wording — `center / uppercase / 2.52px` inside `header.rl-paper-head` on one page, `start / none / normal` and no head on the other. `clauseFrontSplit(html)` → `{front, rest}` is the new reading and it is `_clFrontEnd`, the SAME boundary `clauseFrontClause` and `clauseReplaceFront` draw, so neither page invents its own. `docPaperFrontHtml` builds the head in the negotiate page's OWN classes (`.rl-paper-head` · `.rl-paper-kick` · `.rl-paper-title` · `.rl-recital` — written at the top level for exactly this reason, and contract.js already calls `redlineLayoutCss()`), and `renderDocHtml` gained an additive `opts.lead` that goes FIRST INSIDE `.hati-doc` and is not re-sanitised (the sanitiser has no `<header>`). `docBodyCarriesTop` learned the head, so the tab does not print a second title block. No region, no title, no model → null, and the page draws exactly what it drew before. The tracking is pinned as a RATIO: `--doc-scale` is the reader's own text size and is per page by design.
Tests: f314 (4 claims), f315 (6), f316 (7) — every one red at the parent bar its named controls; saves-serialize-verify (a held PUT and two real edits; 3 of 6 red at the parent), pages-read-alike-verify 10, f277 (19) re-pointed (the key is the page's `k`). `startScriptedAi` learned a FUNCTION form, so a stand-in can answer the page it was actually sent.

## A LATCH MAY NOT BE ITS OWN PROMISE (Young reported it 16 Sep 2026)

*"Fix the record catching up issue."* MEASURED in two seats: the counterparty's acceptance drew on the owner's screen while the record read back off the server still said `pending`, and the refused-readiness trail line was written 0 times where exactly 1 is owed. ONE CAUSE, and it stopped every save in the browser, not just those two.

- **AN ASYNC BODY RUNS SYNCHRONOUSLY TO ITS FIRST `await`, AND A FLUSH WITH AN EMPTY QUEUE NEVER REACHES ONE.** `_flushing = (async()=>{ … finally { _flushing=null; } })()` therefore ran to the end, the `finally` wrote null, and the ASSIGNMENT then put the resolved promise back over it. `_flushing` was left permanently truthy, every later call took the join door and got an already-settled promise, and `dirty` was never drained again for the life of the page. Nothing errored, nothing logged, the screen was right and the record simply stopped moving.
- **THE EMPTY FLUSH IS THE ORDINARY CASE, not an edge one**: `persist` sets a 400ms timer and several callers drain BY HAND straight after (applyResponse, so the write lands before the repaint reloads over it; auto-triage, so the record is on the server before it is read back). The hand drain empties the queue and the stale timer fires on nothing.
- **THE FIX IS THE SHAPE**: `_flushing` is a BOOLEAN raised before the body exists (so a synchronous run cannot overwrite its own clearing), `_flushDone` is the promise, and a joiner that comes back to a queue still holding work goes round again — so the promise means *the queue is empty*, which is what its callers rely on.
- Tests: f314 (2)(2b) pin the shape; saves-serialize-verify section 2 drives the real sequence in a browser (3 of 9 red at the parent — the record one edit behind, one contract still queued, the version unmoved). round-delivery-verify is 34/34 again.

## THE CLAUSE LADDER, AND THE DEAL BOARD (Young ruled 14 Sep 2026)

The paper shows the last TWO moves and that is right; the ladder is how the other moves are reached. `js/ladder.js` is the ONE reading — no route, no store, no field, and it never calls a name that initialises (f313 (1)(2)(3) are the walls; the whole point is that a reading drawn beside every clause on every paint must not create a negotiation by being looked at).
- `ladderRungs(c, clauseId)` reads `c.changes` AND `c.negotiation.rounds` RAW, oldest first, numbered from 1. A closed round is on the ladder — that is the whole point, since `negoAdvanceRound` empties `c.changes`. Ordered by round, then `seq` (the fingerprint chain's own counter), then arrival. `LADDER_CAP` 60 and `ladderOverflow` says what was left out.
- `ladderUnder` reads what a rung STANDS ON off `oldText`, never guessed. `ladderBaseText` is R0 — the first rung's own starting point.
- **A PARKED ASK IS NOT LIVE AND IS STILL A POSITION.** `ladderLive` drops `countered`/`superseded`/`rejected`/`accepted`; `ladderStand`'s own `standing` list keeps `countered`, because the moment you counter them their ask stops being live and the board said "no ask yet" on the most argued clause on the page (measured).
- **IT BORROWS EVERY FIGURE.** `PRECEDENT_TOPICS` carries the reader, the unit and which direction is worse; `resolvePlaybook` + `pbRangeRead` carry the standard; `clauseNameShown` names the clause. `ladderSettledFigure` is the ONE place precedentSuggestions' "worst REPEATED figure" rule is written twice, and says so.
- **THERE IS NO WALK-AWAY COLUMN**: the playbook holds a position and no walk-away, so one here would be a number this product invented and showed a lawyer as their own policy. Precedent stands beside the standard instead.

THE CHIP is drawn by `rlLadderChipHtml` through `pillFor` — ONE closure, so all seven `.rl-clause-top` rows get it and it cannot drift from the pencil. It reserves the pinned pencil's own 62px (it is `position:absolute;right:0`, so anything left in the flow runs under it — measured). **It carries `data-rl-ladder`, NEVER `data-rl-cp-open`**: that attribute is what the click-in-the-wording door hunts for when it looks for a clause's PENCIL, and `querySelector` answers in DOCUMENT order, so a chip carrying it answered instead and a click in the wording opened the panel rather than the editor (clause-door-verify 17a). It stands down in a clean reading, for the pencil's own reason.

THE LADDER is the clause panel's FOURTH section, under History — which is this round's settled asks and empties at round close. `rlLadderSectionHtml` draws it; `.rl-ladder` must be written `.redline-page .rl-cp-src ol.rl-ladder` because `.rl-cp-src ol` restores real list markers and a TYPE selector beats a plain class (two numbering systems on one list, measured).
- **READ AS IT STOOD** (`_rlReadAt`, per sitting, in memory, never sent) redraws ONE clause at an earlier rung from that rung's STORED ops. Asked FIRST by both body builders through `bodyAt`; inert while nothing is pinned. Cleared by `rlCardForgetPins` when the contract changes; not honoured in a clean reading (the pin is kept, so switching back lands the reader where they were).
- **THERE IS NO "ACCEPT THIS EARLIER ASK" VERB** and the absence is load-bearing: a counter PARKS their ask, `negoResolve` refuses a decision on a parked ask by name, and a rival filing supersedes it instead — so the press could never have worked. The rung names the counter that answers it (`ng_rung_decided_with`).
- `openLadderCompare` compares any two rungs through `redlineStructuredHtml`, the version Compare window's own renderer.
- **THE CHIP'S PANEL IS THE LADDER, THE PENCIL'S IS THE WHOLE CLAUSE (Young reported it 15 Sep 2026: *"when I click on ladder, I also get the highlighted area ... I thought you are only supposed to get the ladder"*)**: `rlCpSetShown(scope, id, opts)` takes an ADDITIVE `{ladder:true}` — every older caller means the whole panel — and `_rlCpLadder` / `rlCpLadderOnly` (published) is the posture. **ONE BUILD, TWO POSTURES**: `is-ladder` on `#rl-cp` and ONE stylesheet rule hide every `.rl-cp-sec` that is not `.rl-ladder-sec` / `.rl-pb-sec` / `.rl-fig-sec` / `.rl-notes-sec`, so the chip's panel cannot drift from the pencil's; what survives is the clause's NAME, the ladder and its tail. Named by class, never by position. The head says which panel it is (`ng_cp_ladder`). **THE TOGGLE IS THE CLAUSE, NEVER THE POSTURE** — a first draft narrowed a panel already open in full instead of shutting it and broke the owner's own rule that the press which opens a sliding panel closes it (ladder-verify 11b caught it). `rlCpPaint` carries the posture through a repaint. Tests: f313 (38) (red at the parent), ladder-verify 18g–18j (measured as PIXELS — the sections stay in the markup).

THE DEAL BOARD (`ladderBoard` / `dealBoardHtml` / `openDealBoard`) is one row per clause with a move on it, sorted by distance. TWO DOORS, ONE ACT: the More menu's row and the control-row button both carry `data-rl-board` and ONE handler answers both. **It is NOT a fourth reading**: `RL_READS` is how the DOCUMENT is drawn, and a fourth value would fall through `rlReadSideOf` unknown and shut the pencil everywhere through `rlReadOnlyReading` (f313 (22) pins the three). Our seat only — it reads our playbook and our own negotiating history.

Registered in js/app.js, test/world.js (`LADDER`, `opts.ladder`), test/portalworld.js and the four `test/chromium/*.html` harnesses. Tests: f313 (27 claims), ladder-verify (41 checks, all driven with a real mouse and measured in both themes); clause-door-verify 2f re-pointed in place (the panel has four sections now).

## THE ARTIFACT, BUILT AS DRAWN — THE LADDER'S SECOND PASS (Young ruled 14 Sep 2026, night: "replicate the artifact apart from the top of the contract")

The 14 Sep ladder shipped the engine and three small surfaces; the owner opened the page and found the artifact missing. Four rulings that night, each a reversal of a standing rule, written beside the code they reverse:
- **EVERY MARK WEARS ITS AUTHOR'S SIDE** (reverses "a lone ask keeps the classic green/red"): `redlineOpsHtml` takes `opts.who` as the side of every op carrying none; `rlSideWho(ch, viewerSide)` is the ONE reading of "us or them from this chair" and every paper passes it (negoDocHtml, redlineDocHtml's `redlineBody`, the front matter, the inserted clause, the card's preview, `rlChangeWordingHtml`, the editor's `ceRedlineHtml` / `ceMarksOps` as `'us'`, the portal's viewer from THEIR chair). An added run is UNDERLINED and a struck one struck on the sided classes only (`.rl-us` / `.rl-them` in negotiation-css.js AND index.html — the editor reads the second); a mark with no side keeps green/red. Colour says who, the line says what.
- **THE LINE UNDER A STACKED CLAUSE** (a band, owner-ruled): `ladderBaseline` (js/ladder.js) reads what the plain words are — the wording the LOWER move was written on, with its figure — and `rlBaselineHtml` prints it after the clause section (outside it, so the ruby bar stops at the wording), only on a stack, only in the redlined reading, never inside the editor (`opts.pill`). Its "As agreed" is a real `data-rl-read` press. While a rung is pinned it says so with the way back.
- **THE CHIP** says `· not sent` on our own unsent top (`ladderUnsent`, the RAW twin of negoUnsentAsks — `turnAt` / `holdIds`, never negoPending), sits BESIDE the heading (`.rl-clause-top` is flex-start with a gap; the heading gives up the pencil's reserve when a chip carries it), and is drawn on the editor's paper too, as a static span.
- **THE PENCIL THAT MEANS DONE SAYS SO**: a pressed pill (`pill.pressed`, the editor's clause being typed in) prints its word beside the glyph, wears an edge and sits in the row (`.rl-cp-pill-done`, static, never hover-only). Every other pencil is byte-identical.
- **THE COLUMN IS THE ARTIFACT'S** (reverses 2 Sep's "Open is the one thing on the face"; Open itself went on 15 Sep — see THE ROW IS THE WHOLE OF IT): the clause LEADS the row (`meta = who || ch.id`; the reference rides the hover); the second line is the ARGUMENT — `rlLadderTrackHtml` where the clause is argued in a number, else `R2 · yours on their R1 · summary` (`rlRowSubHtml`); the FACE carries Accept · Reject · Counter on their ask, Edit · Send · Discard on our draft, Ladder on every row (`rlRowFaceVerbs` re-orders the funnel's own `verbs` list and relabels two of its entries — one string, no drift; Open was last here until 15 Sep); the row is `minmax(0,1fr) max-content` with the head `display:contents`, written AFTER the row's own rules because it wins by order; **A PARKED ASK FOLDS UNDER ITS COUNTER'S ROW** on both seats (one row per argument); bands read *Your drafts, not yet sent* · *With the counterparty* · *Settled this round* · *Refused, back to agreed*, and AWAITING YOU is amber.
- **THE PANEL'S TAIL** (`rlLadderTailHtml`): the ladder's top rung carries the same verbs (the card's own attributes), the two moves the paper shows are shaded (`rl-rung-win`); then *Your playbook* (`rlPlaybookSecHtml`: standard = the range, fallback = the clause library's fallback figure or wording, **walk-away is `—` with the reason on the hover — the playbook holds none and HaTi invents none**, precedent = `ladderSettledFigure` or `precedentLine`), *The figure* (`rlScaleHtml`, bounds off the figures themselves; `rlFigureSecHtml` with Propose + *Write it into the clause*, which opens the editor at rest and `ceApply`s `ladderWriteFigure(text, n, unit)` — words and digits, the qualifier kept, nothing else moves), *Notes* (count BORROWED from `negoNoteCounts`; *Open the drawer* → `openNotesPanel`).
- **TWO READINGS REPAIRED ON THE WAY**: `ladderTopic` REFUSES a topic whose own figure reader finds nothing in the clause (an INSURANCE clause read as liability off "product liability insurance" and drew the liability fallback — measured); `ladderStandard` falls back to the range BY TOPIC KEY (`LADDER_RANGE_TOPIC`) where `pbRangeRead` misses the drafted "twelve (12) months" — the standard is still the playbook's, nothing guessed.
- **THE DEAL BOARD IS A PAGE** (owner: "yes build as in the artifact"): `_rlBoardOpen` per sitting; the control-row tab wears `.on` and no reading is lit (`rlReadSegsHtml({none})`); `redlinePanesHtml` draws `rlBoardPageHtml` before the grid and `#view-redline.rl-board-on` hides the grid and the turn strip; a reading press, a row press (`data-rl-board-go`, then the panel on that clause) and the tab again put the paper back; the shell title says Deal board (`rlBoardPaintTitle`); NINE columns (Clause · Your standard · Fallback · Walk-away · Their last · Yours now · Distance · Rungs · Move), "N within your fallback" (`ladderWithin`, by `dir`), *Copy as memo* (`rlBoardMemoText`, clipboard). Still not a fourth reading; still our seat only; `openDealBoard` opens the page.
- **THE RAIL** (js/views/clauseeditor.js): tabs Suggestions · Ladder · Figure · Playbook scan (`_ceTab` widened; the Figure tab hidden unless `ceFigureTopic`); `ceLadderCardHtml` LEADS the conversation — WHAT MOVED · ON YOUR LADDER · WHAT YOU HAVE SETTLED FOR · SUGGESTED REPLY (the fallback figure, else the standard, written into THEIR wording) · NOTE TO SEND WITH IT · *Apply to the box* (`ceApply`) · *Keep the note for filing* (`_ceHeldNote` rides `ceFile` as `why`) · *Accept their R{n}* (leaves through `ceLeaveGuard` and presses the card's own `data-nego-accept`) — WORKED OUT, NEVER ASKED FOR (f313 (35) greps it for every model door); the Ladder tab is `rlLadderSectionHtml(…, {editor:true})` in a `.redline-page .rl-cp-src` wrapper so the panel's rules dress it; the Figure tab writes through `ceFigureWrite` (`oninput` handlers, because the box's own input listener is what f245 (19) reads by name).
Kept by the owner's word: the top of the contract (the room head, the control row's other controls); HaTi's own chips and Playbook scan in the rail. Defaults taken and said: the fallback where the library holds no figure prints its wording; a clause with no topic prints `—`; the memo is plain text.
Tests: f313 (27)–(37), ladder-verify 15–21 (every press real); f245 (18)(19)(26), f246 (4)(5), f207-B/D, f248, f37, f93, clause-door-verify 2f/9a/9b, clause-editor-verify 1b/14a/20f, redline-verify 14b/19/25 re-pointed in place, each with the ruling beside it.

## THE PENCIL IS NEVER THE WAY IN — THE NEGOTIATE PAGE TOO (Young ruled 14 Sep 2026)

A click in a clause's wording on the negotiate page presses the clause's own pencil: the click door in `wireNegotiationTab` (our seat, not a preview, not `opts.pill` — the editor's canvas answers its own clicks — not in the portal; a collapsed selection; not on a control) finds `[data-rl-cp-editor]` / `[data-rl-cp-open]` in the clause and presses it, so the destination is the pencil's own (editor or panel, decided at the draw); a held clause speaks its lock; a drag keeps its menu. Reverses "a press in the wording does nothing" (1 Sep) on this page. `PORTAL_MODE` is a BOOLEAN (core.js, portal.js); six older sites call it as a function behind a `side` guard — noticed, not fixed; this door reads it as either. Tests: f245 (the door's source), clause-door-verify 17 (a real click; 17b/17c are controls).

## THE BUILDER'S DIVIDER (Young asked 14 Sep 2026)

`tbSplit` / `tbFitSplit` / `tbWireSplit` (js/views/templatebuilder.js): the clause editor's divider ported — the pointer's position, a grab offset, the grid observed, `TB_LEFT_MIN` 380 / `TB_RIGHT_MIN` 340 (the editor's own floors; f306 pins them equal), `TB_FMIN` .45 / `TB_FMAX` .8, its own key `hati.v1.tbLeftFrac`. Where nothing is stored the sheet's own columns hold (a 380px rail) and Home / Enter / a double-click put them back. The grid's 16px gap AND its own padding come off both readings (the handle sat a page-margin left of the seam until they did). `.tb-resizer` is dressed in the builder's own sheet and hidden with the rail. Nothing else on the page moved. Tests: f306 (four claims), prompt-and-build-verify 12 (a real drag, the limit, the reset).

## THE BUILDER'S PAPER SCROLLS IN ITS COLUMN, AND THE RAIL STAYS (Young asked 14 Sep 2026)

*"There needs to be a scrolling feature for the contract being created on the left just like in the editor page. For right hand side in copilot, it should stay intact and not move."* The rail was `position:sticky` inside a slot exactly its own height — a sticky element with nowhere to go. Now `.tb-page` is `height:var(--view-h)` (the register's and the contract room's own mechanism): the strip stays, everything under it is in ONE scroller `#tb-scroll` (`.tb-left` is a flex column; no padding above the paper — the first line of the wording measured 137 before and after), the rail fills the row (`#tb-railslot` flex; never sticky, never `100vh`). **The shell's scrollbar channel is painted by a page that is not a view**: `tbGutter(on)` toggles `#content-scroll.view-fixed` on every `tbPaint` and off at the two ways out (`tbLeave`, `tbPublish`); a `setView` recomputes it in `paintScrollGutter` as before — the note beside `VIEW_OWNS_HEIGHT` names this second writer. A repaint holds the reader's place (`tbHoldScroll` / `tbRestoreScroll`, synchronous and ONCE — `keepScroll`'s second restore on the next frame would undo the `scrollIntoView` Add block does a tick later); a fresh open is a navigation and lands at the top (`tbPaint({ fresh: true })`). **THE FOOT'S SECOND SAVE AND PUBLISH ARE GONE** (Young ruled the same day, once the strip held still): they were the same two acts twice on one screen and the second Publish broke "one filled button per page"; `.tb-strip-foot`, `tb-dirty-bottom`, `tb-save-bottom`, `tb-publish-bottom` are STALE, and the note lives in JS above `tbPaint` — a comment inside the emitted markup puts the retired names back on the page the net reads. A backtick in a comment inside a template literal EVALUATES what is between them (the standing rule, paid again here). Tests: f306 (four claims), prompt-and-build-verify 13 (a real wheel: the paper travels, the rail, the strip and the shell do not; a repaint keeps the place; Back gives the channel back) — every one red at the parent.

## THE BUILDER KEEPS YOUR WORK WHEN YOU PRESS ANYTHING ELSE (the build plan's upgrade 1, 17 Sep 2026)

This was the one screen where an hour could go on one press: Back asked and then discarded, and a **sidebar press did not even ask** — the builder is not a view, so `setView` replaces `#content` under it and `viewLayersClosed` knows only about the clause editor. **THE FIX IS THE KEEPING, NOT A SECOND WARNING**: a guard on every navigation would interrupt a page that is now safe and could not cover a refresh or a closed tab.
- **`tbTouch` IS THE ONE FUNNEL.** Nine presses wrote `_tb.dirty = true` themselves; only `tbTouch` and `tbAdopt` write it now, so a tenth cannot move wording and keep nothing. The FIRST touch of a sitting writes synchronously (the strip the caller repaints is already true); the rest are debounced at `TB_DRAFT_MS` 800.
- **THE STORE**: `hati.v1.tbDrafts`, keyed `tid:vid` (a VERSION, never a template), bounded at `TB_DRAFT_MAX` 6 with the oldest out first. `tbDraftKeep` answers **true only where it really landed** — a private window, blocked site data or a full quota all refuse, and that refusal is a fact: `tbDirtyLine` then prints `tb_unsaved` (the OLD sentence, kept for the one case it is still true of) and `tbLeave` spends the OLD blocking guard.
- **A KEPT DRAFT IS NOT A SAVED VERSION.** It reaches no route (f306 (12) greps the store for `api(`/`fetch(`/`persist(`), never follows anybody to another machine, and the strip says "Draft kept in this browser". `tbSave` is the one place it is spent (`tbDraftSettled`), because leaving it would put those words back over a later save.
- **IT NEVER SPEAKS OVER SOMEBODY ELSE'S SAVE.** Each draft records `base` — `tbDraftBase`, a fingerprint over EXACTLY the two shapes `tbSave` writes, in `tbSave`'s own order. Moved since → `tbAskRestore` asks OVER THE SERVER'S OWN WORDING (painted first, deliberately), and a No keeps the draft. Identical to the server → dropped, never announced.
- **ONE SLOT, THREE STATES, ONE READING.** `tbDirtyLine` / `tbDirtyHtml` replace the copy `tbPaint` and `tbPatchDirty` each carried; `#tb-dirtyslot` is the strip's own slot and no band was added. Discard (`tbDropDraft`, delegated on `.tb-strip` because the slot is repainted without a `tbWire`) is drawn only while a RESTORED draft is showing, and presses `openTemplateBuilder` so the screen cannot disagree with itself.
- **THE SIX QUESTIONS CHANGED TWO THINGS**: Q2 walked the leave dialog DOWN a rung (a transient `'ok'` toast, `tb_kept_toast`) because the old warning was no longer true; and Q2 CUT the shell title from the plan — the Back button prints the template's name twelve pixels below it. Q3 is MEASURED: strip 28px, paper top 146px, identical with Discard drawn and without.
Tests: f306 (12) (eleven claims, eleven red at the parent), prompt-and-build-verify 14 (a real keyboard and a real sidebar press; 14d2 is the pixel claim); f306 (7) and prompt-and-build-verify 5d re-pointed in place.

## ONE DOOR TO A STANDARD CONTRACT (Young's go on "One Door to Standards", 24 Sep 2026, every decision as recommended)

Four doors made a company standard and each copied the words its own way: the owner's SaaS agreement failed through the Copilot conversion (it RE-TYPED the file and ran out of room) and "Save as template" left its 47 tables behind.
- **A TEMPLATE KEEPS THE DOCUMENT IT WAS COPIED FROM**: a block may carry `format:'rich'`, the document's own markup through ONE allowlist on both hosts (`tplFormRichSafe`, the SAME sets as js/richdoc.js — f376 pins the no-drift); `tplFormCopyBlocks` is the ONE copier (a long table is cut between ROWS); `templateFormDocHtml` renders it verbatim and derives no number where the document numbers itself (`tplFormNumbersOff`); a replacement touches words, never markup (`tplFormRichReplace`; the builder's ONE writer `tbReplaceWords`).
- **ONE BUTTON, ONE QUESTION, THREE STARTS** (`js/views/newstandard.js`): `#tpl-new` says `+ New standard contract` (decision 4) → `openNewStandard`, `NS_STARTS` scratch · template · contract; the other side's paper is a quiet foot link onto its own door. `tplNewMenu` and `tplMakeItOurs` are PROXIES (decision 1: Make it ours = the template start on HaTi's tab, that template chosen). No new-paper grant → scratch and template GREY with the reason; the contract start keeps the template manager's gate. **STALE**: `#tpl-convert`, `tplConvertDoor`, `saveContractToLibrary`, `#ws-tpl` on BOTH room menus, `ct_save_as_reusable`; `tplLibCreateModal` and `tplLibUploadModal` have NO CALLER and f376 (2) fails on one coming back — no second door.
- **EVERY START ENDS IN ONE BUILDER**: `nsCreate` + `nsOpen` → `openTemplateBuilder(tid, vid, {start})`; `tbStartWith` is the ONE reading of what a start hands over (per sitting, never saved); the open's `_tb.focus` write stays the only one besides `tbFocus`.
- **COPILOT'S FIRST MOVE, ONE PER START.** Scratch: `/api/ai/outline` with `title:true` also answers a name and a category off `TPL_CATEGORIES`; every row says its source (`tbSourceOf` — lib · std · ai), optional rows unticked, a library section arrives WRITTEN at Add, the playbook's positions are folded BY KIND (`tbKindOf`) as well as by name, the start's read is counted, a refused outline leaves the sentence in the ask box. Template: `tbBlankCandidates` (the received-paper matcher; a `{{marker}}` is a blank already and is never offered) asked in pieces through `aiLabelCandidates` (only asked ids, three kinds, bounded strings), rows UNTICKED (decision 3), an unanswered fact COUNTED (`tb_c_unanswered`). Contract: `tbContractCardHtml` — what was taken out (Put back), departures, missing standards ONCE each (`tbMissingStandards`, shared with the publish question), what the other side changed.
- **A COPIED HEADING OWES NO WORDING**: `tbSectionOwes` is the one reading, asked by the foot, the placeholder, the walk and the publish question.
- **THE HEAD** (decision 5): back square · name · draft chip · Category and Stream chips (`tbHeadHtml`; unfiled = dashed amber "choose"), each opening the ONE Template details box (`tplLibMetaModal(t, {focus, onSaved})`: the caret lands AFTER trapFocus's own tick; `onSaved` repaints the head and never navigates). ONE LINE: the name gives first (`min-width:6em`), a ladder asked of the strip's own width (`container-type:inline-size`) folds the chip keys (<900), the draft chip (<640), Save's word (<620); `#tb-drop` never wraps.
- **PUBLISH ASKS ONCE** (decision 2): `tbOpenItems` → `tbPublishAsk`, every row a door, "Publish anyway" → `tbPublishGo`; nothing open → straight on.
- **ONE OF HaTi'S KEEPS ITS BLANKS**: `tplBuiltinKey` puts a built-in's camelCase key in the template's own lower_snake shape on the marker AND the field (the server slugs a key it cannot take — three of seven were "unplaced").
Tests: f376 (47 claims; sections 2–8 all red at 3e8252f, and each repair red with that repair alone reverted), one-door-verify (37 checks, 0 at the parent); re-pointed in place: f102, f306, f312, f330, f331, f333, n8, dialog-balance-verify, templates-tabs-verify 8/9, templates-cleaned-up-verify 6, prompt-and-build-verify 2e/5c/5d.

## THE WHOLE CONTRACT IS READ, AND A QUOTE IS ONE PASSAGE (21–22 Aug 2026, the CUAD scorecard)

- `aiDocChars` / `aiDocText(req, s)` (200,000, `AI_DOC_CHARS`) is the ONE ceiling for ONE contract on `/api/ai/obligations`, `/brief`, `/playbook`, `/renewal` and the client's own sends (four silent 20,000 slices were removed on BOTH hosts — f230 sweeps both); `aiMaxChars` (60,000) still bounds a PORTFOLIO call; a cap marks the text (`AI_TRUNC_MARK`) and sets `req.aiInputCapped` → `aiNotice`. The brief hashes exactly what it sends.
- `AI_QUOTE_RULE`: a quote is ONE CONTINUOUS PASSAGE; if the document says nothing, return nothing. `AI_REDLINE_RULE(jurisdiction)`: a redline is wording, never a note about wording, the smallest change on a deviation.
- AN ANSWER CUT SHORT IS NOT AN EMPTY ANSWER: `anthropicMessages` records `truncated`; `aiNotice` turns it into a sentence on every route; a partial list is KEPT with the warning, an empty AND cut-off one refused; the obligations ceiling is 8,000 tokens (derived from the schema's maxItems by f230); `startScriptedAi` takes `stopReason`. `maxItems` is ADVISORY — bound after the answer arrives.
- THE OBLIGATIONS READER: ten named kinds, read to the END, the restraint kept, quote bounded at 200 characters; it is INCONSISTENT not blind (the same contract answers 0 then 20), so `ob_none_found` says the scan is not always consistent and offers *Scan again* (the same act). "No obligations found" is a 'warn' toast through i18t.
- THE SCORECARD'S NOISE BAND: on ten contracts a movement under about three contracts in a field is NOISE (measured); FOUND is noisy, CORRECT is not; narrow the band with more CONTRACTS. A CALL THAT NEVER GOT AN ANSWER IS NOT A WRONG ANSWER (`call-failed` excluded from every denominator and PRINTED; `AI_DAILY_SPEND_LIMIT: '0'` on the throwaway server only). WHICH DURATION IN THE SENTENCE IS THE ANSWER: the first duration in a renewal clause is the TERM, not the notice (`pickDuration` / `noticeDuration` / `termDuration` in test/cuad/score.js; a figure disagreeing with a healthy FOUND score is a scorer bug); `noticePeriodDays` says which clause wins and names the trap; `expiryDate` is empty where the document supports no date.
- THE PDF READER against 34 real files (test/pdf/, not in npm test; the two readers in SEPARATE processes; pdf.js is an instrument never a dependency): an indirect `/Length` is resolved in a second pass; the `endstream` fallback stops before the end-of-line; `inflateBytes` trims up to FOUR trailing bytes; `mapChar` asks `/ToUnicode` for single-byte fonts and returns null for "no opinion". Still weak: reportlab-overlay, Arabic, a LaTeX book, annotations. f233.
- THE SCANNING PATH: `capConfidenceForOcr` → `migNeedsReview` trips on `medium` (machine-read paper needs a human ONCE, asked of `textSource`); a cut-short page is KEPT and flagged (`truncated`, `partialPages`, said in `ocrProvenanceLine`; empty AND cut short refused); one loop (render, read, next); `ocrPrepImage` never upscales (`Math.min(1,…)`); the unread-pages range counts off `ocrTotalPages`; `ocrRelease` is called. The harness damages paper AFTER the step that would repair it; a figure disagreeing with a healthy recall score is a harness bug. f234, f235, scan-verify, test/scan/measure.js.
- A GAP IS A GAP: `diffCompareText` (js/versioning.js) normalises the two writers' spacing on the way to a SCREEN (per line collapse, trim, drop blank lines) at the windows — NEVER inside `wordDiff` / `redlineBlocks`, which must stay byte-exact; both Compare windows read it; portal.js reads it through window with the raw text as fallback (f232 proves it published; f239 pins the import). `openDiffModal` has no callers.
- COPILOT'S SUB-PARAGRAPH NOTE IS CONDITIONAL ("If it contains numbered items… treat them as sub-paragraphs… Do not ask for sub-paragraphs the passage does not show"; one site, copilotPropose). A NUMBER IN BRACKETS IS NOT A LIST MARKER: `aiSplitItems`' bracketed marker carries a letter or roman, never digits; a bare marker may not sit inside a bracket (the lookbehind spans the digits); a run-on `(1) … (2)` no longer re-splits (named cost, f97d).
- COMMENTARY IS NOT WORDING, IN EITHER PERSON: `AI_TASK_TALK` (third-person talk about the conversation's own objects — the drafter wants, the passage shown, the playbook's concern, an anchored verdict), `AI_ENDS_ASKING` (a passage ending in a question mark), "me" with a drafting verb, "please confirm whether", the drafting register ("a softer version"), who the reader acts for in the second person; every pattern narrowed by a real clause and MEASURED at zero (then one — a "Note:" footnote) across 7,607 sentences of real drafting (f135f–i); a heading is not a disclaimer ("Disclaimer of Warranty"). `AI_PROPOSAL_FORMAT` / `AI_EDIT_FORMAT` name the SHAPE (a remark about the request itself). Tests: f230, f231, f229, f233–f235, f239, f98, f97d, f135.

## AN OBLIGATION CARRIES AN AMOUNT, AND FIND OBLIGATIONS (30 Aug – 1 Sep 2026)

- `amount` — one field, no currency beside it (the CONTRACT's, `contractCurrency`), blank by default and never zero, absent key when not set; `obligationAmount` / `obligationBandTotal` are the ONE arithmetic; money obeys `canViewValues` (not drawn at all, never dashes; the field carried forward on that path); the sum rides the heading that carries a count; a cross-contract total converts through `fxHome` and says what it left out; the verbs reserve one width; the "Whose obligation" toggle stays and the amount is on both sides. f259.
- Find obligations (J-5.3): `obligationAlreadyOn(c, proposal)` (description, whitespace collapsed, case folded) asked at the DRAW and at the ADD; a duplicate is SHOWN unticked with a word saying why; proposals arrive UNTICKED; the confirmation counts what was already there off the PROPOSALS; zero is its own sentence. THE COUNT FOLLOWS THE TICKS: `obPaintAdd` is the only thing that writes the button's label; one delegated listener; zero is two sentences (`ob_add_none` vs `ob_add_pick`); since 13 Sep 2026 "Nothing new to add" is a LIVE press that closes the window (`btn.disabled = !n && fresh`) and only "Tick one to add" over fresh proposals is greyed. f260.
- M-3: THE SCAN SAYS IT IS WORKING at every door (`OB_FIND_DOORS`, `obFindBusy`, the label remembered on the element, stopped in a `finally`, `.ob-spin` still drawn under reduced motion). M-4: NO DUPLICATE OBLIGATIONS, EVER — the form asks `obligationAlreadyOn` (never against itself). f263.

## THE PAYMENT CHAIN (owner-instructed 31 Aug 2026, L)

One new field `after` (absent = not in a chain; no migration). BLOCKED IS THE DIRECT PREDECESSOR ONLY (no walk, no cycle); a pointer at a step that is not there is not a block; readings match on the ID, never identity; `obligationChains` PARTITIONS; `obState` untouched (the BAND moves — `waiting` outranks `overdue`, a fifth band after `later`); `obligationRoll` returns committed / paid / outstanding / overdue with paid + outstanding = committed by construction; the head counts and the worklist foot grew lines (`.obw-total > i`); `srvObligationBlocked` is the server's twin and a held step fires NONE of the four milestones — on the day it comes due one mail goes to the OWNER saying it is held (the control is written first); ONE door onto the order (the form's "Comes after" picker; refuses a loop in words; never offers itself); held back is a SHAPE (set in, dashed connector), NOT faded (opacity fell under AA). Not built: branching, a paid state, a fifth Insights tab, anything on the counterparty's page. f262, payment-chain-verify.

## OUR STANDARDS — THE ROW OPENS, AND THE PLAYBOOK LEARNS (owner-ruled 9 Sep 2026)

`js/standards.js` is the reading (no view, no route, no store; loads AFTER js/precedent.js because `STD_MIN_ROUNDS` reads `PRECEDENT_MIN` at load — f272 pins the order). Ruling 1: the stance chip (`stdStanceOf(cl)` reads the playbook every draw; `.std-chip-pos` / `.std-chip-fb` are two classes; `scope:'some'` draws an outline; silence draws no chip). Ruling 2: one line clipped by WIDTH (nowrap + ellipsis, the whole sentence on the title; the fallback on the closed row as a figure); the row OPENS (one at a time, repaints the LIST). Ruling 3: the learned proposals read the LAST QUARTER (`STD_WINDOW_DAYS` 92; `precedentMine` gained `since`; `stdHeld` keeps `PRECEDENT_MIN`); THIS REVERSES `precedentSuggestions`' fallback-only rule for the standards page ONLY — a fallback is moved by `precedentAdopt` (which takes the ROW it is adopting), a preferred opens `stdOpenPreferred`, which writes NOTHING (the clause editor's Save does); a met position offers nothing and is drawn only beside a proposal. Ruling 4: the two tabs stay; "Change the position" is a proxy. A FIRST PLAYBOOK READ OFF WHAT WAS SIGNED: `stdUsingDefaults()` is the real day-one case (a COMPARISON, not a blank page); reads `metadata`, proposes only above `STD_DRAFT_MIN` (3) and `STD_DRAFT_SHARE` (half), names what it cannot read; `compare` has three answers — `figure`, `home` (governing law asks `jxNamesHome`; proposes nothing, the market setting is the door), `none`; `topN` decides whether there is a usual value and `seen` is what is printed; "what you usually sign" claims nothing below the floor; nothing on file is not a disagreement; "already matches" needs a pattern; the Adopt that overwrote the drafted clause is gone (`std_adopt*`, `std_draft_adopt_all_*` stale) and every move goes through `stdOpenPreferred` (`std_learn_move_pref`). The page's clothes are written in the page. `std_th_position` is Category / Kategori. Tests: f272, f222, f148, standards-page-verify.

## AN UPLOADED CONTRACT IS READ ON ARRIVAL (owner-ruled 9–10 Sep 2026)

`c.triage` — when, by whom, one entry per reading, `seenAt`; absent on every record on file. The tick-box on the upload screen is the owner's ruling (ticked; NO BOX MEANS NO READING). Three calls, not four (the risk scan is deterministic); a document with no text is read by nothing and pays for nothing (the runner asks "any text at all" before spending; the readers keep their own floor `OBLIG_TEXT_MIN`, named once, three readers); nothing is FILED on the reader's behalf (proposed obligations are HELD on the triage record); `opts.quiet` suppresses the toast and nothing else (a quiet caller may never be quieter and worse); `TRIAGE_STEPS` is the one order; each step in its own try; one audit line. **THE RECORD HAS TO BE ON THE SERVER FIRST**: `flushSaves()` is awaited before the readings (the readings are not awaited); auto-triage-verify section 7 starts at the real File contract button. `api(path, method, body, opts)` takes `opts.quiet`; each reading writes `opts.notice` back and the tile says so. The four tiles (Brief · Standards · Obligations · Filed) are ON THE CONTRACT's Key terms tab (`#kt-triage`, `paintKtTriage`, no acts, put away on Home's own stamp `triageAck`); the Home card is off (`triageCardHtml` has no caller); the signing-route tile is held back. THREE STATES per tile (`TRIAGE_HEADS` names three heads; `triageBusy(c)` reads `_triaging`); the side column is repainted when a reading lands (`renderKeyTermsSide`, called BARE). `triageBriefLine` reads `data.overview`; **the brief tile asks the BRIEF live** (`_brief` AND `_hasBrief`; the note is the fallback; the cap rides only the brief the note describes; the busy state wins); a cut-short brief is KEPT and flagged (see THE GAP-MAP BATCH); `wireKtBriefCard` repaints the strip. A READING ALREADY MADE IS OFFERED, NEVER MADE TWICE: `triageHeldObligations(c)` (less `obligationAlreadyOn`) is checked at the funnel `runFindObligations`, which returns BEFORE the scan into the same review dialog; the Checks row says a reading is waiting in STEEL (`checkVerdict` returns an object with `held`). `triageReadAnything` chooses the headline. Not built: the signing-route tile, template drafts, the phone, the counterparty. Tests: f273, f280, f254, f260, auto-triage-verify.

## COPILOT READS IT WHATEVER DOOR MADE IT (Young ruled 17 Sep 2026)

*"Make sure no matter which door, where you create a contract and it is pulled into the documents tab for negotiation or signing, copilot reads it."* MEASURED first: `triageRun` had ONE caller, the upload, so six of the seven creation sites filed a contract nobody had read — the owner's own reference point ("just like draft new agreement") did not read either.
- **`triageAndPaint(c)`** (js/views/contract.js, published) is the ONE launcher both doors call: flush the save, run, repaint. **It asks `triageOf(c)` FIRST** — `triageRun` stamps a fresh `c.triage` every call and has no memory, so "already read" is asked above it; a reading that FAILED is not silently retried and paid for twice.
- **THE MOMENT IS LEAVING DRAFT, NOT CREATION** (owner's choice of three): at creation a template contract is a form with its blanks in it, and measuring our own standard paper against our own playbook answers nothing. Hooked in `contractLeavesDrafting`, so every send door — the share dialog, all four round-sends inside `reshareToLastRecipient`, the phone — inherits it, and a door added tomorrow does too.
- **THE FUNNEL IS NOT THE ONLY WRITER OF THAT STAGE, so two more doors ask by name**: the action bar's `kind==='review'` and the phone's twin do the funnel's job BY HAND (status, lastAction, audit) rather than calling it — pre-existing drift, reported not fixed. **Two are deliberately left alone and asserted so**: `keyTermsProgress` (auto-promotes mid-typing — a form, not a send) and `rejectApprovalStep` (a demotion, never in Draft).
- **SIGNING IS NOT A SECOND DOOR**: *Before you sign* already runs these three readings and, on the default advise gate, HOLDS the signature until they have. THE ONE DOOR refuses the rest.
- The upload's tick-box still governs the upload's own arrival read; a send reads regardless, which is what the owner asked for.
Tests: f273 section 9 (three of four red at the parent; the fourth is a named control), drafting-stage-verify 1g2/1g3 (a real press — `triage: null` at the parent).

## IT IS READ AT CREATION, AND THE OPEN FIELDS ARE FILLED (Young ruled 17 Sep 2026)

*"anytime you create a agreement, including using the door in image 1, copilot has to read the agreement before it lands in overview. When it lands in overview the open fields have to be pre-filled ... All contracts should have the possibility to fill in from the right hand side panel like in image 4."* **THIS SUPERSEDES COPILOT READS IT WHATEVER DOOR MADE IT of the same morning**, whose "THE MOMENT IS LEAVING DRAFT, NOT CREATION" left every contract landing on Overview reading *Not read yet* — the screenshot that came back. That section's reasoning ("a form with its blanks still in it") is true of ONE door: the wizard asks its questions before it mints anything.
- **`contractArrived(c, opts)` (js/triage.js) IS THE DOOR**, registered beside `roomOpenOnTerms` at every creation site (f327 (4) holds the eighth to it as f170 holds that one). `{bulk:true}` refuses the two importers by name — hundreds at once is a bill nobody pressed; those read on their first send. The UPLOAD site is the one exemption and says so on the spot: the tick-box is the 9 Sep ruling.
- **ONCE PER WORDING, WHICH REPLACED ONCE EVER.** `triageNeedsRead(c)`: no reading → yes; a stamped `triage.hash` differing from now → yes; anything else, including a null on either side → NO. `triageWordingHash` is `playbookHashOf` (the obligations scan's own), never a second hasher. Stamped AFTER the fill step and stamped on a FAILED run too, so a failure is not retried on every send for ever.
- **`triageApplies` IS REVERSED IN PLACE**: every contract with anything on it is read; only an empty record is refused. The old sentence is kept beside it because it is the thing that was wrong.
- **FILLING IS THE FIFTH READING AND RUNS LAST** — it is the only one that CHANGES the paper. `runFillBlanks` (js/ai.js): **the record answers first and for free** (`fillBlanksFromRecord`), a model is shown only what is left, and **no key is not a failure** — the free half has already run. A refused route never undoes it.
- **`js/blanks.js` IS THE ONE READING OF WHAT BLANKS A CONTRACT HAS, and it reads them OFF THE PAPER** (`contractBlanks` walks `docBody(c)`) — the Plain English edition's own rule. Two of the PK template's blanks are declared on no field list at all; the declared list supplies LABELS and decides nothing else. No route, no store, no field (f327 (3) greps).
- **THREE FIELDS ARE NEVER FILLED BY A READING**: `BLANK_NEVER_FILLED` = counterparty · value · termYears (that one moves `c.expiry`). `contractBlankSet` refuses the first two outright whatever either host says — it is the third wall after the route's and the browser's.
- **THE RIGHT-HAND PANEL IS A SECOND DOOR, AND THE OWNER LIFTED REFUSAL 5 BY NAME.** What makes it safe is that there is one ACT: `contractBlankSet` is what the paper's handler and the panel both press, and a RECORD field presses THE PAPER'S OWN INPUT so its write, repaint, status check and audit line stay where they were. `paintContractForm` is the ONE chooser for the one slot — a declared field list takes `renderTemplateFormSection`, everything else takes `renderBlankFormSection`, never both.
- **AN UPLOAD IS COVERED SINCE 20 SEP 2026** — see A DOCUMENT SOMEBODY SENT US HAS BLANKS TOO. **NOT COVERED, AND SAID OUT LOUD**: a contract from a SAVED CUSTOM template. Its wording was written out once at creation with the answers baked in, so there is no blank left to read, and filling one afterwards would mean re-drafting from a template that may have been revised since — the one thing `templateProvenanceHtml` promises does not happen. Needs the owner's ruling.
- MEASURED: the contract does not move a pixel (first ink 397 with the panel and without).
Tests: f327 (51 claims, 50 red at the parent), blanks-panel-verify (20 checks, 12 red), auto-triage-verify 12 (6 checks, 5 red); f273 and f213 re-pointed in place — f213's slice ran to the next `\nfunction ` and an `async function` written under it fell INSIDE the region. **PIN THE REGION, NOT A BOUNDARY THAT HAPPENS TO HOLD.**

## A REFUSAL, A COUNT, A SPINNER AND A DATE (Young ruled 17 Sep 2026)

- **THE VERB LIST IS INVERTED.** *"The copilot error reason code is filling into the box where the drafted clause sits and when you apply it applies in the contract."* — "…, **I risk drafting** something that…", drawn as a template section's wording. `AI_MODEL_VOICE` named ELEVEN verbs the model had been seen using and it wrote a twelfth; the list had been extended three times. **What a model says after "I" is open-ended; WHAT A CONTRACT SAYS AFTER "I" IS CLOSED AND ANCIENT** — `AI_MODEL_INSTRUMENT` (hereby · agree · covenant · certify · appoint · guarantee · waive…) is exempt and everything else is the model. MEASURED on the same 7,607 sentences: FOUR hits, three of them `and I or` (an OCR slash, which is why `or` is exempt) and one a signed CERTIFICATE declaration — the named cost, and it lands in the advice bubble where a reader can still type it.
- **AND THE BUILDER IS A SECOND WALL**: `tbCardWording(a)` is ONE reading with two askers — the card draws no Apply for a remark about the job, and `tbAccept` refuses it in words. The first wall is a judgement about sentences; this one is at the press that writes.
- **THE CLAUSE SAYS A COUNT, NEVER A VERDICT.** *"What if the counterparty accepts one change and declines another change in the same clause?"* — the chip read `ladderSettled`, the FIRST accepted rung, and announced it. `ladderTally` / `ladderTallyText` print *"2 changes · 1 agreed · 1 refused"*; a zero part is dropped. `ng_rung_settled` / `_refused` / `_withdrawn` are STALE, inert in both books.
- **A READING IN FLIGHT TURNS** (reverses the note's own "no animation is owed"): the busy chip holds `.ob-spin`, the product's own, with its reduced-motion answer. **The chip's height is STATED from the ladder's chip rung** — measured, a tick resolved 16px and the spinner 11, so the row twitched when a reading landed.
- **A DAY IS A DAY.** `ktDayDot` is the ONE printer where there were FOUR, and only one was guarded. `runScan` stamps `on` (ISO) beside `at` (the locale sentence its own panel prints). **THE SAME NaN WAS REPORTED ON 13 SEP** and fixed at one of the four call sites; four days later it arrived from another. A fault fixed at one of four call sites is waiting for the next.
- NOT TOUCHED by the owner's word: the Checks card saying "21 proposed" beside the Overview's "0 found" (two true readings of different things).
Tests: f328 (42 claims, 36 red at the parent), ladder-verify 22 (the parent prints `Settled · R1 accepted` on the owner's own case), auto-triage-verify 13 (the spinner measured animating); f135's corpus census and f306's tbAccept window re-pointed in place — **PIN THE REGION, NOT A BYTE COUNT**, twice in one week.

## THE OVERNIGHT DESK — THREE KINDS OF PREPARED WORK (owner-ruled 9 Sep 2026)

**Prepared for you** on Home above *Needs your decision* (**under the Map since 24 Sep 2026; for that one day the only list on Home, and since 25 Sep above the two-row decisions card again, `deskCids` evicting again — see THE MAP ON HOME**): a NOTICE ready to serve, a promise they are late on, paper they sent that nobody has read, a renewal closing — AT MOST ONE OF EACH (`deskShown`; **`DESK_MAX` 3 states the ceiling since 16 Sep 2026, because with four kinds "one of each" and "at most three" stopped being the same sentence**; a kind with nothing draws nothing); the sub-line counts the rows on screen (`desk_showing` stale; Discard all still puts away the whole population); ONE DOOR — `deskCids` reads the rows ON SCREEN and evicts their renewals from the decisions list (the renewal source only; a NOTICE row counts too, and a contract never carries both — the notice is the sharper form of the same decision); a reading with one stamp (`c.desk`, a map row key → day, absent on file; dismissed is dismissed), no route, no sweep, no spend (js/desknight.js; f274 greps); every act is the product's own (`obligationChase`, `openWorkspace` + `roomGoTab`, the stamp); a verb that cannot work is not drawn; the deviations row is a pointer to the contract and clears with the strip; the tag carries the urgency and the meta carries the facts; `fmtDDay` is one definition (never `fmtDocDate` on a screen); the header claims no clock time ("Prepared for you"; nothing was sent or filed). Not on the phone. THE RENEWAL NOTE IS WRITTEN BEFORE ANYBODY ARRIVES: `runRenewalPrep` (server) runs `aiRenewalAdvice` (lifted out of its route with `renewalSignalsOf`; `doc` comes in) for contracts entering the window — THE OWNER PAYS (`meter.who` from `c.owner`; an ownerless contract is not prepared), once per renewal cycle (the reminders table keyed on the DECISION DATE; only a successful uncut call writes the row), three bounds (the daily ceiling asked by hand, `aiRenewalPrepMax` per run, the `aiRenewalPrep` switch — absent = ON, on the Copilot engine panel), writes nothing to the contract record, rides the same timer under its own catch, async. `_renewalPrep` rides the LIST ('night' / 'you'); the desk asks it first. The header still claims no clock time; the row says "memo prepared for you". Tests: f274, f275, f219, home-page-verify 11, settings-tabs-verify.

## THE EMPTY CHANGE COLUMN IS A DOOR, AND PREPARE REDLINES HAS TWO (Young ruled 12 Sep 2026)

The audit's first finding: the one screen with nothing to read described the work instead of offering it. **THE OWNER LIFTED THE ONE-DOOR REFUSAL BY NAME** — *"do not delete the feature in image 2"* — so the act keeps its More-menu row AND gains a button in the empty column.
- **TWO DOORS ARE ONLY SAFE AS ONE ACT**: `rlPrepareRowHtml` builds BOTH buttons (one greying, one set of words; the column asks `negoExecuted` itself and draws nothing on a sealed record), and ONE handler answers both — `querySelectorAll('[data-rl-prepare]')`, never `querySelector`.
- **WHERE IT IS WIRED IS LOAD-BEARING**: the cards column is painted into its mount PARTWAY DOWN `renderRedline`, so the head block above that paint cannot see the column's button. The handler lives in `rlWireClauseTools` (after the mount, holding the contract and the repaint). Bound in the head block it was drawn, live-looking and DEAD — found by a real press, not by reading.
- `rlEmptyColumnActsHtml` draws the pair; `mayStart` is the one gate (our seat, not a preview, not a reading, `editable && canAct`). **Every seat the buttons do not reach KEEPS THE SENTENCE** — a branch, never a deletion.
- **"Edit a clause"** chooses its destination at the DRAW, as the pencil does (`rlEditorTakesIt` → `data-rl-edit-first="editor|panel"`); the handler obeys the attribute and never re-decides. It is wired in `wireNegotiationTab` and reaches the page through **`openEditor`, the ONE named reading** — written in `rlWireClauseTools` calling `rlOpenClauseEditor` itself, f245 (1) and (19) went red the same minute. **`rlFirstClauseId` is asked at the PRESS**, because reading the clause list initialises the negotiation — READING MUST NOT WRITE; no clause is a spoken refusal (`ng_empty_none`), never a greyed lie.
- THE CLOTHES FOLLOW THE BUILDER: the menu's rule is scoped to `.rl-more-menu`, so this second home dresses the button itself (`.rl-empty-acts`) — and drops the shared pencil there, because the pencil is also the product's own sign for "edit a clause" and the pair read as two of the same act.

## THE NOTE WINDOW HAS TWO ROOMS, AND COMES UP ON EVERY FILING (Young ruled 11 Sep 2026, C-3 / C-5)

The globe line that read the choice back IS the choice: the drawer's own tabs (`.rl-np-tabs`, `data-rl-note-room`), **INTERNAL lit at rest since D-6 (11 Sep, the owner's later word the same day — reverses this section's own "External lit at rest" and the 1 Sep ruling)**; lead, placeholder and tint follow the room; Add note posts the room's own answer through `negoPostComment` (`'shared'` on External, nothing on Internal — the writer's safe default; no third value); the channel is asked only for the external room; the toast names the room (`ng_note_added_int`); on a note already on file the pair is SET and disabled (a room is fixed at posting, as in the drawer). **ONE WINDOW, TWO DRAFTS (D-6)**: the box holds a draft PER ROOM (`drafts` in `openChangeNoteDialog`; a tab press stores and swaps, posts nothing; a dot on the OTHER room's tab while its draft holds words); Add note posts each non-empty draft to its own room through `negoPostComment` once per room (the channel asked for the external half only; `ng_note_added_both`); greyed with the reason while neither holds words; Skip/scrim/Escape ask through `confirmDialog` where either holds words (`quit()`); a note on file keeps the one-room shape. **THE LEAD IS ONE LINE BY CONSTRUCTION (D-5)**: `.rl-note-lead` reserves one line-height (nowrap, hidden, ellipsis as the net) and the six lead keys are written to fit it (f302 (6) pins a ceiling of 84 with the longest test name; the window's height is measured EQUAL across rooms in notes-two-rooms-verify). **Decision D of 31 Aug is REVERSED**: `rlNoteAskAfterFile` asks on every filing on our seat; the `revisions` reading survives as what makes the window SAY it is a revision (`ng_note_revised_lead*`) and open on the note already given (Save); a DELIVERED note (`negoNoteDelivered`) is a record — box empty, printed above.
