## THE PENCIL FILES ON ONE PRESS (Young reported it 11 Sep 2026, C-4)

MEASURED with the button HELD: the toolbar row grew 45 → 81 px and the pencil moved 36 px out from under the pointer; the mouseup landed on a paragraph and no click fired — the 30 Aug rail-foot fault in a second costume (nothing rebuilt; the control pushed away). The mousedown blurs the box, the blur pulled the text, `ceApply` spoke `ce_applied` into `#ce-say`, the span took width off the tools, the tools wrapped. **`.ce-barrow .ce-say` has a flex basis of ZERO** (`flex:999 1 0`, max 300): it takes free space only, so the row's height with the line on equals its height with it off, by construction. **The blur pull is QUIET** (`cePullText` → `ceApply(..., {quiet:true})` — the reader's own typing read back is not a message). Where the span measures under `CE_SAY_MIN_W` (60 px) the sentence goes out through the toast ('warn') as well — chosen by MEASUREMENT, never a breakpoint; the order's reserved row would have grown the chrome at that rung (refusal 3). Tests: clause-editor-verify 31 (a real press; 4 of 5 fail at the parent).

## THE PLAIN ENGLISH EDITION IS ONE CLAUSE OUT ON THE COMPANY STANDARDS (C-6, owner-reported 11 Sep 2026)

Every reading sat one clause low: rows went out `[0]…[n-1]` beside headings beginning "1.", "2.", so the model read the clause number as the row number; the browser's guard compared the server's own stamped heading with the same list and could not see it; the wrong pairing was CACHED.
- THE ROW KEY CANNOT BE MISTAKEN FOR THE CLAUSE NUMBER: `readKeyOf(i)` sends `[R0]…`, the tool's `key` is a STRING (`readKeyIndex` answers -1 for anything else); `i` (integer) is gone.
- THE ANSWER RESTS ON SOMETHING CHECKABLE: `heading` is REQUIRED and is the row's heading ECHOED (a row with none: its first eight words, `readEchoOf`), compared after `readNorm` — the server's twin of `_docReadNorm`. A failed entry is DROPPED AND COUNTED (`unmatched`); the echo is drawn nowhere. The browser's `docReadAnchors` stays as the second wall.
- A SHIFTED ANSWER IS REFUSED WHOLE, NOT CACHED: more than a quarter failing → `partial:true`, paired readings still returned, NOTHING written to `clause_readings`. The column foot (`.doc-read-partial`, `ct_read_partial_*` + `ct_read_again`) carries the force press (`docReadRun(c,{force:true})`); `docReadHeld` is the ONE reading of "is there an edition to show" for the switch and the painter.
- THE HASH MOVES BY CONSTRUCTION (the key is inside the hashed text) — every stale pairing is re-asked on the next press; no migration.
- The rule's heading block says COPY the row's heading, never write one (the old invented-heading block would have failed every echo).
Tests: f300 (0 of 7 at the parent), f277 (19), plain-english-verify 16 (6 of 8 fail at the parent, none time out).

(CLAUDE.md)
## THE WRITER WRITES NO PARAGRAPH FOR WHITESPACE (owner-reported 11 Sep 2026, C-8)

The paper is INDENTED markup; a browser ignores the gap between two blocks and the Word writer read each one as a paragraph holding one space (32 paragraphs, 21 empty, on an ordinary export). **`close()` in `docxRunsFromHtml` is the ONE place**: a paragraph is written only where a run carries a VISIBLE character (`/\S/`) or where it is `forced` — a `<br>` or a page break. A typed blank line IS a `<br>`: `sanitizeRich` stores `<p><br></p>` and removes any empty block without one, so the writer tells a deliberate blank line from indentation by construction; a spaces-only paragraph cannot reach the record. Not touched: `_dxSpace` (its tab is the hanging indent's, f288), the styles' `after="160"`, the markup. Measured on the parent: 2 paragraphs pretty-printed → 7 (now 2); a contract body → 34 with 26 empty (now 8, 0). "Empty" is counted by what Word DRAWS, not by the absence of a run. Tests: f288 (6).

## THE MAP COPILOT GETS EVERYTHING THE MAP KNOWS (C-1, owner-reported 11 Sep 2026)

`GRAPH_GROUPINGS` (js/views/intelligence.js) is the ONE list of what the graph groups by — the dropdown, `groupLabelOf`, the caption (`graphGroupingWord`), the cue table (`GRAPH_GROUP_CUES` / `graphGroupCue`) and the refusal read it; the server's `GRAPH_GROUP_KEYS` MIRRORS it and its tool enum is `[...GRAPH_GROUP_KEYS,'custom']` (f299 pins the SET). Copilot names the DIMENSION; HaTi cuts the buckets. Four time groupings — signedYear / signedQuarter (`contractSignedAt`), expiryYear (`effectiveExpiry`), createdMonth (`repRaisedAt`, the `_raisedAt` transport) — each ONE reading; a contract without the fact is its own named `none` bucket.
- THE CARD IS FIELDS, NEVER WORDING: `graphCopilotCard(c)` borrows `graphNodeFacts` and the readings the graph already asks; `value` only with `canViewValues`, stripped again by `scopeAiPortfolio` and the route (THE SERVER IS THE WALL); `GRAPH_ASK_CAP` 600, said. `where` is applied by HaTi (`graphWhereIds`, `GRAPH_WHERE_KEYS` on both hosts); an id list is intersected.
- `intelGraphApply(q, res)` is the ONE judge: an unknown key or a `custom` map placing nothing under two labels is read by `graphGroupCue` first, else REFUSED in words (`int_group_refused`) with the map untouched; the chat line is composed off `buildGraphModel()` (`int_did_*`); Copilot's sentence is a second line only where `graphSaysMore`. `igPaintGroupSelect` makes the dropdown follow.
- `graphAskScreen()` travels (grouping, lenses, crowded, lang, currency; server `graphScreenSays` clamps); `graphCopilotContext()` joins `ctx.graph` (streams · cliff · facts · lenses), said by `aiGraphSays` / server `graphSays` line for line.
- **ONE COPILOT, ONE FIGURE (owner's go, 12 Sep 2026 — WORKORDER-copilot-one-figure.md)**: `portfolioFigures(ctx)` / `portfolioSays(f)` (server) and `aiPortfolioFigures()` / `aiPortfolioSays(f)` (browser, the SAME body — f305 pins the two sentences equal) DEFINE "value under management" the Home tile's way (`copilotRows`, not archived, not Declined, `fxHome`, `fxMissing` said) and ride EVERY brain's system block (`buildCopilotSystem`, `_localSystem`) and the graph route, with the rule to quote and never add. A WHOLE-BOOK ASK is a count and a door: the rule in both HOW TO WORK blocks, `wholeBook` on `deliver_answer` (both schemas, `normalizeDeliver`, both final sends), `aiWorklistHtml(list,{wholeBook})` draws `data-ai-worklist-all` onto the full register (`regShowOnly([], '')`). `AI_CHAT_STEPS` 8 on both hosts (three loops; the 40-row page stays). THE STAND-IN SAYS WHY: the server's error carries `kind` (provider · rateLimit · spendCap · noKey; `err.kind` in api.js), `aiDegrade` names it (`ai_engine_provider/_ratelimit/_spend`) and a whole-book ask (`aiWholeBookAsk`, narrow on purpose) gets HaTi's own count and the Contracts door, never a keyword match. f299 (11a–d), f305 (one figure, both hosts), answer-worklist-verify 6–7.
- **THE MAP'S COPILOT QUOTES HATI'S FIGURES, NEVER ADDS (12 Sep 2026)**: it answered 333 contracts / 1.15bn against the Home tile's 159 live / 833M because it summed 600 own-currency cards and the two-sided stream table. `graphPortfolioFigures(ctx)` (server) — `copilotRows` (the ONE loader, shared with `copilotList`), not archived, not Declined (the Home tile's live rule), `fxHome` total, `fxMissing` said — rides the prompt as `graphPortfolioSays` above the cards with the rule "quote these; never add up the cards"; money only with canViewValues, scope the reader's own. f299 (11).

## THE EXECUTED PAPER IS READ, THE HEADINGS ARE DRAWN, THE SIZES ARE MEASURED (Part D, 11 Sep 2026 — WORKORDER-plain-english-four-reports.md)

Four screenshots, each measured on a real page before a line moved.
- **D-1 THE PAPER'S OWN HEADING IS DRAWN, READING OR NOT** (option A): `docReadAnchors` keeps every sent row with a NAME (`row.kind==='section'||row.ownHead`) or a reading; `it.head` decides nothing. The server keeps every paired row (the `!plain && kind!=='section'` drop is gone). The one silence: a bare number over nothing.
- **D-2a THE KIND IS A FACT ABOUT THE ROW**: `kind` is `section` only for a heading with NO wording of its own; a heading WITH wording is a `clause` (template paper draws every clause as h4 over p, and the prompt tells the model a SECTION reads EMPTY — "executed contracts come back with nothing"). `headed` carries the drawing question (h3/.dr-s vs h4/.dr-h). **This moved the sent shape and the hash once, on purpose.**
- **D-2b THE SEAL CARD IS FURNITURE**: `.seal-in` joined `DOC_READ_FURNITURE`; the last row stops at the first furniture AFTER it (`furnitureAfter`), never the canvas end — signers' e-mail addresses were being sent as clause 4's wording.
- **D-2c A REFUSED PAIRING SAYS WHY**: `readings.failed[{key,echo,want}]` rides `_readings` and the server log; `readEchoFold` folds a leading SECTION/CLAUSE label, a `num —` and `heading:` on BOTH sides (the label the route itself wrote — the leading hypothesis for "8 could not be matched"); the doc line puts the heading on its own `heading:` line and the rule names it.
- **D-3a A PAPER SEALED BEFORE 22 AUG FOLLOWS THE READER ON SCREEN**: `#doc-zoom .doc-surface .text-\[13\.5px\],…\[13px\]{font-size:inherit}` in HaTi's sheet (the blob resolves them to a FLAT 15/14px); the sealed markup, the hash, the PDF and the counterparty copy untouched. **D-3b** every entry writes `--dr-hsize` measured off the heading it faces (`.dr-h`/`.dr-s` read it; the typed ratios are fallbacks). **D-3c** `rlSetDocType` repaints the edition in the same press (`layer._docReadC`); the ResizeObserver stays for the paper's own reflows.
- **D-4** `text-wrap:balance` is gone from `.dr-s`; the overlap in the owner's screenshot did NOT reproduce on the h3 shape (17h measures no overlap) — said in the run log, not called fixed.
- plain-english-verify 6c re-pointed (`painted <= 1`): C-7 took the upload's one h-tag and left it red — proved at the parent.

## THE BUILD PLAN — GROUPS 1 TO 5 (owner-approved 13 Sep 2026; the plan is the artifact "HaTi Build Plan")

Six pieces, one idea each. Group 6 (the Word round-trip and BankID) is NOT built, by the owner's word.

**G1 · THREE FACTS MOVE TO WHERE THE WORK IS.** `ceClauseFindings` (this clause's OWN located findings, `ceScanGroups().here`, never the contract-wide `missing`) is a reading only — **the auto-open onto the Playbook tab was REVERSED the same day** (Young: the pencil must land on Suggestions); the rail opens on `chat` unless a caller names `tab:'scan'`, and nobody does. `ceGreetingHtml` prints precedent + their ask and DELIBERATELY NOT the playbook (Prepare redlines and the scan tab each say it already). `reviewDaysWaiting` (null on a review with no stamp) and `reviewRemind` — the ASK'S OWN ROUTE with `reminder:true`, so one place decides who is written to; `rv.reminded` stamped only where something left. The counterparty's wall is ONE line (`po_wall_live`): the held count was the Send button's own label, and "marked shared" described a switch that seat does not have. f306, f180 re-pointed.

**G2 · THE SEND IS ONE SCREEN, AND WORD IS A CHANNEL.** `step(1)` and `step(2)` are one state; the kind question is a quiet door (`#share-other`), not a toll gate. `shareSummaryStepHtml` takes `oneScreen` — lead title, folded manifest, no footer; step 2's head hides. **THE 2 AUG 2026 RULING IS KEPT**: the purpose is a row ON the screen (`sharePurposePickerHtml(c, sel, {compact})`, ONE painter for both shapes, the chosen answer's sentence under the row). **`ch === 'word'`** posts the .docx (base64) on `POST /api/shares` — one branch, never a second route: the share row, the round, the audit line and the version capture all happen as for a link. `wordTrackedFile` is ONE builder for the download and the attachment. A file is NEVER durable and its URL never travels; Sign greys with the reason; the link settings hide. f307, f42 and f45 re-pointed.

**G2.2 · WHAT CAME BACK, IN ONE LINE.** `negoRoundRead` / `negoRoundLine` (js/views/negotiation.js, beside negoNeedsYouIds) read `c.changes` and `c.negotiation` RAW. TWO DEPARTURES FROM THE PLAN, both toward its own rules: it SPENDS NOTHING (every figure was already on the record, "start with clause 4" included) and it ADDS NO BAND — it rides the alert row that already says the round arrived (`a.sub`). f307 (4).

**G3 · THE CHECK BEFORE A CONTRACT IS SIGNED** — see its own section below.

**G4 · A TEMPLATE'S OWN PROMISES** — `TEMPLATE_OBLIGATIONS` beside `TEMPLATE_PAY` (js/templates.js), hand-written, every entry quoting its own template's clause. `templateObligationDue` REFUSES rather than guesses (no date it counts from, no number of days, no words → nothing minted); `mintTemplateObligations` asks `obligationAlreadyOn` and stamps `origin:'template'`; called from the Key terms edit. Said out loud in the code: the built-ins' rejection windows and per-invoice terms are counted from an event HaTi holds no date for, so they get no entry. f308 (7).

**G5.1 · WHICH NOTES ARE NEW TO THIS READER.** `c.notesRead` (reader id → when they last had the drawer open; absent on file). `negoNotesReadAt` / `negoNoteUnread` / `negoMarkNotesRead` (moves only forwards, writes nothing when it has not moved, stamped AFTER the paint). Never travels (not on the payload) and never drawn on their seat. The bell is NOT this: it counts only notes that name you, per browser. f309.

**NOT BUILT, and named so nobody assumes otherwise**: G5.2 (a one-time code before a guest's first Send) and Part B phase 6 (the template baseline read once per version).

## THE SEND OPENS ON THE SEND, AND THE RECORD IS ITS OWN LINK (Young reported it 13 Sep 2026)

Two faults off the one-screen send's first day. **THE FIRST FRAME IS THE ONE SCREEN**: `oneScreenHtml(pre, o)` inside `openShareModal` is the ONE builder for the opening paint (`_noPre`, `opening:true` greys Send) and the settled paint (`shareFillModal(oneScreenHtml(pre))`); `shareKindStepHtml(c, sel, {hidden})` is folded from the first frame; the width is the OPENING's to set (46rem — the fill keeps the frame); `shareWireOpening` holds purpose presses, the quiet door and the kind cards, and what was typed into the first frame WINS over the prefill (`_held`). `shareOpeningHtml` is GONE. **THE SIGNER CARD FOLLOWS THE KIND**: `setKind` hides `#share-signers` on the record. **A HISTORY SEND NEVER REUSES A LINK**: the dialog's reuse predicate refuses `purpose==='history'` (a history link is a read-only pass the PUT refuses anyway), and the server's `PUT /api/shares/:token/payload` refuses a `purpose:'history'` payload onto a contract link (409) — the row's purpose is what is served. **THE WORD FILE IS WHAT THE SENDER CHOSE** (the same evening): `wordHistoryFile(c)` (js/views/contract.js, beside `wordTrackedFile`) is the record's file — `negoHistoryWordHtml(c, report)` is `negoHistoryExportHtml` (the ONE report Export and Print give) cut to its body, less the event glyphs and the colour legend, handed to `docxExportTracked`; `doSend` picks the builder by kind; the server's Word branch reads the row's `purpose` and words the mail as the record (nothing to mark up); `co_ch_word_hist_note` is the channel line on the record (`paintChNote`, asked by `setCh` and `setKind`). **THE DIALOG OPENS ON EMAIL, EVERY TIME** (the same night): `let ch='email'` — `pre.channel` (the last send's) is NOT read by the dialog; the round send keeps it. **THE CONTRACT'S CHECKS STAND DOWN ON THE RECORD** (owner-ruled the same night): `#share-readiness-wrap` is hidden by `setKind` on the record and `doSend` asks for `#sh-ack` only on a contract kind. **THE TITLE SAYS WHAT IS GOING OUT**: `shareLeadTitle(c, s, purposeSel)` is the ONE reading (the builder and `setKind` both ask it; `co_send_history_to` on the record, both books). ## THE CHECK BEFORE A CONTRACT IS SIGNED (owner-approved 13 Sep 2026 — WORKORDER-pre-signature-check.md Part A)

A contract is read when it ARRIVES and never again, but the wording that gets signed is not the wording that arrived.
- **THE REVIEW REMEMBERS WHAT IT READ.** `runPlaybookReview` stamps `wordingHash` (`playbookHashOf` = the obligations scan's own simhash) + `checkedAt` at the ONE place the review is BUILT, both branches. `playbookStale(c)` has THREE answers — true / false / **null = we do not know** (no hash on file). The server's overnight sweep deliberately does NOT stamp (it has no copy of the hasher; a drifted twin would say "stale" for ever), so its reviews read as unknown and unknown is offered a re-read.
- **`js/signcheck.js` IS THE ONE READING**: `signCheck(c)` → ready · standards · obligations · record · open · anyOpen · checked · current. It spends nothing, writes nothing, decides nothing. READING MUST NOT WRITE: `negoOpenPoints` is asked ONLY where `c.negotiation` already exists. Money only with `canViewValues` — the row is not drawn at all rather than drawn as dashes.
- **THE CARD** (`signCheckCardHtml`, js/views/contract.js) is in the signing COLUMN, in the arrival strip's `.kt-tri` shape BORROWED BY ID, drawn only once `ready`. `is-unknown` is a NEW chip tone: a ruby dash on an unread contract reads as an accusation. Acts: `signCheckAccept` (reason REQUIRED, `SIGN_ACCEPT_MAX` 240, index taken BEFORE the filter), `signCheckOpenClause` (`rlPbFindClause`, refuses rather than guesses), `signCheckKeep` (`c.recordAccepted[field]`), `runFindObligations`, `focusKeyTerms`.
- **THE SWEEP** `runSignCheck` runs only what is out of date (`stale !== false`, `unread !== false`), asks once naming the cost, and stamps `c.signCheck` ONLY where everything came back — a cut-short answer is not a check.
- **THE GATE** `signCheckGate()` off · advise · require (admin, **ADVISE by default since 13 Sep 2026** — see ONE LIST BEFORE THE SIGNATURE); `signCheckBlocker` is ONE row in `signBlockers`, counting `signCheckHolding`. **THE SERVER IS THE WALL**: `signCheckRefusal(stored)` at BOTH doors (the in-app save as a difference, and the respond route). It enforces two of the three questions and SAYS SO: the obligations-moved question rests on a browser-side hash, and a server twin that drifted would refuse real signatures.
Tests: f308 (38 claims).

## ONE LIST BEFORE THE SIGNATURE (Young's "go" on the signing audit, 13 Sep 2026)

Five surfaces printed five counts and none was a door. **`signReadiness(c)` (js/signcheck.js) is the ONE reading of what stands between this reader and signing** — rows in weight order: the `signBlockers` rows (every one HOLDS; the intent tick left that list, a `signers` row joined it), the check's own rows (`signCheckRows`, holding or merely shown by the GATE), the risk scan's open HIGH findings (shown, never holding); `holds` · `noted` · `settled`; `opts.light` drops the two hash rows on a register row (the LIGHT LIST). READING MUST NOT WRITE: signBlockers asks negoSigningBlockers only where a table exists.
- **THE GATE IS ADVISE BY DEFAULT** (`SIGN_CHECK_GATE_DEFAULT`, server `scGate` mirrors): `off` holds nothing; `advise` holds **AN UNRUN CHECK** (the `standards-read` and `obligations` rows hold until `signCheck.current` — owner-ruled the same evening: signing before the check ran was "nonsensical"; a never-read obligations list is its own row, `never`; browser-side only, said in the code) and an ESCALATED departure until the colleague asked or an admin accepts it (`signCheckMayAccept`, `signCheckAcceptedProperly` judge the STAMP — `accepted.byId`/`role`, `v.escalation.to`); `require` holds every open row. `signCheckRowHolds` is the one rule; the server's `srvSignCheckOpen(c,{gate})` / `signCheckRefusal` read the same three, at both doors, on every gate but off.
- **ESCALATE MEANS SOMEBODY ELSE**: `signCheckEscalate` stamps `v.escalation` (the review's candidates, a real dialog), then `POST /api/contracts/:id/escalate` TELLS the colleague (member id in, addresses refused, scope checked, link to the Signing tab); the signer alone can never accept it. Ask a colleague leads every escalated row, admin included.
- **EVERY ROW IS A DOOR** (`signCheckCardHtml` draws `signReadiness`; the four tiles are gone): Ask a colleague · Accept with a reason · Open the clause · Take the paper's value (`signCheckTake`, writes where Key terms writes) · Fix on Key terms (`focusKeyTerms(c)`, it took the field name before) · Keep the record · Read it (`scanGoTo`, lifted out of the scan card) · Dismiss with a reason (`signRiskDismiss`, same store) · Run the check · Find obligations · Add signers · Open the negotiation · Place it on the paper. Settled rows fold under their count (`_scSettledOpen` per sitting).
- **THE BUTTON IS THE LIST**: "Sign — N to settle" (live, `data-sign-holds`, the press is `signLandOnList` onto the first held row) · "Sign — N noted" (rows on the hover) · "Sign as X". No paragraph of blockers, no red `ct_high_findings` line (both are rows). The head says `signHeadLabel` ("Sign · N to settle", `signPaintHeadLabel` re-words it after every act; `'sign-scroll'` RETIRED, the press lands on the list while anything holds); the bell's signature row and Home's decision row (`mySignings`) quote the same N.
- **THE REASON WRAPS AND SAYS WHERE IT LIVES**: the accept and dismiss boxes are `multiline`; `sc_accept_msg` names the settled fold and the History tab; the fold's button says show/hide. **THE TERM FACT PRINTS THE DATE THE TERM WAS READ FROM** (`roomFactsHtml`'s `expIso` — `c.expiry` else `metadata.expiryDate`, the span's own fallback; "364 days to NaN.NaN.NaN" was an empty record field formatted).
- **THE INTENT LINE IS THE PAD'S FIRST LINE**: `openSignaturePad({intent:true})` draws it and refuses Adopt & sign without it; `consent` rides the result; `signConsentStamp` writes `c.compliance.consent` and the Consent audit line. The counterparty's pad is untouched. `data-comp="consent"` is gone from the Signing tab; `ct_tick_then_sign`, `ct_review_sign_below`, `ct_high_findings_*` are STALE, inert in both books.
Tests: f311 (34 claims), signing-flow-verify (18 real presses), f308 (3)(5), f256, f167, f176, f99, sign-links-verify 5 re-pointed.

## A NAMED PERSON'S YES BEFORE ANYONE SIGNS (Young ruled 23 Sep 2026 — "Implement scenario 2")

An admin marks a person whose contracts are not signed until a named colleague approves them, **whatever the value**. **Scenario 1 (the Review Desk, filing signed copies) is NOT built** — the owner has not approved it. The owner marked no decisions, so every default is the proposal's own recommendation.
- **`js/signapproval.js` IS THE ONE READING ON BOTH HOSTS** (shared as js/graphwhere.js is): `saRuleOf` (the person's `overseerOn` always|off · `overseerId` approver · `overseerBackupId` · `overseerWhen` lead,sign — an ABSENT `overseerOn` reads the retired workspace switch, `signApprovalLegacyOn`, so the deploy changes nothing), `saNeeds` (the rule bites where the person LEADS — the contract's owner — or is NAMED TO SIGN; one need per approver; a gone approver falls to the backup, none to the admins), `saState` (unasked · pending · approved · refused · lapsed), `saMayDecide` (approver · backup · admin — NEVER the asker, never a person the rule is about).
- **THE REQUEST IS ITS OWN RECORD**, `c.signApprovals` (absent on file): asked by the lead, carrying `stamp` (hashes of the wording as TEXT — so formatting and notes do not lapse it — value, currency, dates, parties, signers) and `shows` (what the approver reads). It LAPSES on any stamp drift, or `SA_UNUSED_DAYS` 30 after an approval nobody used. A refusal and an admin's decision need a reason.
- **THE CHAIN DRAWS IT, NEVER STORES IT**: `buildApprovalChain` appends one `sa:true` step per need AFTER the rule steps; `approveContract` / `rejectApprovalStep` decide a rule step first, else hand to `signApprovalDecide` — so the gate card, the Approvals page and the phone press one verb. **A decision pressed on the OLD overseer step is NOT honoured** (it had no request and no named decider): those contracts are asked again.
- **`signBlockers` carries one `signapproval` row per need** (stage `people`, `rowKey 'sa:'+key`). `signBlockers(c,{noSa:true})` is the paper-only reading `signApprovalRequestable` asks — the recursion guard, read off `arguments[1]` because five tests pin the one-argument signature. The Sign button says **Send for approval** only where the approval is ALL that holds (`saOnly`), then **Waiting on X's approval**. **ONCE SIGNING HAS STARTED** (a signature, or a counterparty's signed response) it asks nothing.
- **A NEGOTIATION IS NEVER STOPPED BY IT** — `contractReadiness` counts rule steps only; a refusal usually means "ask them for 45 days", a round that has to go out. What it holds is SIGNING: the sign-purpose link, the route's links (`signApprovalHoldsLinks`, drawn disabled with the reason; the internal row shows no resend and no "email failed" while held) and the internal signer's turn notice (`awaiting-approval`, released by the approval).
- **THE SERVER IS THE WALL**: `srvSignApprovalMerge` (the stored list is the base; a new request is server-stamped; only the named decider decides; a stale echo keeps the stored decision), `srvSignApprovalRefusal` on PUT, POST /api/shares purpose sign → 409, respond action sign → 409 with a NEUTRAL sentence — **the counterparty never learns an approval exists** (`signApprovals` is not on the payload allow-list).
- **MAIL**: `POST /api/contracts/:id/sign-approval-notify` (ask · remind · decided; member ids in, a body address refused; remind once a day, 429 after; the value left out for a reader without money; `mailReport`). `runSignApprovalReminders` on the reminder sweep: `SA_REMIND_WORKDAYS` 2 reminds the approver once, `SA_ESCALATE_WORKDAYS` 5 tells the backup and the admins. Every screen says sent / outbox / not configured.
- **TRANSPORT**: `_signNeeds` (the server reads the whole roster — a colleague's rule is admin-only) and `_signState` (for a reader without money) ride GETs and the PUT answer, stripped on save. `overseerOn` · `overseerBackupId` · `overseerWhen` join ADMIN_ONLY_USER_FIELDS.
- **THE DRAWER**: the person drawer's section 6 is Off | Always · approver · backup · when · a sentence reading it back; `stPersonSays` and the People row say it. The workspace switch LEFT the approval-rules panel: `ov_rule_on`, `ov_rule_sub`, `ov_rule_saved`, `ov_person`, `ov_person_sub`, `ov_says_on/_off/_not_enforced`, `ov_no_owner`, `st_sum_overseen` are STALE, inert in both books. `overseerFor` and `saveOverseerCfg` have NO CALLER; `overseerCfg().on` is read only as the legacy default.
Tests: f375 (the reading, the server journey with a backdated sqlite row for the sweep), approval-before-signing-verify (34 checks, three people, a phone); f199, f242, f148 re-pointed in place.

## THE POP-UP DIET — twenty-six pop-ups lose the words that repeated their controls (owner-approved 13 Sep 2026)

*"the pop ups have too many words which are unnecessary and makes the card overwhelming"*, then *"strip the unnecessary wording"*, then, over real before/after renders, *"Build all 26 as shown"*. Measured on the real product: 2,626 words on the 26 faces → about 1,400.
- **THE RULE**: anything a control already says GOES; how the machinery works GOES (to the hover — `title=`); ONE line that tells a first-timer what happens next STAYS; a COST stays next to the button (`ct_plain_text_warn`, the signing-locked bullets); what the OTHER SIDE sees stays in one line (`co_purpose_*_line`, `dk_cp_knows`); a hint ARRIVES WHEN NEEDED (`WORD_REFUSAL` at the drop, `co_note_goes_nowhere` on the link channel only).
- **THE SEND IS THE OWNER'S DRAWING**: title → what travels (`#share-manifest-line`, the fold, `#share-other`) → `purposeBlock` → `#share-tabs` → name/email → ONE note box (`shareNoteBoxHtml`, id `sh-summary`, drawn in step 2 on one screen) → `readinessPanelHtml(c,{fold:true})` (notes fold to `co_worth_checking_n`; a BLOCK never folds — it carries the tick) → `co_email_off_line` → link settings → Send. `SHARE_PURPOSE_COPY[k].line` is the one-screen sentence; `#share-purpose-say[data-compact]` tells `paintPurpose` which shape. `#sh-ch-note` and `#sh-msg-where` print only where the channel costs something. The address SOURCE rides as `data-prefill-src` on `#sh-email`; the sentence is gone. `#sh-msg`, `#sh-prefill-note`, `co_personal_message`, `co_recipient_*`, `co_ch_email_note`, `co_note_goes_email/_whatsapp/_with_file`, `co_one_link_current`, `co_not_emailed`, `co_press_create_link`, `co_what_link_for` (compact) are STALE.
- **RETIRE A SENTENCE BY NOT CALLING IT**: the keys stay in BOTH books (inert); f310 (2) is the sweep that fails on a CALL coming back. Stale on the face: `ct_thats_all`, `rv_who_hint` (at rest), `rv_entry_sub`, `dk_add_hint`, `dk_ho_you_stay`, `dr_example`, `ng_memo_send_sub`, `pb_adds_preferred`, `lib_bring_standard_paper`, `lib_so_you_can_send`, `tf_our_party_hint`, `ik_ask_sub`, `ap_tip_autofill`, `ap_signers_execute`, `ct_change_and_save`/`ct_headings_bold`/`ct_tick_when_typing`/`ct_changed_become_pending`, the `→ hint` arrows after labels (three forms). Shortened in place, both books: `rv_modal_sub`, `fa_*`, `dk_*`, `dr_lead`, `ob_tick_to_add`, `me_fields_highlighted`, `set_grant_streams`, `ap_route_locked_why_*`, `ap_restart_*`, `co_ch_word_note`, `co_upload_word_tail`, `ct_upload_hint`, `RICH_EDITOR_NOTE`.
- The clause picker's wording opens on a press (`pb_read_wording`); the edit-document window keeps ONE warning line and no character count.
Tests: f310 (60 claims), f182 / f307 / f42 / n4 / share-recipient-verify re-pointed (the source is a fact on the box; the email channel line is empty).

## THE NUANCED REDLINE — THE SMALLEST CHANGE, AND THE FILL MEANS ARRIVING (Young's go on the artifact of that name, 15 Sep 2026)

Four decisions, all built. *"The redline has to be nuanced and only tackle where the misalignment is."* Reported off clause 7 of a distribution agreement: seven rules under one heading, the playbook disagreed with one of them, and the redline struck all seven and pasted our stand-alone payment clause over them — deleting the interest charge, the dispute procedure, the credit limit and the set-off right, and calling the parties names this contract does not define.
- **ON A CLAUSE WE ALREADY HAVE, THE LIBRARY'S WORDING DOES NOT LEAD.** `pbFitWording` (js/playbook.js) is the ONE reading of the smallest change: the figure written into THEIR sentence (`ladderWriteFigure` on the block `pbQuoteBlock` located, every other block byte-identical through `pbSwapBlock`), else the model's fitted draft, else NOTHING — an honest refusal beats a paste. `lead`/`leadKind` follow it on an EDIT landing; `preferred` keeps its own name and its own slot (the 26 Aug ruling stands) and is still the answer for an ADD.
- **THE WALL**: `pbUnquotedLoss(bodyHtml, quote, newText)` counts the blocks the finding never named that would not survive the proposed wording; `rlFilePlaybookProposal` REFUSES on a non-zero count unless `opts.wholesale`. The batch never passes it (`n.broad` counts what it left); the review window's two library presses ASK first (`askThenFile`, `ng_pb_broad_ask`) and the answer is what lifts it. **DECISION 4**: `redlineWholesale` / `redlineReplacementHtml` untouched — a rewrite somebody CHOSE keeps its one-struck-block picture.
- **THE FILL SAYS ARRIVING.** `del.rl-us` / `del.rl-them` STATE `background:transparent` — never merely omit it, because `.nego-del` carries `--n-del-bg` at (0,1,0) and would paint it straight back; `ins` keeps the fill. Colour still says who, the line still says what. Both sheets, identical.
- **THE FOURTH WORDING IS NAMED**: `pb_w_figure` in `RL_PB_WORDING_KEY`, `ce_use_fit` on the clause editor's scan rail (which applies `fit.html`, never rebuilt lines), so the two surfaces cannot drift about what the smallest change is.
Tests: f314 (47 claims, 38 red at the parent), redline-verify 28 (painted pixels), f245 (13) and f207-C re-pointed in place.

## BEFORE YOU SIGN IS THE WHOLE LIST, IN THREE STAGES (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026)

Four reports off the last screen before a signature, one fault under all of them: the card is called "Before you sign" and a reader takes that to mean EVERYTHING between them and a signature. It was a list of what was blocking at that MOMENT.
- **A BLOCKER NAMES THE CLAUSE.** `negoBlockerClauses(list)` (js/negotiation.js) through `negoRefusalClause` — one clause said ONCE however many changes sit on it, capped at `NG_BLOCK_CLAUSES` (3) with `ng_block_clauses_more` counting the rest, `ng_this_clause` where it cannot be named. The CHG id was the last place a reader was given a handle the screen stopped drawing on 14 Sep.
- **READING A CONTRACT DOES NOT REQUIRE KNOWING WHO SIGNS IT.** `signCheckReady` DROPPED `signingRouteOpen` — that condition hid the three readings and the Run control until a signer was named on each side, so the list understated its own length every time. Signers are a ROW, never a condition on the list. **`signCheckWaiting(c)`** replaces the other half: while the negotiation is open the readings are DRAWN and say they are waiting (`row.waiting`, no hold — the row it waits on already holds; verbs stand down; `runSignCheck` refuses with `sc_wait_nego`).
- **THE BRIEF JOINS THE CHECK.** `signCheckBrief(c)` → none · unknown · stale; **staleness is asked of the RECORD** (`signCheckBriefAt`, the newest filed change across `c.changes` and `c.negotiation.rounds`, read RAW) because the brief's own `inputHash` is the SERVER's over what it sent and a second hasher would drift. It can over-report on a refused change — the safe direction, and named. `_hasBrief` without `_brief` answers UNKNOWN and draws nothing. Dropped from the light list with the other two hash rows.
- **THREE STAGES.** `SIGN_STAGES` = paper · read · people, `SIGN_STAGE_OF` / `signStageOf` the one map (an unknown kind lands in `paper`). An empty stage draws nothing; the order inside a stage is `signReadiness`'s own. **The Run control moved onto the readings' stage and KEPT `#sc-run`.** The stage heading is a label — no border, fill or radius.
- **A ROW WHOSE WORK IS ON THIS SCREEN POINTS AT IT.** The signers row keeps its HOLD (removing it would let a record be sealed with nobody named) and loses the sentence the Signing order card prints word for word 300px below; `signLandOn('#signing-order')` is the press, `openSignerPlanEditor` the fallback where that card is not drawn.
- **THE PLAIN ENGLISH CAPTION SAYS WHEN IT WAS READ.** Pressing the switch ON already re-asked where the wording moved; a switch left ON showed the pre-round edition with changed clauses BLANK and said nothing. `.doc-read-moved` on the caption already drawn, counting what the edition can no longer speak for, reusing `data-doc-read-again`. It never re-reads by itself.
- **A PRESS IN THE WORDING DOES NOTHING** (reverses 14 Sep on the negotiate page; the clause editor's own click-to-type of 13 Sep is untouched). The paper is for reading: a stray click or a drag released a pixel short took the reader into a full-window layer. The pencil and the redline row's Edit are the two doors.
Tests: f319 (31 claims, 29 red at the parent), signing-flow-verify 6 (ten checks on the REAL APP, 8 red — `6a` reads `[]` and `6g` reads `modal:true`), f245 and clause-door-verify 17a reversed in place, f308/f311/signing-flow benches given a brief.

## A SUGGESTION HAS AN ADDRESS — AND THE WALL BECOMES A SEATBELT (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026)

*"why would a suggestion try and delete clauses nobody complained about or not impact by our standards?"* **IT NEVER MEANT TO.** A suggested wording is text with NO ADDRESS ON IT and the act that puts wording into a clause knows one move — replace everything — so on a CONTAINER clause (a numbered heading with six rules under it) a suggestion about one of them takes the other five as COLLATERAL. Built that way because both sides of the swap are usually one paragraph; it breaks the moment the other side writes six rules under one heading.
- **`pbFitInto(bodyHtml, quote, words)` (js/playbook.js, published) IS THE ONE READING** and the address is the finding's OWN QUOTE through `pbQuoteBlock` — the reading the FIGURE path has always used, offered to any wording rather than to a number alone. Null on no DOM, no blocks, no confident address, or wording identical to the block it lands in; every caller falls back to what it had.
- **THE DRAFT TAKES IT AT THE SHARED READING** (`pbFitWording`'s draft branch), so the batch and the editor cannot disagree about where a draft goes. `preview` stays the words themselves; `text`/`html` become the whole body with one block swapped.
- **THE EDITOR ADDRESSES EVERY FRAGMENT VERB** (`preferred` · `fallback` · `draft`) and EXEMPTS `fit`, which already IS a fitted body — slotting one into a block would nest the clause inside itself.
- **THE SEPTEMBER WALL IS NOW A SEATBELT, AND IT IS SECOND**: with an address `pbUnquotedLoss` counts zero and there is nothing to ask, so the question fires only on `!fitHtml` — the one case left where a suggestion still replaces a whole clause. It uses the review window's OWN keys (`ng_pb_broad_title/_ask/_go`): two doors, one act, one sentence. A refused question applies nothing and records nothing as taken (`applyScan().then(done => …)`).
- MEASURED on the owner's own clause: the draft alone would delete FIVE parts; addressed it deletes NONE.
Tests: f318 (12 claims, 12 red at the parent), prepare-redlines-verify 7 (nine checks on the REAL APP — 3 red at the parent, reproducing `struck: 20.1, 20.2, 20.4, 20.5, 20.6`). **NOT in clause-editor-verify**: parity.html deliberately does not load js/playbook.js and is shared by ten browser files, so the press there would measure the fallback, not the fix.

## THE CONTRACT KEEPS ITS SHAPE, THE COMMENT NUMBER COMES INSIDE THE PAGE, THE WAY BACK IS A SIGN (Young ruled 15 Sep 2026)

- **A CLAUSE CARRYING A MARK IS STILL THE DRAFTER'S PARAGRAPH.** `redlineShapeMap(html)` (js/redline.js) reads a clause's stored markup once into line → `{tag, shape classes, innerHTML}`; `redlineOpsBlocksHtml` takes `opts.shape` and draws an untouched line (`every op keep`, not `attributed`) from that markup VERBATIM. `_RL_SHAPE_KEEP` is the FILE READERS' vocabulary only (`rl-hang`, `hati-lv-1..3`, `hati-tight`, `hati-pb`, `hati-toc`) — never a mark class; the tag is bounded to `p|h1|h2|h3|h4|li`; a line said twice keeps the FIRST. Ops are PLAIN TEXT, so every bold lead-in, italic phrase and step was thrown away the moment somebody proposed a change, and the shape was re-guessed from the characters.
- **`rlClauseShape(cl)` IS THE ONE READING** (js/views/negotiation.js, published; reads the same `rlHangRichHtml(negoRichBody(cl))` the clean reading draws). FOUR papers ask it and a fifth must: the negotiate page's ops branch, its stacked branch (through `rlLayeredHtml`'s `opts`), its front matter, the contract room's `negoDocHtml`, and the clause editor through `ceShapeMap()` (`ceRedlineHtml` + `ceMarkedHtml`). A card preview is NOT a paper and is deliberately not swept.
- **THE MARKER IS PLACED AGAINST THE PAPER'S OWN INSET.** `--rl-paper-pad` is declared beside EVERY `.rl-paper` padding (five rungs, 56/56/30/26/20); `.rl-note-mk` is `left:calc(2px - var(--rl-paper-pad,56px))`. The flat `-48px` was inside the sheet at one rung and 27px out in the grey at another. The ring is `--st-steel-bg` with `--accent-ink` in it (light ground, dark number); `.out` is the amber twin.
- **THE CRUMB'S WORD IS A SIGN.** `#ws-back` restyled, never replaced — same id, same `data-back`, same handler, both destinations. `.room-crumb-back` is `border-radius:50%` (a circle is not a corner — the 26 Aug ruling), `--btn-edge` for the outline (blue on navy, green on teal, from one token), sized `calc(var(--t-label) * var(--lh-tight))` so the row keeps its height and the paper does not move (MEASURED: first ink 293 room / 309 workbench, before and after). The word is the `title` and the `aria-label` — a sign a reader cannot name is a guess. `::before{inset:-7px}` is the press, which costs nothing on layout.
- Noticed, not fixed: `.nego-redline .rl-marker{font-weight:var(--w-strong)}` bolds an invented marker on the room's canvas — now only on lines that really moved.
Tests: f317 (24 claims, 19 red at the parent), clause-editor-verify 35 (35d–35f red at the parent; 35g–35i are named CONTROLS), round-two-comments-verify K (measured at two window widths, 4 of 6 red), pages-read-alike-verify 1 and f184/f313 re-pointed in place.

## THE DECISION THAT COULD NOT BE MADE, AND THE COLUMN THAT WAS BLANK (Young reported all three 15 Sep 2026)

- **AN INCOMING ASK IS MEASURED FROM WHAT NOW STANDS** (`negoClauseNowById`), never the round baseline. `applyNegoProposals` wrote the baseline onto every arriving ask, so one written on top of an ask we had ALREADY ACCEPTED read as its rival, and `negoResolve`'s accept guard refused it — for ever. Accept one ask of theirs on a clause and no later ask on that clause can be accepted again, which is the clause being argued hardest. `stacksOn` still leads: a PENDING ask of ours is not in the standing text and only the sender's own `oldText` can name it.
- **A REFUSAL NAMES THE CLAUSE, NEVER A CHG NUMBER.** `negoRefusalClause(other, ch)` through `clauseNameShown`, with `ng_this_clause` as the honest fallback; `ng_accept_blocked_adopted` and its mirror `ng_reopen_blocked_downstream` reworded in BOTH books and both pointing at the ladder. Since 14 Sep the clause leads every row and the id rides the hover, so the id named nothing the reader could see.
- **THE PLAIN ENGLISH COLUMN MIRRORS THE FRONT MATTER.** `docReadFront` / `docReadMirrorStyle` draw every painted block above the FIRST PAIRED clause as `.doc-read-mirror`: the contract's own words, `aria-hidden`, level with what they face. **ITS OWN CLASS** (four checks counting readings found the mirrors among them) and **ITS OWN FLOOR** (sharing one put the first reading 28px below its clause). Shape — align, weight, case, letter-spacing, size, leading — is MEASURED through a narrow door, never typed. The paper HEAD is mirrored though it is `DOC_READ_FURNITURE` to the route. Nothing is sent, cached or hashed: `docReadClauses` does not move by a byte.
- **AND THE WHEEL WORKS OVER EITHER COLUMN**: forwarded to `#doc-scroll` (deltaMode honoured; swallowed only where the paper actually moved). The edition is still a clip and a transform and must never grow a scroller of its own.
- **A CONTENTS ROW IS TWO COLUMNS AND THE MIRROR KEEPS BOTH** (Young reported it 15 Sep 2026): a page number is a right-hand COLUMN, and flattened into the line it reads as the last word of the heading. `docReadMirrorToc(el, text)` is the ONE reading — the tail is the FILE'S own (`.hati-toc-n`, the class read through `window.RICH_TOC_TAIL_CLASS` so the mirror and the sanitiser cannot drift) and it REFUSES rather than guessing where the collapsed line does not END with it; the mirror then draws the paper's own `<p class="hati-toc">…<span class="hati-toc-n">`. **THE CLOTHES FOLLOW THE BUILDER**: `.doc-read-mirror` is the FOURTH home that markup is drawn in and carries the same pair of declarations, because every other one is scoped to its own sheet and none can reach a layer that is a SIBLING of the paper. Nothing about the tail reaches `docReadClauses` or the hash.
Tests: f314 (6)(7), plain-english-verify 18 and 19 (a GEOMETRY — the parent's markup carries every word in order; only painted boxes show that a number is a column), competing-redlines-verify re-pointed.

## HaTi'S NEXT FIFTEEN — FIVE SCREENS (owner's order, 16 Sep 2026)

The artifact is "HaTi's Next Fifteen"; the owner asked for screens 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 and named screen 4 (Obligations) as out. **THE SEPARATE "Six Screens, One Grammar" ARTIFACT IS NOT THAT ORDER** — the first run applied its restyling to Home, Contracts, Insights and Requests and it was REVERTED to the byte the same day. Those four pages are promised "weight added: none" / "nothing else moves"; a second screen takes the section grammar only on the owner's word.

### "DO THIS TO THESE N" — one act above the Contracts table (S13/S14, `js/cohort.js`)

**THE CONDITION IS THE FEATURE**: `cohortButtonHtml` draws nothing unless `regNarrowed()` — with nothing narrowing, the same button offers to act on the whole book. `cohortSet` asks `regFiltered` and holds no copy of the rules; never on the Negotiations seat. The page header leaves a SLOT (`PAGE_ACTIONS.register = ['cohort','new']` → `#reg-cohort-slot`) and `regPaintCohort` fills it on every register paint — the room's own rule for a control that changes under a head built once.
- `COHORT_ACTS` is the four rows, keyed in stable English. Export is a PROXY onto `regExportCsv`; the amendment goes through `createAmendment` (which gained an additive `opts.says`, one paragraph, escaped); the document ask goes through `obligationRequireDoc` + `obligationChase`; the pack is deterministic and every value in it a literal.
- **"SENT" MUST MEAN SENT**: the row says DRAFT where the artifact said Send. `cohortRun` walks in waves of `COHORT_WAVE` 4 and asks the stop BETWEEN every contract; a refusal is counted and NAMED in the report.
- `obligationRequireDoc` (js/obligations.js) is the headless writer — the form stays the one door a person TYPES into, and both ask `obligationAlreadyOn`. `obligationChase` gained `{confirm:false, quiet:true}`: quiet moves the reporting to the return value, never removes it.
Tests: f324 (50 claims, 0 at the parent).

### A REQUIRED DOCUMENT (S8) — one reading, three surfaces

`doc:{file,until}` on an ordinary obligation, absent on every record on file. `obligationDocState` answers lapsed · missing · soon · held (`OB_DOC_SOON_DAYS` 30); `contractDocuments` sorts worst first. Read by the Overview's *Documents they must hold* section (`paintOverviewDocs` is the published door; `obligationSurfacesChanged` calls it), the Contracts filter (`docs`, sixth on `REG_BAR_FILTERS`, behind Adapt filters, drawing the moment it narrows) and the `cert-lapsed` alert (lapsed only; one row per contract).

### THE EXPOSURE REGISTER — Insights' sixth tab (S10, js/views/intelligence.js)

`EXPOSURE_KINDS` is five readings over `c.metadata` and nothing else; `exposureData` counts, `exposureHtml` draws and computes nothing. **NO SCORE, and the refusal is the feature** — a rating a lawyer cannot derive is worse than a count they can press. A contract whose field is absent or `unclear` is in NO exposure row; it is in the LAST row, which is `copilotRead`'s own "not read closely enough". Every row is a door onto `regShowOnly`; the worst one is the largest by value and money obeys `canViewValues` (absent, never dashes). Two new metadata fields, `indemnityCapped` and `terminateForConvenience`, written parallel to `liabilityCapped`. Tests: f321 (31 claims, 3 at the parent).

**AND IT SAYS WHERE TO LOOK (Young ruled it 19 Sep 2026: *"very bland and does not highlight where your eyes should focus on"*)** — AN ORDERING AND WEIGHT PASS, never an arithmetic one (f338 (7) is the wall). `exposureData` RANKS the rows by what they are worth, by how many where money is hidden — the same pair `worst` picks a contract by — and names `lead` (the first non-empty row; null on a clean book, because a bar over an absence is an alarm about nothing). **A ZERO ROW STANDS DOWN AND IS NEVER HIDDEN**: the label shade, no verb, still on the page, because "no contract has this" is a fact worth reading. The leading row takes a 3px **RUBY** bar RESERVED transparent on every other row (the arrival strip's lesson) and the page's one figure at `--t-section`. **ONE TONE, AND THE DEPARTURE IS SAID**: the order asked for ruby above a threshold and amber below, and this product holds no such threshold — inventing one is the score coming back through a side door. **THE UNREAD ROW LEFT THE TABLE** for a coverage line beneath it (it is about HaTi's coverage, not about the contracts) and kept `data-exp-go="unread"`, so the wire needs no branch. `fxLeft` counts CONTRACTS as a SET (one record in two rows is left out once) and `int_exp_fx_line_one/_other` says it. Tests: f338 (4)–(7), four-off-the-screenshots-verify 4.

### THE REQUESTS QUEUE GROWS A CLOCK, A LANE AND A TRACKER (S4 · S3 · S5)

Four nullable columns on `intake_requests` (`assignee_*`, `promised_at`, `lane`, `track_token`), all absent on file; the row keys stay absent rather than null.
- **ROAD is worked out, never typed** (`intakeRoad` → `{k, why}`): `IK_THEIR_PAPER` (up to TWO words between "their" and the noun — three would match "their side of the agreement") and `ikKnownCounterparty` over `state.contracts`. Their paper AND a counterparty nobody has dealt with is the close read; either alone is ordinary work. A lane that fired outranks every judgement.
- **PROMISED is a promise a person types** — nothing here computes a date. `intakePromise` has four answers; `intakeStoppedAt` freezes the clock when the ball is theirs; `intakeMedianDays` refuses below `IK_MEDIAN_MIN` 2.
- **A LANE IS FOUR CONDITIONS AND A DESTINATION**, in Settings (`state.settings.intakeLanes`), READ by `intakeLaneFor` and written nowhere else. It NEVER clears a request about the other side's own paper, whatever else the rule says. `intakeRunLanes` presses `createFromTemplate` and writes `lane` on the record; it signs nothing. It fires when a person who may draft loads the page, not on a timer — the server has no template catalogue and building one would be a second place contracts are minted from.
- **THE TRACKER** is `GET /track/:token` — public, read-only, no form and no route that writes; it carries the request the reader wrote and nothing else; a bad token is the same 404 as a missing one.
Tests: f322 (36 claims, 4 at the parent).

### THE MAILROOM, AND THE NOTICE DESK (S1 · S6 · S7 · S8 on Home)

- **THE MAILROOM adds no surface**: `POST /api/mailroom` writes the IMPORT QUEUE's own shape (`migration.needsReview`, `blocked:'unread'`) so the migration page learns nothing new and the Portfolio tile stops reading zero. One shared secret, `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, rotated in Settings; off is 404 and a wrong key 401; `MAILROOM_TYPES` / `MAILROOM_MAX_BYTES` / `MAILROOM_MAX_FILES` each SAID in `skipped`. **IT FILES, IT DOES NOT READ** — a second extractor on the server would be two readings of one document.
- **THE NOTICE DESK** (`js/notice.js`): `noticeDraft(c)` composes a non-renewal or termination letter from the record — not one word from a model, no route, no spend. `noticeBlockers` refuses rather than guesses (amendment · archived · not in force · **not yet**, which is `renewalWindow`'s own `inWindow` · no counterparty · no notice period · no expiry) and every refusal is a REASON the renewal card prints. **HaTi DRAFTS; A PERSON SERVES** — the trail says a letter was drafted and copied and no more. Two doors: the renewal card's *Serve a notice* and the desk row. A contract never carries both a notice row and a renewal row.
- `owed` on `KPI_ALL_ORDER`, not in the default four; every figure borrowed, converted through `fxHome`, what has no rate LEFT OUT and counted, and the COUNT where the reader may not see money.
Tests: f323 (30 claims, 0 at the parent), f274 re-pointed.

### THE RENEWAL CARD IS ONE ROW, AND THE MACHINERY IS ON THE HOVER (Young ruled 17 Sep 2026)

*"the buttons should be on the same line but also, the comments should be at a minimum"* — the POP-UP DIET applied to a CARD. Measured: ~95 words and two button rows became **32 words and one row** (five buttons, `buttonRows:1`, no divider).
- **ONE ROW**: `decideRow` is now just the three answers plus a `<span>` label, placed BY the acts row (`${settled?'':decideRow}`); the divider and the block heading are gone. **The label survives inline on purpose** — dropped, "Renew" beside "Start the renewal" reads as two ways to do one thing, which is the one ambiguity this card cannot afford.
- **CUT IS NOT DELETED**: `srcWhy` (the notice-period arithmetic), `rn_not_asked` (what Copilot would weigh up — now the hover on the button that does it) and `rn_to_none_why` are `title` attributes. `rn_fix_terms` / `rn_no_notice_fix` are STALE ON THE FACE, inert in both books; the way forward is a clause inside the line above instead.
- **AN ABSENCE IS STILL STATED ON THE FACE**: "No notice period recorded — this counts to expiry, {date}. Set it on Key terms." may never move to a hover; without it the expiry is silently shown as the deadline, which is the fault that line was built for.
- **WHO GETS CHASED SITS UNDER THE ROW**, not in it — in the row it competed for width with six controls and pushed them onto a second line.
Tests: f310 (2) (three more retired calls) and f310 (5) (four claims); renewal-decision-verify unchanged at 23/24 — `6b` is red before and after this change.

## THE FIVE RENDERS THE OWNER PICKED (Young ruled 17 Sep 2026: "Image 1 = B, Image 2 = D, Image 3 = A, Image 4 = as recommended, Image 5 = A")

Five boards were drawn before any code (artifact "HaTi — renders to choose from"); the owner answered with letters. **THE RULE UNDER THREE OF THEM IS ONE RULE**: a count, a question or a card that says something the press then contradicts is worse than saying nothing — so each rests on the reading the ACT itself obeys, never a second tally.
- **1 (B) "RUN THE CHECK" IS A BUTTON THAT SAYS WHAT IT COSTS.** `signCheckWillRun(r)` (js/signcheck.js) is the ONE reading of which of the three readings are out of date — `runSignCheck` spends it and `signCheckCardHtml` prints it, so the label cannot promise two and make three. Unknown asks on all three (`!== false`); not ready answers ZERO and the button keeps its plain word. `.sc-stage-act` is REWRITTEN as the secondary button (accent edge, transparent face, `--ctl-h`, 13px/400, `#i-search`) rather than given `.ui-btn` beside it — both are (0,1,0) and the face would be decided by source order. **NEVER FILLED**: one filled button per screen and here that is Sign; the owner was offered it by name and did not take it. `#sc-run` keeps its id. Keys `sc_run_n_one/_other`.
- **2 (D) THE REDLINE ROW IS THREE LINES.** `.rl-card-d` is ONE column — **and the rule that governs is the LATER one** (the 14 Sep artifact row at `grid-template-columns:minmax(96px,1fr) minmax(0,max-content)`), not the base grid 300 lines above it; declaring it up there alone measured two columns in a browser while the source read one. `.rl-card-side` takes `grid-row:3`, `grid-column:1/-1`, `justify-content:flex-end`, and still wraps. Nothing is reserved at the right, so **the verbs keep their WORDS** — that half stands, and it is what redline-verify 29g still asserts. **The second half of this sentence is REVERSED**: it read *"symbols were only ever wanted because the verbs ate a third of every row"*, and later the same day, off a fresh set of renders, Young chose a mark BESIDE each word (see THE MARK BESIDE THE WORD). The reasoning above is why that was safe: with the verbs on their own full-width line there is room for both, and the marks measured no taller and still one line. The cost is height, about half a row per change, said out loud. `--rl-verb-floor` STAYS DECLARED (their seat reads it). **Discard leaves the middle for the END of the row** (after Ladder, reversing "Ladder is last" of 14 Sep) and takes `--st-ruby-fg` (reversing the grey): it is the only press on that row that throws work away. The counterparty's seat and the contract tab are untouched.
- **3 (A) OPENING NEGOTIATE OFFERS THE FIELDS FIRST.** In `openRedlineWorkbench` — the ONE funnel, AFTER the sealed-record wall — a contract with open blanks raises `negoBlanksAsk`: the count, the first `NG_BLANKS_NAMED` (3) labels, then "and N more". Two doors on one screen (`ng_blanks_fill` filled / `ng_blanks_go`), Escape carries on. **The answer RE-ENTERS the funnel** (`opts.blanksAsked`) so the dialog cannot become a second path in. `_rlBlanksAsked` is a Set, in memory, **never persisted** — a question answered again on the next press stops being read. `negoBlanksOpen` borrows `contractBlanksOpen`, refuses PORTAL_MODE, and **asks `docFillable` FIRST** — `contractBlanksOpen` reads the WORDING, which still carries blanks past Draft, while the fill panel draws nothing at all once `docFillable` is false; without that wall the question fired on a contract whose panel does not exist and *Fill them in* landed on a page with no boxes. Found by competing-redlines-verify, whose contracts are mid-negotiation; every reading of the source looked correct. "Fill them in" → the Document tab + `contractFieldFocus`, after the paint.
- **4 BOTH DIRECTIONS OF THE FIELD LINK** (the owner left it to me; it is one machinery). `contractFieldKeyOf(el, c)` answers the FOUR shapes the product draws (`data-blankf`, `data-tplf` — an INDEX, so the contract turns it back into a key — `data-field`/`data-sync`, `data-field-key`); `contractFieldPeer(key, where)`; `contractFieldLight` clears every other light first; `wireFieldLink` binds ONE delegated `focusin` per element, armed from `paintContractForm` (the one function that knows a panel was drawn). A cursor in the panel **scrolls the paper to that word** and lights it; a cursor on the paper lights the box and **never takes the caret back**. **THE SCROLL DEFAULTS ON**: it first read `opts && opts.scroll !== false` and every caller passes no opts, so it never ran once — the guard-that-is-always-false class, and the check that should have caught it ("the contract does not move by a pixel") was passing BECAUSE of it, having measured the first paragraph's VIEWPORT top and conflated re-flow with scrolling. Layout is measured INSIDE the document now and the scroll has its own claim. It writes nothing. **The light is an outline and a wash, and it costs the contract NO layout** — and it is written at (0,1,1) because the paper's blanks carry `.field`, which states `outline:none` at one class weight, later in index.html: the source read `2px solid` and the computed style read `none`. Fixed by SCOPE.
  **AND AN ANSWERED FIELD IS STILL A FIELD (Young reported it 20 Sep 2026: *"whenever I click on any entry field, whether filled in or not, it should take me to the section in the contract ... That is not currently happening"*).** MEASURED on a company-standard contract: the paper carried a key for every EMPTY blank and NOTHING for an answered one, because `tplFormSlot` printed a filled field as plain escaped text — so the link pointed at half the form's boxes and was **silent** on the rest, a key nothing matches being an error nowhere in this machinery. `RICH_FIELD_DONE_CLASS` = `hati-field-done`, admitted to `richSpanClassOk` and to the `data-field-key` guard beside `hati-field`. **IT IS ITS OWN CLASS, NOT A SECOND ONE ON `hati-field`**: that test admits EXACTLY ONE class by design, and `hati-field` is DRESSED as a gap (grey box, pointer, hover, click-to-type) which an answered term must not take — three screens' behaviour changed to fix a fourth. **IT CARRIES NO RULE**, so the contract is painted exactly as it was; the print sweep still turns only `.hati-field` into a ruled line, so an answered term prints its value. `js/templateform.js` is required by the SERVER too, so both hosts write the same markup from one function. **Noticed, not fixed**: `readOnlyDocHtml` drops `data-field` when it freezes an input, so a built-in template's paper loses its keys past Draft — moot today, because `renderBlankFormSection` draws no boxes there either (measured: panel drawn, 0 boxes).
- **5 (A) A LADDER RUNG SHOWS ITS WHOLE CLAUSE** (**and see THE LADDER JOURNEY below — the press became a door onto the clause on 18 Sep, and the card was found to be clipped away entirely**)**.** `rlRungPeekHtml(c, clauseId, rungId)` draws the rung's full wording on a sheet at `right:100%` inside `#rl-cp` — over the grey, never over the paper, the contract measured unmoved. **IT COSTS NOTHING**: every rung already carries `text` and `oldText`, and the wording comes from `rlChangeWordingHtml`, the ONE builder, so the card and the row cannot disagree. R0 is drawn plain (nothing has moved yet); a rung with no wording SAYS so. **HOVER ALONE IS NOT ENOUGH** — a point, a tab, a press and Escape all answer; **pointing opens it loosely, pressing PINS it** (so its two verbs can be reached with a mouse) and the same press lets it go. A press on a button inside the row is still that button. `rlPeekShow` finds its host off the ROW (`#rl-cp` or `.ce-rail`), so the clause editor's Ladder tab inherits it. **IT OPENS BESIDE THE RUNG**, clamped inside the panel: `top` was a fixed inset and MEASURED rungs at y=217, 343, 513 and 640 all opened a card at y=84, up to 550px above the pointer — drawing correctly every time where nobody was looking, which is the owner's *"I do not see the updated clause"*. A hover card that appears somewhere else is a hover card nobody finds.
Tests: f329 (43 claims, 41 red at the parent — the two that pass are named walls; **plus four for the answered field, three red**), ladder-verify 18c/18c2 + 23 (14 red), redline-verify 29 (a GEOMETRY — the markup barely changed), blanks-panel-verify 9 **and section 10 (7 checks, 4 red — the parent prints `company:MISSING · provider:found` and `scrolled to 0 · lit false`)**, negotiations-door-verify 10, signing-flow-verify 7. **f106's two claims that a filled value "carries nothing" are REVERSED IN PLACE**, with the old reasoning kept because it was half right: a filled term stops being a BLANK and keeps its KEY. Re-pointed in place: f308 (4), f319 (5), f95's phone-overlay slice (**it took the LAST `@media (max-width:1023px)`, which was its own only because nothing had been written below it**), f148's SAME_IN_BOTH.

## THE LADDER JOURNEY — A RUNG IS A DOOR, AND READING BACK IS RED (Young ruled 18 Sep 2026)

*"When I click on ladder … when I click on the card it should take me to that clause in the contract. When I then click on read as it stood, it should show me what the previous read was. The Read as it stood, when it becomes back to now, it should then turn red until I click back."*
- **`rlLadderGoClause(clauseId, row)` IS THE ONE READING** of "put this clause in front of me", pressing the page's own `rlJumpToClause` (a change card's Edit has always used it). THREE DOORS ask it — the rung row's press, *Read as it stood*, *Back to now* — plus Enter and Space on a focused row. It REFUSES inside the clause editor's rail (that page is one clause), asked of the ROW exactly as `rlPeekShow` asks which surface it is on. Every attribute is read off the node BEFORE `rlRepaintFrom` takes it out of the page.
- **POINTING READS, PRESSING GOES.** This REVERSES 17 Sep's "PRESSING PINS IT": the press did nothing a reader could see, because the card was already under their pointer. Pinning is GONE whole (`_rlPeek` is `{clauseId, rungId}`, `rlPeekHide` takes nothing) — the card's two verbs stay reachable because it holds while the pointer is ON IT, which is the grace crossing that was already there. `cursor:zoom-in` → `pointer`; the row's title says the press goes to the clause (`ng_rung_go_title`).
- **THE CARD IS DORMANT SINCE 18 SEP** (Young: *"Remove the hovering feature in the ladder card for now"*) — see THE STREAM IS ASKED WHERE IT IS CREATED below. Everything in this bullet still describes the code, which is kept whole.
- **AND THE CARD WAS NEVER PAINTED.** `#rl-cp` is `overflow:hidden` and the card hangs OUTSIDE it by design, so the panel clipped the whole thing away: its rect read 526..946 and `elementFromPoint` answered the paper's own paragraph at every point across it. **A RECT IS NOT A PAINTED PIXEL** — every check that passed on it was reading one (`ladder-verify 23e2` is the instrument now). Where the host clips, `rlPeekShow` mounts into the host's positioning parent and places the card against the host's MEASURED edge; the clause editor's rail does not clip and keeps the plain mount.
- **READING BACK IS RED**, in ONE rule reversed in place — the chip on the clause (`.rl-rung-reading`), both *Back to now* buttons, the ladder row's `is-reading` outline and the clause's own `.rl-read-at` outline. The old ruling ("violet, because a reading of the past is not one of the two SIDES") is kept beside it as the half that was wrong: the accent is also every live door on this page, so the one chip saying "these are not today's words" looked safe to press. It reads beside a refused rung's ruby without confusion — both mean NOT THE LIVE WORDING. `outline:none` was re-scoped from the `:hover`/`:focus-visible` pair to `:focus-visible` alone: at (0,3,0) it was deleting the reading outline exactly when the pointer was on the row. By SCOPE, never `!important`.
Tests: f329 (16 claims, 16 red at the parent), ladder-verify 23e2 and section 24 (11 checks, 9 red); 23h/23i reversed in place.

## THE STREAM IS ASKED WHERE IT IS CREATED, AND THE HOVER CARD IS PUT AWAY (Young ruled 18 Sep 2026)

- **THE LADDER'S HOVER CARD IS DORMANT.** *"Remove the hovering feature in the ladder card for now."* The mouseover, mouseout, focusin and Escape doors are DELETED, not guarded — a listener on every mouse movement answering nothing is a cost with no reader — and `rlPeekShow` returns false before it looks anything up. **"For now" is not "delete it"**: `rlRungPeekHtml`, the `.rl-peek` rules and `ng_peek_esc` / `ng_peek_none` are kept whole, and `document._rlRungKeysWired` in negotiation.js is where the listeners go back. **THE PRESS SURVIVES** — the row is still a door onto its clause (`rlLadderGoClause`), Enter and Space beside it, `cursor:pointer` and the title unchanged.
- **THE TEMPLATES PAGE SAYS `+ Build new template`** (`lib_new_template`, one key, both books). **SUPERSEDED 24 Sep 2026: it says `+ New standard contract`** — see ONE DOOR TO A STANDARD CONTRACT.
- **WHERE A CONTRACT IS FILED IS ASKED WHERE IT IS CREATED.** MEASURED: one door of six asked — the UPLOAD — and every other took the stream SILENTLY off the template, which serves more than one part of a business. `folder` joins **`TEMPLATE_BASE_FIELDS`** (the wizard, bulk) and **`CONTRACT_ESSENTIALS`** (the company-standard and saved-template doors), LAST on both lists and never required, because filing is not a term of the agreement. `type:'stream'` draws `folderOptionsHtml` and binds `bindFolderSelect` — the product's own pair, so "+ New value stream" works here too. **THE TEMPLATE'S OWN FILING IS THE ANSWER ALREADY IN THE BOX**, written BY DESCRIPTOR onto a clone in both places: the shared lists are returned by reference and a default written onto one would freeze one template's filing onto every other (the getter trap, in its filing costume). The saved-template fill form gained its own box beside party and email, for the reason those two are hand-written. **THE WALLS ARE UNCHANGED**: `applyTemplateValues` still refuses a folder off the map, `POST /api/templates/:id/contracts` still scope-checks it, and re-filing a saved contract is still an admin's act.
- **"CONVERT A DOCUMENT" DRAWS THE SAME ROW** as "New standard template" (`tplLibCatStreamRowHtml` + `tplLibWireCatStream`, the third caller), and `POST /api/templates/upload` files it with `tplFolderOf` and `TPL_CATEGORIES` — the readings the other route already used. **Neither dialog has a door since 24 Sep 2026** (`tplLibUploadModal`, `tplLibCreateModal`): a document is COPIED through the one door's template start, and filing is the builder head's Stream chip.
- **ASKED, NOT ASSUMED**: whether the Overview's stream row should open to non-admins after creation would reverse the 14 Aug ruling and widen a server guard, so it waits on the owner.
- `createFromTemplate` (js/app.js) mints with no dialog and is reached only from the Requests lanes, so it has no reader to ask.
Tests: f330 (19 claims, 15 red at the parent), templates-tabs-verify 9, term-and-fields-verify 4 (the wizard driven: a stream is picked and the contract read back), ladder-verify 23 reversed in place; f326 (3) and n8 (3) re-pointed.

## CATEGORY AND VALUE STREAM ARE THE COMPANY'S LISTS (Young ruled 17 Sep 2026)

*"it is not clear how you create category and value stream but also how you delete them. There should be a fairly simply process of creating the folders."* MEASURED: a value stream was written to **localStorage** and no colleague ever learned of it (the panel admitted it in a note), and a **category could not be created at all** — `TPLLIB_CATEGORIES` was five words with no writer anywhere in js/ or server/.
- **ONE SHARED STORE, ONE ROUTE**: `state.settings.valueStreams` + `state.settings.templateCategories`, written by `saveValueStreams` / `addTemplateCategory` through **`saveFilingSettings` → `PUT /api/settings/filing`** (`auth, templateManager` — the templates route's twin, and for its reason: PUT /api/settings is admin-only and a template manager files the paper). An ALLOW-LIST, bounded at `FILING_MAX` 60 and 64 characters a value; **it never touches `folderAccess`** — a name is not a permission, and re-filing a contract is still an admin's act.
- **`foldersFromSettings()` merges the shared list into `FOLDERS`** — the ONE map every dropdown, filter chip, card stripe, map cluster and report reads — called from `loadBootstrap` AFTER `state.settings` lands (js/templates.js builds FOLDERS at module load, long before anyone has signed in). A shared stream OUTRANKS a local one of the same id.
- **THE LOCALSTORAGE READER STAYS AND IS NOT A MIGRATION**: older streams merge as before carrying `local:true`; the ROW says `st_p_folders_thisbrowser` and offers `shareCustomFolder` — one press, never a silent lift. `st_p_folders_local` is STALE ON THE FACE (a false sentence in a drawer is worse than a loud one), inert in both books.
- **EVERY PICKER USES THE PRODUCT'S OWN DOOR**: `tplStreamOpts` gained the `__new__` sentinel `folderOptionsHtml` has always carried, answered by `bindFolderSelect`; `bindCategorySelect` is its twin. `tplLibWireCatStream` wires BOTH dialogs so neither grows a dead option; `tplLibPick` refuses a sentinel reaching the route. **`promptNewName` is ONE name box, two lists** (`promptNewFolder` is its first caller); `dataset` is asked for BEFORE it is written, so a stage without real elements still opens the dialog.
- **`templateCategories()` is the ONE list and `tplCategoryName` the ONE presenting reading** — no screen reads `TPLLIB_CATEGORIES[t.category]` any more, or a card would print "Other" for a category the picker names. Built-ins cannot be renamed or removed (no store to be renamed into); a removal in use is REFUSED (`st_p_cats_holds`), the stream list's own rule.
Tests: f326 (28 claims, 26 red at the parent; the folderAccess wall is the named control), templates-tabs-verify 8 (a real pick, a real name, a real press, read back OFF THE SERVER), settings-tabs-verify (both lists on one panel, add and remove driven); f312 (4) re-pointed in place for the create door.

## THE CONTRACTS ACT IS DRAWN ONLY WHILE THE TABLE IS NARROWED — AND THAT IS THE FEATURE

Young, 17 Sep 2026: *"I do not see the feature implemented in HaTi."* It IS implemented. MEASURED in a browser: at rest `regNarrowed()` is false and `#reg-cohort-slot` is deliberately EMPTY; set any filter and the slot fills with `Do this to these N ▾` (143×30) whose menu is the artifact's four rows. The artifact's own words: *"It is not drawn at all while the table shows everything, because then it would be an offer to act on the whole book."* If it looks missing, nothing is narrowing the table.

## WHO MAY MAKE NEW PAPER (Young ruled it 18 Sep 2026, over a render of the person drawer)

A FIFTH PER-PERSON GRANT beside the four that already work this way. "New paper" is paper with no approved wording behind it: writing a company-standard template. Drafting from an approved template, uploading received paper, negotiating and signing are the everyday acts and stay open — the owner's own note on the row promises it.
- **`mayMakeNewPaper(u)` (js/core.js, published) is the ONE reading**: a viewer never, an admin always (a workspace that could lock its owner out of its own templates is a worse failure), everybody else on an explicit `newPaper === true`. **OFF BY DEFAULT** — the one place it differs from its four neighbours, and it means the deploy DOES change something for Editors.
- Column `users.new_paper` DEFAULT 0; `newPaper` joins `ADMIN_ONLY_USER_FIELDS`; the RAW fact travels and one predicate answers for admins on each host.
- **THE SERVER IS THE WALL**: `paperMaker` NARROWS `templateManager` on the FIVE routes that mint or write a company standard (create · upload-as-template · new version · write a version · publish). NOT gated, and asserted so: rename, re-file, archive, delete, `POST /api/templates/:id/contracts`, and `/api/settings/templates` (a counterparty's own template is importing their wording, not writing ours).
- PATCH /api/users/:id is an ADMIN'S GRANT only; refuses a stored "no" on an admin and a stored "yes" on a viewer.
- **ONE REFUSAL, ONE WAY FORWARD**: `newPaperBlocked` / `newPaperBlockLine` / `newPaperBlock` (js/views/templatelib.js, published). Drawn doors GREY with the reason on the hover; the funnel toasts a 'warn' — the kind that carries an ACTION — onto Requests, the door that already exists.
Tests: f331 (1)–(6), paper-beside-questions-verify 4 and **6 (the wall DRIVEN as an Editor, not read: refused, self-service refused, granted, revoked, rename still open, a new version refused)**.

## A REAL CONTRACT, NOT AN OUTLINE (the build plan's upgrade 1, 18 Sep 2026)

All twelve built-in templates produced EXACTLY FOUR CLAUSES. They produce eleven to thirteen now.
- **AN ANCHOR IS AN IDENTITY, NOT A POSITION.** `clause(n,title,body,key)`, key defaulting to `'c'+n`; flags and `data-anchor` read the KEY. ~30 hand-written risk findings point at `c1`…`c4` and NOT ONE had to be re-pointed — the template's own four keep their addresses wherever they land. The flag sweep matches `^(c\d+|s-[a-z]+)$`.
- The twelve builders return DESCRIPTORS (`{key,title,body}`); the assembler numbers the run. Own lead three first, the nine next, the template's OWN LAST CLAUSE STAYS LAST (never Governing Law at 4 and Termination at 8).
- `DOC_SHARED_CLAUSES` is the nine; `SHARED_BODY` words them inside docBody (they need its market pack and its TERM reading). **Four are READ FROM THE CLAUSE LIBRARY** through `docLibWording(id, fallback)` — cl-term · cl-liab · cl-conf · cl-dp — each with its own literal fallback, because js/playbook.js is not on every stage.
- **NOTHING IS SAID TWICE**: `DOC_SHARED_SKIP` per template, and `docSharedSkip(tk)` folds in `DOC_TERM_IN_CLAUSE` rather than writing the term list twice. The recital's appended term sentence is GONE.
- **`cl-dp` IS A GETTER NOW** (js/playbook.js), like `cl-law` beside it: it was hardcoded Kenyan while nothing printed it, and upgrade 1 prints it. Measured: a Swedish workspace cites the GDPR and the IMY.
Tests: f331 (8)–(11), paper-beside-questions-verify 3; f131 and f245's cl-dp claims re-pointed to the RELATION.

## THE PAPER BESIDE THE QUESTIONS (the build plan's upgrade 2, 18 Sep 2026)

Both fill screens draw the agreement beside the boxes. **ONE builder, two doors** (js/templatefields.js, all published).
- `fillPreviewContract(kind,o)` calls the SAME two pure functions the create path calls (`applyTemplateValues`, `fillTemplateBody`) and stops before the side effects; `fillPreviewHtml` renders through `docBody`. The throwaway has no id, never reaches state and nothing persists it (f331 greps for `nextId(`, `persist(`, `contractArrived`…).

- **THE PREVIEW TAKES NO TYPING, AND IT SCROLLS** (the second half REVERSES the first build's `inert`, same day — see THE TWO SCROLLS below). docBody draws a draft's blanks as REAL inputs, so without something a reader types into a contract with no id. Found on a screenshot, not in an assertion.
- **AND THE THIRD DOOR DRAWS IT TOO** since 18 Sep 2026 — see EVERY CREATION DOOR DRAWS THE PAPER below.
- **THE COUNT IS LIVE OR IT IS NOT DRAWN** (`fillPreviewLeft`, `#tf-preview-left`): written once at the draw, the next keystroke contradicted it.
- `FILL_PREVIEW_MIN_W` 1000: under it both dialogs are exactly what they were.

MEASURED first: most of it was built. `copilotPropose` is shown the section's wording as `passage`, `tbMarkedHtml` draws it against what is there, the four refinement chips already need a written section. **WHAT WAS MISSING was one guard and one sentence.**
- `if (lib && !txt)` offered your own standard wording ONLY to an EMPTY section. Now `tbLibraryOffers(sec, lib)` — a reading about the library, not about emptiness — asked at the DRAW (the chip stands down) and at the PRESS (`tbUseLibrary` refuses in words), so the sign and the wall cannot disagree.
