## THE TWO SCROLLS — THE SETTINGS TABS STAY, AND THE PREVIEW MOVES (Young ruled both 18 Sep 2026)

*"for the team & settings page, make that when you scroll, you do not lose the tabs. The body scrolls up behind the tabs line."* Then, of the fill screen: *"The page on the right needs to scroll."*

- **THE TAB ROW IS STICKY INSIDE `#content-scroll`.** MEASURED at the parent, 1440×620 on Platform settings: the row rests at y=84 and 600px down it is at y=-516, off the scroller entirely — seventeen panels is more than one screen by construction. `position:sticky; top:0; z-index:3; background:var(--color-bg)`. **NOT VIEW_OWNS_HEIGHT**: an inner scroller would take the scrollbar gutter, `keepScroll`, `openSettingsAt`'s landing and `settingsHoldHeights` with it, to arrive at the same row in the same place; the register pins its band and head exactly this way, and `#content` carries no rule at all so nothing between them clips.
- **AND IT IS A SECOND RULE, `.st-tabs-pin`, BECAUSE THE ROW HAS THREE HOMES** — Team & Settings, Our standards and the Templates page's two tabs, made one control on three pages on purpose, so a pin on the BASE rule pins all three. **RE-KEYED FROM THE PAGE ONTO THE ROW on 18 Sep 2026** (it was `.st-page > .st-tabs` for one day) when Young asked for Our standards too: with two of the three wanting it, the decision belongs beside the markup that draws the row, not in a selector here that grows a page name each time. **THE PAGE OPTS IN** with `class="st-tabs st-tabs-pin"`. The Templates row is NOT opted in — not asked for, and it carries an inline `margin-bottom` the bleed's shorthand would fight; MEASURED on all three, it is byte for byte what it was (`static`, glyph 48, left 16).
- **OUR STANDARDS IS THE SECOND PAGE** (Young ruled 18 Sep 2026: *"Similar to the team and settings page, the page should scroll behind the tab line"*). Same class, same rule, and its tab press lands at the top through **`stLandTop`, the settings page's own reading**, asked through `window` because that module is not on every stage — never a second copy. Its handler still does not rebuild the page (that page's own rule); a scroll is not a rebuild.
- **IT BLEEDS TO THE PAGE'S EDGES AND PUTS THE PAGE'S OWN TOP PADDING BACK INSIDE** — `.room-band`'s idiom, read from `--page-pad-t` / `--page-pad-x`, never typed. Two things ride on it: the first painted glyph does not move (MEASURED equal at four widths, at rest and pinned, so "ONE HEADER TOP" still measures what it measured), and a pinned row has to be opaque across the WHOLE width or the body shows through the gutters.
- **AND A TAB PRESS LANDS AT THE TOP.** Until the row was pinned this came for free — reaching a tab meant already being at the top. `stLandTop()` is the ONE reading (lifted out of `openSettingsAt`, which keeps its own behaviour byte for byte) with two callers; a press that NAVIGATES may land at the top, and this one is four different pages of settings that already clears the search.
- **THE FILL PREVIEW: `readonly`, NEVER `disabled`, AND NOTHING COVERS THE COLUMN.** `inert` + `pointer-events:none` stopped the reader scrolling as well as typing — a subtree the browser will not hit-test takes no wheel and no scrollbar drag. MEASURED at the parent: 1,758px of agreement in a 320px box, a scrollbar drawn, a real wheel moving it **0px**. Answered where the box is DRAWN (`docBody`'s own `locked` line, `c._preview`, set in one place and read in one place). **`disabled` IS THE WRONG ATTRIBUTE AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE SCREEN**: `.field:disabled` deliberately dresses a locked blank as plain settled text — right on the counterparty's copy, wrong here, where the blanks ARE what the reader is answering and the one under their caret is lit. MEASURED both ways: `readonly` leaves the blank rgb(17,94,89) on rgb(204,251,241) with a dashed rule, byte for byte what it was, and `tabindex="-1"` keeps six boxes that answer nothing out of the tab order. The paper does not move: first ink 54, 1758×492, identical to the parent.
Tests: f193 (the landing re-pointed in place to the reading, plus the rule's claims, the opt-in re-keyed in place), f272 (the standards page's own two), f331 (12) extended; white-band-and-tabs-verify 6d–6g, standards-page-verify 7a–7e, paper-beside-questions-verify 1g reversed in place and 1g2 — every new claim red at the parent bar the named controls.

## EVERY CREATION DOOR DRAWS THE PAPER (Young ruled 18 Sep 2026, off two screenshots)

**EXCEPT THE NEW AGREEMENT POP-UP SINCE 23 SEP 2026**, which mounts these three forms and hands them no paper host, so it draws none on any device (the owner's ruling — see THE NEW AGREEMENT POP-UP IS AN ASK ACROSS THE TOP). Each door's OWN dialog below is unchanged.

*"Make sure all drafted templates come with the page on the right but just for filling in the basic entry fields currently on the pop-ups."* The wizard and the saved-template fill screen gained the pane that morning; **`openContractEssentials` — the PUBLISHED COMPANY STANDARD and a saved template with NO blanks — did not**, so the one creation screen saying least about what it was making showed nothing at all.

- **THE SAME THREE NAMES, NOT A THIRD PREVIEW**: `fillPreviewFits` / `fillPreviewPaneHtml` / `fillPreviewWire`, `#ce-cols` in the other doors' own two-column shape, `maxWidth` 1040 / 620 and `FILL_PREVIEW_MIN_W` unchanged. **THE QUESTIONS DO NOT CHANGE BY A BOX** — the owner asked for the paper, not for more to answer; `CONTRACT_ESSENTIALS` is still the one list of seven and two browser checks are CONTROLS that pass at the parent to prove it.
- **`kind:'essentials'` IS A THIRD KIND, NOT A THIRD BUILDER**: the caller hands the WORDING (`o.paper`, a string or a promise of one) because only the caller knows where it comes from, and `fillPreviewContract` applies the answers through **`applyContractEssentials`** — the very function the press uses, so there is no second mapping to drift from the first.
- **THE COMPANY STANDARD'S PAPER IS READ OFF THE SERVER**: `GET /api/templates/:id/versions/:vid` gained an additive `values`, resolved by the server's own `templateFormResolveDefaults(…, tplOrgValues())`, and the browser renders through `templateFormDocHtml` — the SAME function `POST /contracts` calls, shared out of js/templateform.js. **A browser twin of `tplOrgValues` was the other answer and is the worse one**: it would drift the first time the org profile grew a field, and a preview showing a blank where the contract says "Highland Corporate Ltd" is this codebase's most expensive fault class. f104 pins the two as EQUAL rather than as any particular string.
- **A WAIT AND A REFUSAL BOTH SAY SO** (`tf_preview_reading` / `tf_preview_none`, both books): a pane empty while a fetch is out reads as a product that drew nothing, and one empty after a refusal reads as a contract with no wording. Neither ever blocks Create or Skip.
- **THE COUNT IS ONLY DRAWN WHERE IT IS TRUE**: `fillPreviewCounts(kind)` is false for `'essentials'`. "N blanks left" counts empty ANSWERS, and on the other two doors those answers ARE the document's blanks; here the wording is already complete and the questions are record facts, so the same arithmetic would print a number about the form and attach it to the paper.
Tests: f331 (three claims, all red at the parent), f104 (the no-drift wall, red at the parent), paper-beside-questions-verify section 7 (7a/7b/7e/7f red at the parent; 7c and 7g are the named controls).

## THE OVERNIGHT RUN — IDEAS 4 TO 9 AND THE SEVEN REPAIRS (Young ordered it 18 Sep 2026)

Thirteen pieces off "HaTi, today and next". Tests: **f332** (the repairs), **f333** (the upgrades) — 49 of 57 claims red at the parent; the eight walls are named in each header.

- **THE STANDARDS CHECK KEEPS WHAT SOMEBODY DECIDED.** `pbCarryDecisions` inside `runPlaybookReview` — the one place a review is BUILT, so all eight callers inherit it. Paired by category IN ORDER (a category is not unique: the supply book carries Liability cap as a position AND a range); carried onto an OPEN departure only; a stamp whose wording has moved is kept and marked `staleQuote`, which stops it settling the row (`signCheckAcceptStale`). `signCheckAccept` records the quote it answered.
- **THE BUILDER'S THREE.** `tbDropExtraBody` — Apply replaces the SECTION, removal only, highest index first (`tbAddBlock` is still the one push; the three content writers are still three). The clause number is DERIVED in `templateFormDocHtml` and the builder's paper asks the same two readings (`tplFormHeadingNumbered`): the first heading is the document's TITLE and carries none, a heading carrying its own keeps it. `tbBlanksRun` counts itself (`_tb.reads++`, three call sites) and speaks a refusal through `tbSay`; an empty answer stays silent.
- **A CONTRACT CAN BE RENAMED.** A `name` row first on the record, one builder with the grid, writes on BLUR with one audit line, an empty box never clears it. Still frozen at execution.
- **WHICH SIDE OF THE MONEY WE ARE ON** is asked at both creation doors (`key:'side'` → `maps:'category'`, the field the upload path fills). "Neither" writes nothing. `fieldOpt` is the ONE reading of a `{v,l}` option, asked by the coercer and both field renderers.
- **RE-FILING IS DELEGABLE**: `mayReFile` (browser) / `mayReFileRow` (server), `users.re_file` DEFAULT 0, on ADMIN_ONLY_USER_FIELDS, an admin's grant only.
- **"NOTHING FITS" LEADS TO REQUESTS** — `draftNothingFits` opens `openIntakeForm(pre)` with the sentence already typed; the picker stays.
- **THE PLAYBOOK READS THE TYPE THE RECORD HOLDS.** `contractTypeRead` / `copilotContractType` (`CKIND_SAYS_NOTHING` = external document · contract): the curated template kind wins, the extraction answers where it says nothing. f133 requires both hosts to answer the same key. The Overview prints it (`ov_f_type`).
- **THE APPROVAL CARD HAS A CLEAN STATE** — `approvalChainHtml(c,{clear:true})`, opt-in so every older caller is byte-identical, asked by the Signing tab only while the record is open.
- **OBLIGATIONS ON THE PHONE**: a fourth tab (`mObligHtml`, `mObligCountHtml`, `M_OB_BANDS`), the desktop tab's own count and bands, every reading borrowed, READ-ONLY by construction.
- **A CONTRACT IN DISPUTE IS FROZEN AND MORE VISIBLE.** `c.hold={at,by,why}` beside the status, absent on file, NOT on EXECUTED_IMMUTABLE; `contractOnHold` / `contractSetHold` (a reason is required); a display overlay outranking the other three (`HOLD_META`, ruby). THREE server refusals asked as a difference — wording, negotiation, signature — plus `signBlockers`' first row. A menu row beside Archive and a `hold` filter. **It is not Archive: it stays on every list.**
- **A FIFTH LINK PURPOSE, `advise`** (js/adviserlink.js — **NOT js/advice.js, which is the Advice Desk and was overwritten once**). `shareAdviceBody` narrows the payload to the chosen clauses and the full wording is dropped in the same breath; `shareIsAdvice` is deliberately NOT on `shareIsReadOnly` (a note is the whole product of the link) and `refuseIfAdvice` guards the three routes that touch wording, a decision or a signature. **Where it LIVES was reversed the same day — see BUILT TO THE DRAWING below: it is the fifth segment on the send screen, not a door of its own.**

## BUILT TO THE DRAWING, NOT THE HEADLINE (Young reported it 18 Sep 2026)

*"Upgrade number 4 is not working according to the artifact. It seems almost all the changes did not go to main or were not coded."* **The code HAD reached main and upgrade 4 DID work when driven** — every name verified against `origin/main`, the door pressed end to end in a browser. What was true is the half under it: each upgrade was built to its HEADLINE and not to its DRAWING. Measured panel by panel, five of six fell short; U8 alone matched.

- **THE REFUSAL NAMES THE NEAREST AND CARRIES TWO DOORS** (U4): `/api/ai/draft` answers `why` in BOTH cases and a new `closest`; the no-pick branch returns `closestName` — a NAME the route resolves, never a template id. `draftNothingFits(out, sentence, why, closest)` prints the reason, then *Send this as a request* (→ `openIntakeForm({need})`) and, only where `mayMakeNewPaper()` says so, *Write a template from this*. `intakeAnswerLine()` says who has been answering and how long they take, and REFUSES rather than averaging two requests into a promise.
- **THE TYPE DECIDES, AND ONLY THEN THE VALUE STREAM** (U5) — see the four passes written into `playbookKeyFor`; `copilotPlaybookKey` mirrors it pass for pass and f133 requires the same key. The "did anybody read a type" question carries its OWN regex fallback, because js/core.js is not on every stage and an ABSENT constant read as "yes" would take the stream fallback away from every upload. The Overview's reading row prints the book the review STAMPED (`c.playbook.label`), never one re-resolved today.
- **THE APPROVAL GATE'S CLEAN STATE IS A RECEIPT** (U6): `approvalClearRows` / `approvalClearHtml` / `APPROVAL_CLEAR_SAYS` (js/approvals.js, published) — one line per rule that was actually checked, asked through `ruleMatches`, the same reading the gate asks. **KEYED ON THE CONDITION TYPE**, never the rule's typed name. A workspace with NO rules keeps the old prose: a receipt for nothing is the reassurance this replaced. It is not permission to sign.
- **CHASE AND MARK DONE ARE ON THE PHONE** (U7), through the desktop's own funnel (`obligationChase`; `openObligationDone` when completing, `toggleObligation` when reopening) and drawn only where they would work. Required documents ride in on the same list with their own state line in `M_OB_DOC_TONE` — the desktop table's own tones. The amount prints through `obligationMoneyText`; it was drawing a bare `840000`. **The rule the first build reached for — "the phone files no change of its own" — is about a negotiation CHANGE to the wording**, and an obligation is not one.
- **ADVISER IS THE FIFTH SEGMENT ON THE SEND SCREEN** (U9), beside Sign · Negotiate · View, and the More-menu row is a PROXY onto it (`openAdviserLink` → `openShareModal(c,{purpose:'advise'})`) — the first build gave it a dialog of its own, which is a second door onto one act. `shareAdviseBlockHtml` asks the one question the other purposes do not (which clauses) and says the link lives `ADVISE_LINK_DAYS` 14 and that no seat is used; built once and TOGGLED by `paintPurpose`, never rebuilt. The payload is REBUILT at send time (`let payloadObj`) so the narrowing stays in `buildSharePayload`. Advise joins sign and history in never being refreshed onto somebody else's standing link, and the **Word channel is refused** — that channel attaches the whole contract, which is what this purpose exists not to send.

**TWO REAL DEFECTS FOUND ON THE WAY, both only visible when driven:**
- **AN ADVISER'S NOTE REACHED THE COUNTERPARTY.** `GET /api/shares/:token` served the whole message table to every link holder and an adviser posts through the SAME route. `MSG_SIDE_ADVISER` is a THIRD side value (so every `WHERE m.side = 'counterparty'` count stays honest) and `contractMessages(contractId, opts)` DROPS those rows unless the caller says who is asking — `{adviser:true}` for a signed-in colleague, `{adviserToken}` for that adviser's own link, nothing for everybody else. Every call site says; a reader that forgets gets the walled answer.
- **THE ADVISER PICKER COULD NEVER OFFER A CLAUSE.** It filtered on `cl.id`; `clauseSegment` writes `clauseId`, and only once `negoStampContract` has stamped the body. `adviserClauseKey(cl,i)` is the ONE handle — the stored id where there is one, the clause's POSITION where there is not — with `shareAdviceBody` resolving by the same rule over the same body in the same breath. A clause is its `headingHtml` PLUS its `bodyHtml`; taking `html` alone returned '' on every clause.

Tests: f333 (17 claims red at the parent, plus a DRIVEN wall test that reproduces the counterparty reading the adviser's note before the fix), f133 (two new cases, both red); phone-verify's obligations section (5 of 8 red — the parent prints `840000`), signing-flow-verify 8a–8d (4 of 4 red), share-recipient-verify's adviser section (its gate red at the parent, because the picker offered nothing). Re-pointed in place: f17 (**it pinned a LINE BREAK, not the relation**), f42, f133's mirror world (**it was measuring the FALLBACK, not the real pair**), f333 (4)(7c)(7c4)(9e).

## THIRTEEN OFF A MORNING OF SCREENSHOTS (Young, 19 Sep 2026)

Four of the thirteen were INVISIBLE IN THE SOURCE. Tests: **f335** (67 claims, 53 red at the parent), **thirteen-screenshots-verify** (20 checks, 13 red).

- **AN EMPTY `<select>` IS LEGAL MARKUP.** The On hold filter's `holdOpts` was the only one of eight without the `.map` that turns its pairs into `<option>`s, so the raw array interpolated as bare text, the browser discarded it, and the control drew with ZERO options — minimum width, unswitchable, silent. **AND THE SAME REPORT CARRIED A SECOND FAULT**: the filter block narrowed a `rows` that exists nowhere in `regFiltered`, so every press threw inside a READING. **THE LINT SWEEP HAD BOTH ALL ALONG** — the only two errors in js/, read as pre-existing noise. `npm run lint` is at **0 errors**; it is the one check that asks "is every name being called a name that exists", and a standing error is a defect nobody has driven yet.
- **`#share-step2-head` IS HIDDEN IN THE MARKUP**, not by `step(1)` — which runs after three awaits. The kind step eight lines above records this exact fault in its own note. MEASURED at the parent: 90px on the opening frame, 0 after. **THE OPENING FRAME IS READ IN THE SAME TICK** — a sampler on a timer never sees it locally (every await resolves in one microtask flush) and a stub on `window.contractShares` does not bite, because `openShareModal` calls the BARE name.
- **`focusKeyTerms(c, field)`** — FOUR faults under one report. It looked for a box the Overview does not draw (The record is SHUT, and a shut `sectionHtml` draws no body AND no acts); `roomGoTab` is a NO-OP when already on the tab so nothing repainted; a Key terms row shows its read-out at rest and opens its control only ON A PRESS (the `data-ov-move-stream` handler records the same measurement); and the row you came FROM repaints the panel 120ms later on blur and took the caret back out. Posture first, ONE paint, press the row as a reader would, then a BOUNDED wait for a PAINTED box (`KT_FOCUS_TRIES` 12). **`_ktLanding` is `_ktFolderBusy`'s own idiom** — one flag, one guard, the panel holds still while the product itself moves the caret. `KT_FIELD_HOME` is the one map field → box → section; f311's "hands the contract, NOT the field name" is REVERSED IN PLACE (every row landed on counterparty).
- **A FINGER IS NOT A WHEEL.** The 15 Sep fix forwarded `wheel`; the Plain English layer is a CLIP whose scroller is its SIBLING, so a touch drag had nothing to pan and moved nothing, silently. `touchstart`/`touchmove` push `#doc-scroll` by the distance travelled, one finger only, swallowed only where the paper moved, `touch-action:pan-y`. **IT STILL GROWS NO SCROLLER OF ITS OWN.**
- **THE EIGHTH PER-PERSON GRANT**: `mayHoldContract` / server `mayHoldRow`, column `hold_contracts` DEFAULT 0, on `ADMIN_ONLY_USER_FIELDS`. The owner asked for "Legal/Admin only" and was shown that HaTi's `legal` role IS the Editor — so that rule would have changed nothing — and chose the tick. **The PUT guard is asked as a DIFFERENCE, BOTH DIRECTIONS**: letting anyone lift a freeze makes setting one worth nothing. `hold`/`release` join `REG_ROW_ACTIONS` (the drawing's own home); `holdWhyShort` + `HOLD_WHY_ROW` print the reason on the row with the whole of it on the hover; the CHIP is not swept (a card has room for a sentence).
- **`SIGN_WHY_WORDS` 15** on the Before-you-sign rows, whole sentence on the hover — there was no cap at all and the negotiation row ran to 35 words, growing with the clause list. `signWhyShort` cuts WORDS, never characters, and a short sentence is byte-identical. The standards row's double full stop (`{pos}` already ends in one) is stripped in the PRINT, never the record.
- **"Key terms" → "Overview" in seven live sentences, both books.** `tab_key_terms` STAYS — the PHONE draws its own tab. `rn_fix_terms` / `rn_no_notice_fix` stay inert.
- **ONE MARK, ONE MEANING.** `scan` was Focus mode AND the risk scan, one glyph doing both. `readpaper` is the READING family's own and the whole family moved together (the three checks, the Contracts findings badge, the playbook Run button, the OCR and metadata lines, Copilot's pulse) — a badge that no longer matches the button that produced it is the fault this removes. `scan` keeps Focus mode (four callers, all of it). **Focus mode is a BUTTON on both heads** (`.room-focus`, `order:5`, the checks' own square so the row does not grow) AND keeps its ⋯ row: TWO DOORS, ONE HANDLER, `querySelectorAll` — f295's lesson.
- **PLAIN ENGLISH MARKS FACTS, NOT OPINIONS.** `docReadMark` BORROWS `briefMark` (the brief's deterministic pass for money, periods, percentages, dates) — no model, no spend, and what the route is sent does not move by a byte. `docReadFlags` ambers a clause only where the playbook disagreed or the scan flagged, placed through `rlPbFindClause`, which REFUSES rather than guesses; computed ONCE per paint. **RED AND GREEN ARE ABSENT AND THAT IS THE RULING** — a colour saying "this is bad for you" with nothing behind it is Copilot on its own authority, and in HaTi green and red are the redline's own grammar.
- **A POP-UP WITH ONE ROW IN IT IS A BUTTON WEARING A COSTUME**: `tplRowMoreMenu` runs the act directly where only one row survives (a built-in has no versions, no wording, no blanks, nothing to delete). `ACT` names every row's act ONCE and the wiring presses the same table. The head gains a 2px accent rule and the template's category and stream as chips, the stream's dot read from `FOLDERS`' own colour.
- **REVIEW VS PLAYBOOK IS OFF THE MORE MENU** (the shield runs the same pass twelve pixels away) — **REVERSED 20 Sep 2026 when the shield itself went; the row is back and is the only door on that page.** **Prepare redlines STAYS** by the owner's word. Said out loud: `rlOpenPlaybookReview` — the window that lets you TICK which proposals to file — is now unreachable; the act survives in Prepare redlines, the CHOOSING does not.
- **THE SEAT SWITCH WEARS THE CONTRACT VIEW SWITCH'S CLOTHES** (`--accent-ink` on the box and the resting word; the two differed in exactly those two places). The clause panel's History | + notes moves with it — one rule, two homes, f236 pins the pair.
- The Contract Graph's caption is GONE (a page explaining itself under its own title, and hard-coded English); its under-899px rule went with it. The total count went with it, said out loud.

## FIVE OFF FOUR SCREENSHOTS (Young ruled it 19 Sep 2026, afternoon)

*"Image 1, make the highlighted KPIs to be KPI cards. Image 2, delete this card. Image 3, shading should fill the whole shaded button. Image 4, focus mode button in the editor page is not working. Finally, Plain English contract should highlight obligations in Amber."* Two of the five are this codebase's own standing faults in new costumes, and a third was found on the way.

- **THE FRICTION BRIEF'S FOUR FIGURES ARE KPI CARDS** (`.igf-kpis` / `.igf-kpi` in index.html, the dress in a RULE so it follows `.hm-tile` the next time that is retuned — a second set of inline literals could not). Same four readings, same order, same labels; the LABEL leads now, which is the tile's own order. **ONE EDGE COLOUR AND IT IS THE ACCENT**: Home cycles four tones BY POSITION, which is decoration there and would be a claim here — ruby is this product's word for "this is against you" and HaTi holds no target for any of these four. **NOT BUTTONS**: Home's rule is that a card opens the list that would change its number and none of these has a list.
- **BIGGEST BY CONTRACTED VALUE IS DELETED** (`pfBiggest` gone, not stubbed; off the publish list; the note in js/views/portfolio.js says what was lost). **WHAT SURVIVES**: the counterparty FILTER it fed is on every dot of the risk map (`data-pf-cp`, one handler), and it was never in `PF_PANEL_DATA`, so Copilot loses nothing. `pf_biggest`, `pf_largest`, `pf_in_focus`, `pf_click_a_row`, `pf_no_counterparty` are STALE ON THE FACE, inert in both books.
- **THE SEAT SWITCH'S FILL REACHES THE EDGE.** MEASURED at the parent: a 3px gap top AND bottom, a 2px radius inside a square corner, `overflow:visible`. **THE ANSWER IS `.doc-read-seg`'s OWN MECHANISM, not a second set of numbers** — the box clips and the segment takes `height:100%`. **AND THE CAUSE WAS ONE RUNG UP**: `.redline-page .rl-head .rl-segwrap:not(.rl-readwrap) .rl-seg` stated `height:20px` at five classes and outranked it. That declaration is DELETED — it was the answer from the days when the lit half was a pill in a box, and the rule it lives in is about the row's TYPE rung. Fixed at the cause, never by out-weighting. The clause panel's History | + notes moves with it, as f236 pins.
- **FOCUS MODE ON THE NEGOTIATE PAGE** — f295's lesson a third time. `roomHeadHtml` draws the button for BOTH shells; the contract room answers `data-ws-focus` in `wireWsFocus`; this page wired `#ws-focus`, which is the MENU ROW's id. It drew, it was hit-testable, and pressing it left the head standing at 146px. `querySelectorAll('[data-rl-focus],[data-ws-focus]')` is the one act, and `rlPaintFocusBtn` paints the button **scoped to `rlFocusPage()`** — two painters on one element is drift, and in the room there is no redline page around the head.
- **THE PLAIN ENGLISH AMBER IS KEYED ON THE HEADING, AND THAT IS WHAT MADE IT WORK AT ALL.** **THE OBLIGATIONS HALF OF THIS IS REVERSED THE SAME EVENING — see THE WORDS THAT MAKE A PROMISE below**; what stands is the KEY. `docReadSheet` walks the PAINTED page and its rows carry no clause id at all, while `rlPbFindClause` MINTS one for any body never stamped — so the amber added on 18 Sep had never marked a single clause. `_docReadNorm(headingText)` is the pairing `docReadAnchors` already uses; an empty heading is REFUSED rather than collapsed into one key. **THE MAP CARRIES EVERY REASON**, not the first, because the hover is the only place a reader is told why the bar is there — it stays on two sources, and the collect is what would make a third safe.
Tests: f337 (31 claims, 22 red at the parent; section 5 REVERSED IN PLACE that evening), five-screenshots-verify (29 checks driven in a real browser, 17 red — the parent prints `gap 3 top / 3 bottom`, `146px before / 146px after`, `0 flags`; 5b–5f reversed in place and are named controls now). Re-pointed in place: f335's `add` guard (**the half it pinned was the bug**) and f277's control-row slice (**PIN THE REGION, NOT A BYTE COUNT**, paid again).

## THE WORDS THAT MAKE A PROMISE (Young ruled 19 Sep 2026, evening)

*"I was asking to highlight only the absolute action guiding key words that make a clause an obligation."* Then, over a render of the two readings: *"Let's go with option 2 … turn that area to a trigger for turning on the amber highlighting."* And yes to all four decisions the render put to him.

**THIS REVERSES THE OBLIGATIONS BAR OF THAT MORNING**, kept above because the reasoning under it is what makes this safe. The bar was a fact about the CLAUSE where the ask was about the WORDS; and once the words are lit, one colour would say two things twelve pixels apart. So `docReadFlags` keeps its two JUDGEMENTS (your playbook disagreed, the risk scan flagged it) and the PROMISE is on the wording. `ct_read_watch_oblig` is STALE, inert in both books.
- **NO MODEL DECIDES WHICH.** `DOC_DUTY_RE` (js/views/contract.js, beside `docReadMark`) is a DUTY MODAL followed by an ACTION VERB, borrowing `heuristicObligations`' own five families — payment, notice, reporting, delivery, insurance — narrowed from the SENTENCE that reader finds to the VERB PHRASE inside it. `will` and `may` are absent by name and the file says why. "shall be governed", "shall be deemed", "shall survive", "shall have the right to" all mark NOTHING.
- **"NOT" TURNS A DUTY INTO A CARVE-OUT ON EXACTLY TWO VERBS.** MEASURED on the 50 real agreements already committed for the extraction scorecard: six of the eight negated phrases are genuine negative covenants, and the two commonest are not — "shall not be liable" (23 hits) and "shall not be responsible" (5) are LIMITATIONS. `DOC_DUTY_STATE` is those two and refuses `not`. Rate on that corpus: 10.2% of sentences, 135 distinct phrases.
- **THE WALL, TWICE.** No pattern contains a digit, bracket or angle bracket (a letters-and-spaces pattern stops dead at a `<`), and `docDutyMark` refuses any match carrying `<`, `>` or `&` — because it runs over text `briefMark` has already marked up. The two passes never mark the same words: figures are numbers, duties are verbs.
- **THE CAPTION SLOT IS THE SWITCH.** `.doc-read-duty` takes the `<em>`'s exact place and register (`margin-left:auto`, the label size, weight 400, no letter-spacing, no upper case, no fill, no edge); *"a reading, not the contract"* is the `title` on `.doc-read-lbl`; the COUNT is on the face and at zero the switch is not drawn. It rides the LAYER's one delegated listener — the head is rebuilt every paint. **OFF at rest, remembered in this browser** under `DOC_DUTY_KEY` (`hati.v1.docDutyMarks`), its own key, DOC_READ_KEY's own shape. Colour and the ring, never the weight.
- **BOTH COLUMNS, AND THE PAPER IS NOT WRITTEN ON.** `docDutyPaperPaint` writes spans into TEXT NODES on the painted canvas — `signSpotsPaint`'s own precedent and lifetime (`wireDocCanvas`) — and takes them off again; it never crosses an element boundary (`redlineHangHtml`'s rule), never touches a box the reader types in (`DOC_DUTY_PAPER_SKIP`), sets text with `textContent` and never `innerHTML`, and caps at `DOC_DUTY_PAPER_MAX` 1200 as a guard, not a product limit. **IT COSTS NO LAYOUT AND THAT IS A REQUIREMENT**: a wash and an underline, no padding, margin, weight or size — measured block by block with the marks off and on.
- **THE COUNT ASKS BOTH COLUMNS.** A reading is a translation, not a transcript — "shall pay" comes back as "must pay" — so a clause counts where either carries a duty phrase. **WALL**: nothing here reaches `docReadClauses` or `docReadSig`, so what the route is sent does not move by a byte.
- i18n: CONTRACT TEXT IS NEVER TRANSLATED, so this reads English drafting and a Swedish contract marks nothing and counts zero, which is honest.
Tests: f339 (46 claims, 41 red at the parent), duty-marks-verify (34 checks in a real browser, both themes, 26 red — the parent prints the switch `not drawn` and `0 marks` in both columns; every claim that could pass on an unmarked page is GATED). Re-pointed in place: f337 section 5 and five-screenshots-verify 5b–5f.

### CAPITALS, A TICK-BOX, AND THE TWO SHEETS AT ONE HEIGHT (Young ruled 19 Sep 2026, night)

*"Make Highlight obligations to be in Capital letters but Not [bold]. And instead of having a Circle to [select], make it a Square and [select] with a checkmark."* Then, ringing the gap across both columns: *"the top edge of the contract pages do not start from the same point. They should be the same distance between top the edge and the contracts."*
- **THE CAPITALS ARE THE ROW'S OWN, NOT THREE NEW NUMBERS.** `.doc-read-head` already states the product's label treatment (`--t-micro`, uppercase, `.09em`) and the switch was opting OUT of all four of its declarations. **It opts out of exactly ONE now — the weight** — so it is the same label as PLAIN ENGLISH beside it in body weight: a RELATION that follows the row when the row is retuned. The `<em>` keeps its own four because it is STALE and nothing draws it.
- **BUT THE CAPITALS HAD TO BE NAMED, AND `inherit` IS WHAT NAMES THEM.** The compiled Tailwind preflight sets `text-transform` to none on every BUTTON, and **INHERITANCE IS NOT A CASCADE CONTEST** — any matching declaration beats it, so the head's uppercase never reached this button and it drew in sentence case while the source read perfectly. The third costume of that fault here (`--dr-face` on the edition's headings was the last), and `inherit` rather than the word, for the edition's own reason. The letter-spacing genuinely inherits and is left to.
- **A NOTE THAT QUOTES A RULE MAY NOT SIT INSIDE THE BRACES.** Written there, its closing brace cut this rule in half for every net that slices one rule out of the sheet — measured. It also put `--dr-face` inside `.doc-read-head`'s region and failed f277 (18), whose sweep reads CODE: **comments are prose**, and that sweep strips them now.
- **THE TICK-BOX IS THE PLATFORM'S OWN CORNER AND THE PRODUCT'S OWN CHECK.** `.dr-tick` is 12px square at `--radius` (what "square" means here) holding `#i-check`; the symbol is in the markup at BOTH states — `color:transparent` at rest, inked when pressed — so a press changes no box and moves nothing in the row. `.dr-ring` is GONE from this switch. **THE INK ON THE FILL IS `#2A1B04`, A LITERAL ON PURPOSE**: it is `#hdr-notify-dot`'s own, the one other place this product puts ink on `--st-amber-dot`, and that token is the SAME colour in both themes, so a theme-aware token above it would be the wrong kind of answer.
- **THE TWO SHEETS START AT ONE HEIGHT.** MEASURED against the grid's own top: the cream sheet at 4 and `#doc-read` at 0. The 4 is `#doc-scroll`'s own `padding-top:var(--s-1)` and the card is absolutely positioned on the grid and knew nothing about it. **THE CARD COMES DOWN TO THE PAPER, NEVER THE PAPER UP TO THE CARD** — refusal 3 by construction rather than by measurement — and it reads THE SAME TOKEN that padding reads, never a literal 4. The readings do not move with it: `docReadPaint` measures every note against the CLIP's own top, so a clip 4px lower yields tops 4px smaller and the same screen position.
Tests: f339 (six claims re-pointed and added, all six red at the parent), duty-marks-verify (41 checks, 7 red at the parent — it prints `switch 12px/400 normal none` against the label's `11px/700 0.99px uppercase`, `no .dr-tick`, and `sheet 4 · card 0`); f277 (18)'s caption sweep re-pointed in place and still a WALL at the parent.

## FOCUS MODE ON THE NEGOTIATE PAGE MIMICS THE DOCUMENT PAGE (Young ruled 19 Sep 2026, night)

*"When in negotiation page make it mimic the document page when on focus mode."* Ruled over a render of the two side by side, after two readings were put to the owner and withdrawn. **THIS REVERSES the 16 Aug "taken further than before, because you asked for the WHOLE page"**, kept beside the code because it is exactly how the READING CONTROLS came to be swept up with the chrome.
- **MEASURED AT THE PARENT**, entering focus on this page: bar 44→0, rail 240→0, head card 146→0 **and the control row 44→0** — so As agreed, With changes, the Deal board, the text size, the Internal/Counterparty seat and All negotiations were all unreachable from inside the mode a reader entered to read. The Document tab's own focus leaves bar 44, rail 240, row 39 and drops only the head card. One button, two meanings.
- **THE RULE IS THE ROOM'S**: the head card goes, this page's strip (`#rl-banner`) goes with it (the room hides `#ws-strips` in the same breath — that is why it is not an exception), the CONTROL ROW STAYS, the SHELL IS LEFT ALONE. `body.rl-focused`'s three rules — `#side-nav`, `#top-header`, and the zero-width first `#app-shell` track — are RETIRED; the 16 Aug void cannot return because nothing collapses the template any more. **The class itself stays set** as the mode's marker (setView clears it; `pw-focused` is told apart from it).
- **THE ROW KEEPS THE PAGE'S OWN GAP**: `.redline-page.rl-focus .rl-tabrow{padding-top:var(--page-pad-t);height:calc(var(--rl-tabrow-h) + var(--page-pad-t))}`. This page carries that gap INSIDE `#ws-head`, so hiding the head takes it away; and everything is border-box, so the height must be restated or every control in the row is squashed by 16px. **`--rl-tabrow-h` is the row's height said ONCE** — two rules that must agree about one number is a token.
- **THE COUNTERPARTY'S SEAT IS THE WALL**: their page mounts the same `.redline-page` and takes `.rl-focus` with it, so `.redline-page.rl-focus{padding:…}` is re-scoped to `body.pw-focused`; their shell really does stand down and their `#rl-banner` exception stands. Measured byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **THE LIST IS NEVER LEFT IN FOCUS**: All negotiations is ON that row and paints the list without `setView`, where app.js's reset lives — `renderNegotiationsList` is the ONE funnel (the door, the fall-through, the stale page, the sealed record) and clears the mode before it writes.
- **REFUSAL 3 BITES AND THE OWNER LIFTED IT**: focus used to hand the paper the whole window, and this takes the bar, the rail and the row back off it. Said before building; ruled twice and approved as a render.
Tests: f94 (F94-M, seven claims, six red at the parent; the white-row pin is the named CONTROL), focus-mimics-document-verify (24 checks in the REAL APP — 13 red at the parent, printing `negotiate bar 0 rail 0 row 0 head 0 · document bar 44 rail 240 row 40 head 0`; every claim gated on the row actually standing, or a hidden row satisfies them at 0). negotiations-door-verify 10 reversed in place; f94's "stands the whole page down" reversed in half.

## NO BRACE REACHES THE PAGE (Young reported it 20 Sep 2026)

*"where copilot generates information … i see this sign {} being generated. Fix this bug to Remote it from Outputs."* **THE RENDERER IS THE WALL, NEVER THE PROMPT** — `AI_TONE_RULES` is already written as duties with a worked example because asking nicely came back with no markers at all; a tighter sentence is one more thing a model may ignore on any given answer, and the reader would still see the braces.
- **THREE SHAPES, every one reproduced against `aiToneHtml` before a line moved**: DOUBLED BRACES (`{{+78%}}`, `{+78%}}`, `{{+78%}`) — the old pattern matched the INNER pair and left the outer ones standing, which is the owner's screenshot; **BOLD INSIDE A MARKER** (`{+**78%**}`), which the prompt ASKS FOR by instruction (one rule bolds every figure, another wraps the verdict), so `mdParse` makes it `<strong>` and the old body class `[^{}<>]` refused the `<`; and a doubled brace with NO tone character, the model garbling its own emphasis.
- **FOUR PATTERNS, ONE READING.** `AI_TONE_RE` makes both braces optional on each side so all four counts fold to one span; its body admits only NAMED INLINE tags (`AI_TONE_INLINE`) — a BLOCK tag would mean the marker ran across a paragraph, and a span wrapped round `</p><p>` is broken nesting. `AI_TONE_LOOSE_RE` unwraps a doubled pair carrying no tone character to its words and gives it NO colour (which verdict was meant is not knowable, and picking one is the product asserting one nobody wrote). `AI_TONE_ORPHAN_RE` is the last net — anything that HAD a closer is already a span, so a `{` still in front of a tone character is a marker the model never finished. `AI_TONE_SPLIT_RE` runs on the RAW text inside `aiRichText`, because a marker crossing a blank line is cut by `mdParse` and its closer is stranded in the NEXT paragraph.
- **THE WALL: `{{counterparty}}` KEEPS ITS BRACES.** That is this product's own template-blank syntax (`AI_TEMPLATE_RULE` asks the model for `{{lower_snake_case}}`), so the loose unwrap is guarded on the body containing a SPACE — emphasis is a phrase, a blank is one word.
- **TWO SURFACES, ONE READING, MEASURED**: `aiToneHtml` has no caller outside js/aimd.js and `aiRichText` has exactly two — the chat bubble (`aiFmt`) and Copilot's read on the Insights dock (`igFmtRich`) — which are the two screens the owner named.
Tests: f53 (eight claims), no-braces-in-copilot-verify (14 checks DRIVEN on both named screens; **4 red at the parent**, which prints `{sign within round 1}`, `under 1 day}`, `{2 hours`, `{-21% of deals}` and `{{2.0 rounds per deal}}` — the screenshot reproduced). The bold claim is asked as `.ai-tone strong`: **bold anywhere on the page passes at the parent and proves nothing.**

## THE LANDING IS THE FIRST TAB (Young ruled it 20 Sep 2026)

*"when you Navigate to the templates page, you should First Land in the first tab which in this case its The Book."* **THIS REVERSES 18 SEP 2026**, whose reasoning is kept whole in the code because it is what makes the reversal safe rather than a swing back: it MEASURED the overview at 44 pressable things and NOT ONE of them a verb, and landing a reader on the one screen in the section that cannot act was the cost. **THE SCREEN THAT ARGUMENT WAS ABOUT NO LONGER EXISTS** — the overview tab went on 19 Sep and the BOOK replaced it, and the book's cards ARE doors (each presses `tplGoBucket`, which lands on the table already narrowed to that bucket). The tab a reader arrives on now answers "what have we got" AND carries the way to the verbs.

## FOUR OFF TWO SCREENSHOTS AND A POP-UP (Young ruled it 20 Sep 2026)

*"the entry field for the end Date and value stream are overlapping … even through I have answered which side we are on, i still get an error when i try to create draft … the pull should generate from the company Standard contracts … review the pop up in image 3 and [ensure] it has no bugs plus it works easily without adding any confusion."* Two of the four are the SAME defect in two costumes: an option is a `{v,l}` pair and three readers were still stringifying it whole.

- **A SELECT THAT WAS ANSWERED IS NOT REFUSED.** `validateField`'s select branch read `(f.opts||[]).map(String)`, which turns a pair into `"[object Object]"` — it matched nothing, so a correctly answered select refused EVERY answer and named the options as objects. **The note beside `fieldOpt` says it is "asked by the coercer and by both field renderers"; THE VALIDATOR IS THE FOURTH READER** and was never added. `fieldOptHit(f, raw)` is the ONE reading of which option an answer names, asked by the validator AND the coercer, so the wall and the coercion cannot disagree; **the LABEL is accepted as well as the stored value** (a pasted or bulk answer carries the sentence a person read); the refusal prints the LABELS, because the stored word is one the reader was never shown. **THE SAME FAULT WAS IN TWO MORE PLACES**, both on the draft path: `js/draft.js` sent the model `opts.map(String)` and the route sent `one_of: …map(o=>String(o))`, so "Which side are we on?" could not be answered from a sentence at all. Both send `{value, means}` now.
- **A FIELD COLUMN MAY NOT GROW PAST ITS SHARE.** `1fr` is `minmax(auto,1fr)` and `auto` FLOORS a track at its content's min-content — and a date input's min-content is the BROWSER'S. **MEASURED: Chromium wants 151px and never blows out at any width this dialog reaches; Safari, where the report came from, draws "20. Sep 2026" with a stepper and wants far more than the 226px share**, so the track grew and the form painted over the contract beside it. `FIELD_GRID_CSS` (js/templatefields.js, published) is the ONE declaration — `minmax(0,1fr)` — read by all three doors that ask these questions (the essentials, the wizard's answer step, the saved-template fill), with the literal as the fallback for a stage that has not loaded that file. **THE OTHER HALF CANNOT BE WRITTEN INLINE**: `.field-grid > *{min-width:0}` lets the item shrink to its track and `.field-grid input,.field-grid select{max-width:100%;min-width:0}` stops the control painting past its own box. Either half alone still overflows.
- **THE COMPANY'S OWN STANDARD IS LOOKED AT FIRST.** `DRAFT_BUCKETS` (js/draft.js) is the ONE ordered list — company standard, then saved template, then HaTi's own — and the RANK is a fact off the record, never a judgement the model makes; `draftCandidates` sorts by it and the route is told the rule (`DRAFT_BUCKET_WORD`, an ALLOW-LIST so a caller cannot invent a fourth shelf). **IT IS A PREFERENCE, NOT A WALL**, because the owner named both escapes: *Pick one myself* was always on that screen, and a sentence that names a shelf wins (only an EXPLICIT ask counts — a preference is never read into the kind of agreement). **A COMPANY STANDARD USED TO TRAVEL WITH `fields: []`**, which made it the least described paper on the list; it carries `essentialFields()` now — the questions its own door asks. The offer card says which shelf it came off, read off the record and never off `why`. **THE LEAD SAYS WHERE COPILOT LOOKS FIRST AND STOPS THERE** — the POP-UP DIET pins `dr_lead` by name, so the other half (that you may ask for another shelf in your own words) rides the ask box's own hover, `dr_shelf_hint`, which is the diet's answer for machinery and the one control where knowing it changes what a reader types.
- **THE PICKER SHOWS YOUR OWN PAPER, AND HAS A WAY OUT.** The note above `lib` in js/wizard.js says company standards "stay pinned on top"; MEASURED, they were not drawn on that screen AT ALL — `lib` and `mine` reached only `rows`, which feeds the folders and the search, so the only paper a reader could SEE was four HaTi built-ins, and reaching your own standard meant knowing its stream or its name. `ours` draws them in ONE section above FOR YOU (one, not two — that screen already carries a curated row, a line of business, a search box and six folders, and the ask was to make it LESS confusing), drawn only when non-empty. It drew **no Cancel and no ✕** — Escape and the scrim worked, both of which a reader has to already know — so it has the same Cancel, in the same place, as every other dialog on the way to a contract. And `openWizard(preTid, prefill)` **dropped the prefill on two of its three doors**: pick a standard or a saved template and every answer Copilot had read was silently lost.
Tests: f340 (23 claims, **21 red at the parent**; the two that pass are named CONTROLS), form-and-picker-verify (18 checks in a real browser, **12 red at the parent**, which prints `203px past, 187px over the preview` and the owner's toast byte for byte). The overlap is STAGED — a control that wants more than its column, with structural selectors that bite on both builds — because Chromium cannot reproduce Safari's date control. f332 (6c) re-pointed in place to the relation (two askers, one reading) and given a sweep for the defect's own shape; f270's candidate order **reversed in place** and asked as the relation to `DRAFT_BUCKETS`.

## THE LINE OF BUSINESS KEEPS SEATS (Young reported it 20 Sep 2026)

*"What is the purpose of the line of business filter and does it work? It does not seem to be doing anything."* … *"simply fix the pop up to make sense for its purpose."*

**IT WORKED ON A NEW WORKSPACE AND COULD NOT WORK ON A USED ONE.** `forYouTemplates` picked usage → line of business → `TEMPLATE_STARTERS`, capped at four, so the moment a workspace had drafted from four built-ins usage filled every seat and the dropdown was never reached. **MEASURED on the owner's own case (five templates used): all four cards identical under all four lines of business** — Raw Material · NDA · Packaging · Marketing, which is the screenshot — **while the heading above them named one.**
- **THE CONTROL SAYS "tunes this list", SO IT TUNES THE LIST.** `FOR_YOU_MAX` 4 and `FOR_YOU_LOB_MIN` 2: usage still LEADS (what you actually draft is the better signal) but takes at most `MAX − LOB_MIN` seats **while a line of business is named**, then the line of business fills, then usage takes back whatever it left (a list this role cannot create from must not cost the row a seat), then the starters. **WHERE NO LINE OF BUSINESS IS SET THE OLD ORDER IS BYTE-IDENTICAL** — usage takes all four, and a fresh workspace still gets its industry's four whole. Both are named CONTROLS.
- **ONE READING**: `forYouPick(tmpls)` → `{list, lob, lobIds, lobShown}`; `forYouTemplates` is `forYouPick().list` under its old name, so no caller changed. The row and the heading above it cannot disagree about which cards these are or why.
- **THE HEADING IS TRUE BY MEASUREMENT, NEVER BY PROVENANCE.** It read `industry` — the stored setting — so it went on claiming a reason that had chosen nothing. It reads `fy.lobShown` now: **is at least one of the four ON that line of business's list**. A card usage picked that retail also wants IS retail paper and the heading may say so; where not one of the four is on the list it says nothing.
- NOT WIDENED, said out loud: `INDUSTRY_LABEL` is hardcoded English in js/wizard.js and is drawn on screen — a pre-existing i18n gap, one line in BUGLOG.
Tests: n6 (4) (ten claims, **six red at the parent**; the other four are named CONTROLS — 4c and 4j pass on both sides by design), form-and-picker-verify section 5 (six checks DRIVEN in a real browser, **2 red at the parent**, which prints the same four cards four times and the heading naming retail over them). **5d PASSES AT THE PARENT BECAUSE OF THE FAULT** — the old heading named the setting whatever the cards were — so it is labelled a CONTROL and **5e is the claim that the name is true**, checked against the cards rather than taken on trust.

## THE SHELF IS LOADED BEFORE IT IS OFFERED, AND A DATE BOX IS AN ORDINARY BOX (Young reported both 20 Sep 2026)

*"when i try to draft an agreement by describing what i want, it still Pulls from hati templates and not Company Standard"*, and, on an iPad, *"Start Date and end Date Field still seem not be lining up or to be the same size as the Rest."*

- **MEASURED FIRST, AND THE MEASUREMENT REFUSED THE OBVIOUS THEORY.** `tplLibRefresh()` is fired when the `+ Draft new agreement` menu opens and is NOT awaited, and both doors under it read `tplLibPublished()` SYNCHRONOUSLY — so the reading looked like a race. Driven in a browser with a real published standard: ONE fetch of `/api/templates` lands at boot, the shelf is warm on a settled page, and the ranking really does put `lib:` first. **The race is real and narrow (a slow connection, a fast reader), not the report.** `tplLibReady()` closes it anyway because it costs nothing: ONE SHARED FLIGHT, **the latch a boolean raised before the promise exists** (A LATCH MAY NOT BE ITS OWN PROMISE) and lowered on a failure so a failed load may be asked again. `draftRead(given)` reads the list AT THE PRESS — `given` is for a caller that names one, and the door names none; the picker re-draws when the standards land, **never over a typed answer** (`!tid`).
- **THE LIKELY FAULT IS THE SILENCE, AND IT IS THE ONE FIXED.** With NO company standard published there is nothing to prefer, and the card said only *"A HaTi template"* — leaving a reader unable to tell a standard that was PASSED OVER from a shelf that was EMPTY. Those are different faults and the screen answered neither. `draftNoStandards(cands)` is a FACT off the list handed to the route, never the model's word and never a guess; `dr_no_standards` rides the line that already names the shelf, only where the pick is not a standard AND there was none to be. Where standards DO exist the model's own `why` already says what they did not cover.
- **A DATE FIELD IS SIZED AND ALIGNED BY WEBKIT, NOT BY US.** MEASURED off the screenshot: every other box 477px, both date boxes about 520, and the date text CENTRED where every other field's text sits left. **NEITHER `width:100%` NOR the `max-width` added earlier that day reaches either** — `appearance:none` is what makes it an ordinary box and `::-webkit-date-and-time-value{text-align:left}` is what puts its text where the rest of the form's is. Scoped to `.field-grid`, so it reaches the three creation forms and nothing else; the native picker is untouched (appearance changes only how the box is drawn). **CHROMIUM NEEDS NEITHER RULE AND IS UNCHANGED BY BOTH, so this could not be reproduced here** — it is reasoned from the two numbers the screenshot carries and wants confirming on the reporter's own iPad. Said out loud rather than called fixed.
Tests: f340 (5) and (6) (eight claims, **seven red at the parent**; 5e is the named CONTROL). Section 6 pins the RULES — a browser that does not need them cannot measure them.

## SEVEN OFF FOUR SCREENSHOTS (Young ruled 20 Sep 2026)

*"go to wording is not working … Delete the Playbook Review Symbol button then move the Chat Symbol from the top bar and one to be next to the more button … there should be an alert that Pops up on the chat, obligations and Copilot Scan Buttons … the brief Witten and obligations should have Doors to the respective cards … make sure the Field actually Field the contracts correctly … the start and end Dates entry Fields should be the same size."* Then: *"simply add an arrow after the Header."* **THREE OF THE SEVEN WERE INVISIBLE IN THE SOURCE** and only showed up when the control was pressed.

- **`scanCanvas()` IS THE ONE READING OF WHICH CONTRACT PAGE IS ON SCREEN** (js/ai.js, published) — `#doc-canvas` else `#rl-doc`, asked by `clearQuoteMarks`, `scrollToQuote` and `scanGoTo`. All three named the Document tab's element, and the three check buttons that open the risk panel are drawn on the OTHER page, so every walk answered null and `scanGoTo` returned false **in silence**. The anchor fallback is asked INSIDE the root (`.rl-clause[data-clause-id]` is that page's own limb); no canvas at all SAYS so (`sc_goto_no_doc`). **THE FLASH PANE IS ASKED OF THE ELEMENT** (`closest('#doc-scroll, .nego-scroll')`) — named, it was null there, and a null pane made `small` true unconditionally, which would flash the whole sheet. MEASURED: `returned false · moved 0px` at the parent, `moved 1076px` after.
- **ONE DOOR ONTO THE PLAYBOOK READ, NEVER TWO AND NEVER NONE.** The shield is off `roomChecksHtml` and **the More-menu row is back** (`data-rl-pbreview` → `rlOpenPlaybookReview`, the RICHER door — it lets you tick which proposals to file, which the 19 Sep note recorded as lost). That note ended *"the handler and the two null-safe readers stay wired for that day"*; this is that day. `wireRoomChecks` keeps its playbook branch, said out loud. The glyph left `ng_review_vs_playbook` — key and markup each drew one, hence "✦ ✦". **The Document tab's Checks card is a different surface and is untouched.**
- **THE CHAT DOOR MOVED INTO `.room-acts`** (`roomChatDoorHtml`, first inside `.room-checks`, so "beside More" holds by construction). Same id, same `paintChatDoor`, same panel — MOVED, never copied; `index.html` draws it nowhere. It NARROWS honestly: the shell bar read `state.activeId` and sat live on the Contracts page pointing at whatever was open last, while `roomHeadHtml` is drawn by exactly the two pages that show one contract. **THE PRESS IS DELEGATED** (`_hdrChatWired`) — that head is rebuilt every render and a listener bound at boot would be live-looking and dead (f295's lesson). `paintChatDoor` is called from `wireRoomHead`, because `updateAlertBadge` runs on the view change BEFORE the markup lands. `icon('chat')` needed a key in the ICONS map: a missing key renders an EMPTY SVG, the sprite fault in that map's costume.
- **A COUNT ON THE CONTROL IS RUNG TWO, NOT A BAND.** `roomCheckBadge(n,tone)`, silent at zero (the bell's rule), amber open / ruby late-or-high. **`checkVerdict` CARRIES `n`** — the first build wrote a second arithmetic and it called `obState` with two arguments where it takes one, so two overdue drew an amber 2 beside a ruby label reading "2 overdue". Found by driving the page.
- **AN ARROW AFTER THE HEADING, AND THE TILE IS THE DOOR** (`doorFor`, `data-kt-tri-go`) — reverses this strip's own "NO ACTS ON IT, DELIBERATELY" for the two tiles Young named, and the arrow is the product's own mark (`Run →`). **ONE PRODUCER OF THE BODY** whichever shape the tile takes: a door is a `<button>`, a plain tile a `<div>`, and `class="kt-tri-td"` appears once. The strip's fixed height is untouched (measured 0px spread).
- **THE FILL TILE SAYS WHY, NOT MERELY WHETHER.** `contractBlanksNone(c)` (js/blanks.js, published) → `form` · `upload` · `nego` · `none`, null where there IS something to fill. The tick meant "I found no boxes" and the words said "I filled them in". **BOTH `form` ANSWERS ARE REASONS AND NEITHER IS A TICK** — returning null for a company-standard contract with fields still open let the tile fall through to the very tick being retired (item 7; `tplFormOpenCount` is the count its own panel already makes). TWO HEADS for the two kinds of nothing, or a tile contradicts itself twelve pixels apart. Steel, never green or ruby. The run stamps `none`; an older note falls back to a LIVE reading (the brief's own remedy).
- **A DATE BOX IS AN ORDINARY BOX**, widened: `box-sizing`, `width`, `min-width` STATED, the value's text left, the picker pinned right, no spin button. Chromium needs none of them and is unchanged by all of them — **wants one look on the reporter's own iPad**.

Re-pointed in place: f335 (4) and f269 (10) REVERSED, f264 (9), f266 (6), f340 (6), nego-redesign-verify 13. **THREE BYTE-COUNT SLICES RE-POINTED TO THEIR REGIONS** (f273's 900 characters, f295's 4,800) — and f295's "beside the playbook pass" had been passing against the COMMENT that explained the row's retirement. **PIN THE REGION, AND READ CODE, NOT PROSE.**

## A DOCUMENT SOMEBODY SENT US HAS BLANKS TOO (Young ruled 20 Sep 2026: "build it", item 9)

MEASURED: `contractHasBlanks` refused every upload BY NAME, so a received document carrying `[Insert Company Name]` twice and two ruled lines answered `[]` — no panel, no count, and the arrival tile reporting "nothing to fill" on the one kind of contract whose blanks nobody in this workspace wrote. `js/uploadblanks.js` is the reading: no route, no store, no new field (f342 (3f) greps).
- **THE PAPER IS THE ANSWER**, as it is for a drafted contract. Three shapes — `[Insert Company Name]`, `{{effective_date}}`, `_{3,}` — and the REFUSALS are the design: a bracket with no letter, a list marker or citation (`[1]`, `[a]`, `[iv]`), over 60 characters, or carrying `<`/a newline (the walk crossed an element boundary — `redlineHangHtml`'s rule); an underscore run flanked by word characters is part of a word.
- **THE MARKS ARE PAINTED ONTO THE CANVAS, NEVER BUILT INTO `docBody`** — `signSpotsPaint`'s and `docDutyPaperPaint`'s own precedent, third on `wireDocCanvas` and for the third time the same reason: docBody TRAVELS (their page, the PDF, the phone), and the C-7 header block was proved doing it on two real links. **Nothing serialises a painted canvas**, so our answers stay a working note on our own screen. `[data-upwording]` scopes the walk to the agreement, so HaTi's own signature block, file strip and seal card are never marked.
- **ONE MATCHER, TWO WALKS, AND THE PAINT NEVER MINTS A KEY.** The READ walks the wording SOURCE (so it answers in Node, at creation, before anything is mounted); the PAINT walks the canvas and takes its keys from `uploadBlankSeq`. **A WALK THAT DISAGREES WITH THE READ BY ONE CHARACTER DRAWS NOTHING** (`ceMarksPaint`'s rule) — count and `raw` both checked — so if a renderer ever did move a word the marks go quiet rather than land on the wrong sentence.
- **THE KEY IS THE PLACEHOLDER'S OWN NAME** (`up_insert_company_name`), so a name said through a twelve-page draft is ONE question answered once and marked everywhere. A ruled line has no name and takes the words in front of it, cut at the last sentence end (`…for one year. Delivery address: ____` asks for an address), with an ordinal where two follow the same words. Its own namespace, so it can never be `counterparty` or `value`.
- **NOTHING IS GUESSED**: `kind:'upload'` is what keeps these out of `fillBlanksFromRecord` (which filters `kind === 'field'`) — we did not draft the document, so we do not know whose side `[Company Name]` names. A person types them, through `contractBlankSet`, the ONE writer.
- **IT STOPS WHEN THE WORDING BECOMES OURS**: `uploadBlanksLive` refuses a redlined or versioned upload (`uploadWordingEdited`), a sealed record, a Viewer and PORTAL_MODE. **It is `docFillable`'s COUNTERPART, never its rival** — that reading refuses every upload by name and this one refuses everything else — which is what makes the panel's `||` one door rather than two. Asserted as a wall.
- The panel says `bf_title_upload`; the one fact about the machinery is a HOVER (`bf_upload_note`), never a band; an unnamed line is `bf_blank_n`. The mark is amber unanswered, the accent answered, and **costs the contract no layout** (measured block by block, off and on).
Tests: f342 (58 claims, **56 red at the parent**; the two that pass are named CONTROLS), upload-blanks-verify (29 checks in a real browser, **26 red**; every claim an empty page would satisfy is GATED).

### AND THE THIRD SHAPE: A WORD FILL-IN FIELD (Young ruled 20 Sep 2026: "Build the last item")

The artifact the owner approved promised *"brackets, underscores, a shaded Word field"*; the build above delivered the first two. MEASURED on a real .docx: a FORMTEXT field and a content control both arrive as ORDINARY SENTENCE TEXT (`"made with Enter buyer name of Click or tap here to enter text.."`) — both readers keep the result and drop everything saying it is a field. There is no pattern in the characters to find, so **THE FILE IS WHAT KNOWS** and `docxRunsHtml` marks it on the way in.
- **`DOCX_WFIELD_CLASS` / `RICH_WFIELD_CLASS` = `hati-wfield`**, admitted to `richSpanClassOk` beside `RICH_TOC_TAIL_CLASS` and for its reason: a SHAPE, not a mark, carrying no value of its own — the prompt is the span's own text. **NOT `hati-field`**, which means a blank on OUR template form routed by `data-field-key`. The drafter's own name (`w:name` / `w:alias`) rides as `data-wfield`, in EXACTLY `RICH_FIELD_KEY_RE`'s machine-safe shape and only on that span — Word's stock prompt is UI chrome, so without the name every gap in a real form would share one key.
- **ONLY AN UNANSWERED FIELD IS A BLANK, and Word says which**: `w:showingPlcHdr` on a control; a FORMTEXT whose result is empty or still its own default. A field somebody filled is their wording and is untouched. A tick box or a dropdown is not a gap for wording.
- **THE TEXT DOES NOT MOVE BY A CHARACTER**: only `html` is wrapped, so the two readers still project the same string (f257's wall, and the one the Word round trip rests on), and marking a field moves the stored projection by NOTHING. A mark begins and ends BETWEEN runs, so it can never cross a bold or italic wrapper.
- **TWO SANITISER PASSES HAD TO LEARN IT, and both were found by driving a real form**: the empty-block sweep deleted the span AND its characters (Word pads an empty box with non-breaking spaces, which do not survive `.trim()`), and the source-indentation sweep took the padding — each leaving `the fee is .` against the plain reader's `the fee is      .`, which is the phantom-change fault. Exempted exactly as `<pre>` is.
- **`report.fields` joins `docxHasStructure`**: a Word FORM is plain paragraphs with grey boxes, so without it the one kind of file this reading exists for stores no body at all.
- **THE PAPER'S OWN SPAN IS MARKED IN PLACE, NEVER UNWRAPPED** (`UP_MADE_ATTR` tells the painter's spans from the paper's); `upNodeHits` is the ONE per-node reading both walks ask, so a field is one unit and its words are never read twice.
Tests: f343 (28 claims, **24 red at the parent**; the four that pass are named CONTROLS), upload-blanks-verify section 8 (10 checks, **all 10 red**). **Noticed, not fixed**: `richToText` collapses a run of non-breaking spaces, so the STORED body's projection differs from the plain reader's on any nbsp-padded document — measured identical at the parent with no field span involved, so pre-existing and far outside this request.

## THE BRIEF TILE PULLS THE BRIEF, AND A FIELD'S NAME IS NOT BOLD (Young ruled 20 Sep 2026)

- *"Brief Witten is supposed to pull the brief side Panel but it does not."* It **scrolled to `#brief-card`** — the card ABOUT the brief rather than the brief itself — so the reader landed beside a heading and still had to find its Open button, which is exactly what the tile's arrow promises to have done for them. **TWO DOORS, ONE ACT**: the tile presses `openCheckPanel(c,'brief')`, the very call the card's own `data-kt-brief="open"` button makes, so the two cannot drift about what "open the brief" means and the panel, its 500px width and its filling all stay `openCheckPanel`'s. The door is drawn only where the brief was WRITTEN (`doorFor` asks `x.ok`), so it can never open on nothing. The obligations tile is untouched — a TAB is the right destination for it.
- *"the names of the fields are currently in bold grey letters. They should not be in bold letters. The answered fields should stay in black bold letters."* `.sec-f-l` gives up its WEIGHT alone (`--w-title` → `--w-body`) and keeps the product's own label treatment — the micro size, the uppercase, the `.09em` — so what carries the eye down the card is the ANSWER. **`.sec-f-v` and `.sec-f-v.is-none` ARE UNTOUCHED** and are pinned as walls: an answered field was already `--w-strong` in the page's own ink and an em-dash already light and grey. **`.ov-reads th` is deliberately NOT swept** — a table head is not a field name, and every other table in the product keeps 700.

## THE REDESIGN — THE REFERENCE'S CLOTHES ON HaTi'S BODY (Young's overnight order, 20–21 Sep 2026; built on branch claude/youthful-hopper-nr3jmm and MERGED TO MAIN on Young's word, 21 Sep 2026)

Built to `prototype/hati-redesign-reference.html` (the owner-approved artifact) against `docs/FEATURE-INVENTORY.md` (1,219 numbered items, Phase 0) and audited in `docs/REDESIGN-AUDIT.md` (Phase 2: none missing, eleven FIXED and named). THE ONE RULE: nothing a person could press disappeared. Where this section disagrees with an older one above, THIS ONE IS THE CODE on that branch.
- **TOKENS**: the teal ramp (#0E5F58 at 600, ink #0B4A45), page grey #F4F6F5, inks #141F1D / #5A6866 / #6B7876, paper #FFFDF9; `--t-body` 13, `--t-page` 20, `--w-title` 600; `--radius` 4 for CONTROLS and `--radius-lg` 8 for CARDS (the paper, its marks and the seal stay 0); `--ctl-h` 30, fields 32; `--btn-edge:var(--rule-strong)`; `--accent-fill` = accent-600 (7.5:1 under white — f238 COMPUTES it). `--font-mono` is for DATA (Geist since 24 Sep 2026 — see THE PROTOTYPE'S FACES; it was JetBrains Mono) — a caption or a title reads `--font-heading`. The census was re-recorded TWICE — in the tokens commit (as the order asked) and again at the end of Phase 2, because the shell, the button face and the library tones moved colours after it and the two inks below moved once more; both times audited as a SET DIFFERENCE first and the whole difference the redesign's own. **THE AMBER AND GREEN INKS ARE ONE STEP DARKER THAN THE REFERENCE'S** (`--st-amber-fg` #A24E07, `--st-green-fg` #1A6F45): the reference's pair measured 4.39:1 on their own wash, under the 4.5 contrast-verify holds 13px text to. `HM_ROW_INKS[0]` reads `--accent-ink-700` (accent-700 by day, accent-400 at night) — the redesign's accent-700 is 1.73:1 on the dark ground and the Home figure was drawn in it. **A SEGMENT LISTENER IS BOUND BY TAG**: `button[data-reg-density]` — the table carries the same attribute and a bare selector bound the density press onto every ROW, so a row press repainted the register over the page it had just opened (negotiations-door-verify caught it; the density, mode and view segments are all bound by tag now).
- **THE SHELL (DECIDE 4 / 1)**: no dark bar. One white 224px rail floor to ceiling (`.rail-mark`, `#rail-ws` names the workspace) in THREE GROUPS — Work (Home · Contracts · Negotiations · Approvals & signing · Obligations · Calendar), Library (Templates · Our standards · Requests · People), Company (Insights · Advice desk · Import · Team & settings; `data-section="settings"` is the fold) — and a 48px light bar in the page column that keeps every control (language, brand swatches, dark, Copilot, alerts, the activity door wearing `#i-clock`, avatar). The live door is a tint, no left rule; below 1440 the rail floats as before. `.ui-btn` is white face + hairline + page ink at label weight; `.ui-btn-plain` keeps the accent ink; `--rl-btn-line` reads `--btn-edge`.
