## NINE OFF FIVE IMAGES (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

Four of the nine were INVISIBLE IN THE SOURCE.
- **RESERVING ONE LINE DOES NOT FIX A CARD'S HEIGHT.** MEASURED: the New agreement cards drew 103px and 121px. The sentence slot was reserved (21 Sep) and doing its job; the NAME wrapped, and a grid stretches every cell in a row to the tallest. **EVERY LINE IS RESERVED NOW** — name, sentence and foot, one each — and the sentence dropped to ONE line, because "not big either" is measured against the artifact's own ~80px card. `naCardTitle` is the one hover carrying the whole of each cut. **Two `.na-door b` rules were folded into one**: a second rule about one element winning by source order is this file's own recorded fault, and it made the first draft of the claim pass against the wrong rule. MEASURED after: six cards, all 86px.
- **A HEAD AND A COUNT ARE TWO FACTS.** *"the open fields does not tell you how many fields are still open"* — a `none` state forced the count to null, so the one tile with a number worth reading drew a steel dash. **ONLY `working` SUPPRESSES A COUNT**, the strip asks the NUMBER before its three old states (mark and tone alike), and the fill tile's number is how many are OPEN where its head says open. The "— N more" tail came off that branch: two numbers about one thing on one tile is what made it unreadable. f341's count claim is REVERSED IN PLACE; what held it — a `none` tile with no number is a dash, never a tick — is held by the strip.
- **THE STANDARDS TILE IS THE THIRD DOOR**, pressing `openCheckPanel(c,'playbook')` — the Checks card's own act. The 20 Sep note ended "on his word"; this is that word.
- **THE RE-WRITTEN BRIEF IS SHOWN.** The Before-you-sign row already re-wrote it and did not open it. `openCheckPanel(c,'brief')` after a run, and **only where one was really written** — `c._brief` holds the OLD brief after a refusal.
- **"THE ONE PLACE EVERY PROPOSAL PASSES THROUGH" WAS NOT ONE.** The 21 Sep cleaner sat inside `pbFitWording`; `it.draft` is the RAW model string and BOTH "Use Copilot's draft" presses file that. It is cleaned in **`rlPlaybookProposals`**, the one producer the rail, the review window and the batch share, on a LOCATED clause only. Reproduced at the parent: `draftHead: "Limitation of Liability. Each Part"`.
- **A MISSING CLAUSE IS ADDED WHOLE; A PRESENT ONE IS ADJUSTED.** Two holes under one report. `pbFitWording` let an UNADDRESSED whole-clause rewrite count as the smallest change (the owner's *"Replaces all 69 words — keeps none"*) — it now refuses one that would lose unquoted blocks, **asking `pbUnquotedLoss`, the WALL's own reading, so the preview, the lead and the filing door cannot disagree** (a seatbelt in practice: an address is found by the same quote that locates the clause). And on an **EDIT** landing `lead` no longer falls back to the library's stand-alone clause — `lead:null`, both card surfaces say `ng_pb_nofit`, and the three named wordings stay as presses that ask. **An ADD landing is byte-identical.**
- **THE COPILOT DRAFT CAN BE READ**: the 120px cap is the RESTING shape and a press drops it (`ceScanPvToggle`); the control is drawn only where the box really overflows, **measured after the paint** (`ceScanFitPv` — `rowsThatFit`'s own idiom), never a character count.
- **A DROPDOWN THAT WEARS HaTi'S OWN CORNERS.** The list a native `<select>` drops is the OS's and no stylesheet reaches inside it. **THE `<select>` STAYS AND IS STILL THE TRUTH**: `selectMenuWire` intercepts the POINTER and draws `.hati-selmenu`; a press writes the value back and fires the select's own `change`, so nothing downstream learns a menu was involved. **THE KEYBOARD IS LEFT ALONE** (arrows, Home, End, type-ahead) — rebuilding those in a div is how a control loses behaviour nobody noticed it had. Mounted on `document.body` at `position:fixed` so the sticky band cannot clip it (the ladder card's own 18 Sep lesson); the press that opens it closes it. **SCOPE, said out loud**: armed on the Contracts filter bar alone; every other `<select>` still drops the system's list until somebody asks.
- **THE HORIZON BAR IS 8px**, the artifact's own, with the TOP moved so the centre line did not — that is where the expiry tick and the notice nip already sit.
Tests: f349 (44 claims, 36 red at the parent; the 8 that pass are named walls and controls), nine-off-five-images-verify (24 checks, 18 red — the parent prints `heights: 121 · 103`, `18/36/18/36/18/18`, the dash, no arrow, no panel, no menu and `14px`), prepare-redlines-verify section 8 (9 checks, 5 red). Re-pointed in place: f319's brief-press claim (**it pinned the whole statement including its `await`**), f341's tone/mark claim and its count claim (REVERSED), f347 (2c)(3d).

## THE COUNTERPARTY LEADS, AND THE WORK ORDER'S FOUR (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

Two orders in one run: the two tables built to renders he approved, then WORKORDER-five-off-five-images.md built after he read it. *"Important that the columns are well spaced so we can see the most of the counterparty name. Balance is key."*

- **THE ROW'S IDENTITY IS TWO LINES, ONE CELL, BOTH SEATS.** `CELL.name` is DELETED (nothing outside the row builder read it); `CELL.counterparty` carries the counterparty on `.reg-title` over the contract's name on `.reg-sub`, and took the family indent, the ↳ arrow and the `data-fam-toggle` control with it. **`.reg-title` / `.reg-sub` KEEP THEIR NAMES AND THEIR DRESS** — they were never "the title" and "the kind", they are the row's IDENTITY LINE and its sub-line, which is the re-pointing white-band-and-tabs 5d/5e already took; only WHICH FACT each carries moved.
- **CONTRACTS LOSES A COLUMN (10), NEGOTIATIONS KEEPS ITS COUNT (8)** — the owner ruled each page by name. `kind` is the Negotiations seat's OWN column (`reg-typecell`, straight right of the counterparty): a round is what that page is about. On Contracts the same two words ride the identity cell's hover. `kindRound` is the ONE reading both spell them through. **`reg-typecell`, never `reg-kind`** — that class is retired and f240 pins its absence BY SUBSTRING, so even the prefix would have tripped it while meaning the opposite.
- **BALANCE, MEASURED**: `[6,26,13,11,7,9,7,13,5,3]` / `[6,26,14,13,9,13,11,8]` (**since 24 Sep 2026 `[6,39,11,7,9,7,13,5,3]` / `[6,39,14,9,13,11,8]` — the stream column went; see SIX OFF FIVE IMAGES**), both 100, the counterparty the widest column on either table (349px at 1440) and the six shared columns cut identically IN PIXELS. The give moved with the fact: it was the title's, and the counterparty now takes both old shares (13+17 → 26); the four points that did not fold went to value stream and expiry, both measured short.
- **`kind` SORTS** (`REG_CMP.kind`, kind then round) and **`name` is a sort with no column**, which `risk` has been since the dropdown was built.
- **A DROPPED CONNECTION IS RETRIED; A REFUSAL IS NOT.** MEASURED from the owner's screenshot: the brief failed while three Copilot calls in the same breath came back — not the key, not the cap, not the network. `anthropicMessages` had NO timeout and retried only a REJECTED MODEL, so a `fetch` throw went straight to the tile. Now `AI_HTTP_TIMEOUT_MS` bounds the wait and a THROW — no answer at all — is retried ONCE after `AI_RETRY_PAUSE_MS`. **A STATUS IS NEVER RETRIED, AND THAT IS THE NARROWING**: a first draft also retried a 5xx and turned five green files red (f132, f133, f234, f275, answer-worklist-verify all say a provider error maps to 502 and the loop stops) — any HTTP status is the provider ANSWERING and already carries its own sentence and mapping. spend books off a RESPONSE so it cannot double. ONE PLACE, EVERY metered call; the streaming twin is left alone.
- **A FAILED READING CARRIES THE PRESS THAT RUNS IT AGAIN** (`doorFor`'s `retry`, asked FIRST) — "Try again in a moment" with no way to try again was the half that was breaking the refusal rule. `triageAndPaint(c,{again:true})` is a PERSON asking and the only thing that lifts the once-per-wording guard.
- **THE BRIEF IS THE FIRST BUTTON BEFORE SIGNING** (`#sc-brief`, the card head's own `runCtl` slot, so no band): drawn in EVERY state — *Write the brief* / *Open the brief* — carrying `data-kt-brief`, so `wireKtBriefCard` (which learned an additive `after`) is the one act and the panel is the same panel. The secondary button, never filled; the cost on the hover; `#i-file`, a sprite symbol that exists. **THE HOLD IS NOT WIDENED** to a brief that is current and merely unread — that would re-spend on every signature. Said out loud, the owner's to rule on.
- **THE COPILOT RAIL NAMES ITS CLAUSE** (`.ce-ah-cl`, through `ceClauseLabel`). **AND IT MAY NOT GROW THE ROW**: MEASURED 44 → 61px, because the name took width and the TABS wrapped, then the LABEL wrapped. `.ce-tabs` and `.ce-ah .sp` take `flex:none`; exactly one thing on that row gives, and it is the name. Back to 44px.
- **THE NEW AGREEMENT POP-UP HAS A GROUND**: `.na-body` takes `--color-bg` and the card edges step to `--rule-strong` (the token `.ui-btn` uses). The cards are NOT tinted — six cards each claiming something, when only the chosen one has a claim. `.na-sec + .na-sec` stands clear and the line-of-business control takes `--field-h`.
- **EVERY DROPDOWN DRAWS HaTi'S OWN LIST**: `selectMenuSweep()` armed ONCE on the body, delegated, so every select the product ever paints is covered with no list to keep in step (the filter bar's own call is RETIRED — two roots would open the menu twice). `SELECT_MENU_SEL` names what stays native (`[multiple]`, `[size]`, `[data-native]`); `selectMenuStandsDown` refuses PORTAL_MODE and a phone, asked AT THE PRESS. The `<select>` is still the record and the keyboard is untouched.
Tests: f350 (18 claims, **0 pass / 18 fail at the parent**), counterparty-leads-verify (32 checks in a real browser, **24 red at the parent**; 2a and 2f are named CONTROLS). The node suite is **8,404 tests, 0 failed**; the browser set is **40 of 129 red against 40 of 128 at the parent** — the one file red here and green there (answer-worklist-verify) came from the 5xx draft above and passes on the tree. Re-pointed in place with the reason beside each: f258 (reversed — the Negotiations seat has one column of its own now), f281 (**an anchor-and-byte-count slice on `sortableTh('name'`**), f240 (the hover moved with the fact), f273 (**four slices pinned `function triageAndPaint(c){` and an options argument broke all four — pin the REGION, not a signature**), contracts-page-verify 1e/17a/18 (**party and stream were pinned by COLUMN INDEX**), flat-rows KIND. **Noticed, not fixed**: flat-rows 2d/2e/2f and its `.reg-day` size exception are red at the parent too.

## THE DEAL CARD IS EVERY CONTRACT'S, AND EVERY FIELD CAN BE TYPED (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

*"the deal card in the overview page is very sales dimensional. The fields there should be ones that are found in most contracts no matter the Type of contract. Also then you try to edit the details, you should be able to edit all fields."* Then, of the three questions put back to him: *"narrow only, only where something is recorded, take all three."* MEASURED first: of the fifteen fields the card drew, FIVE exist only in a supply or distribution agreement, and `Edit these details` opened FOUR boxes against fifteen values.
- **TWO LISTS, AND THE SECOND IS DRAWN ONLY WHERE IT IS ANSWERED.** `OV_DEAL_FIELDS` is on every contract; `OV_ALSO_FIELDS` (the rebate, its tiers, the price review, the rejection window, exclusivity, retention, warranty) is its own shut card, drawn only where `ktAlsoRecorded` finds something — **NO GUESS IS MADE ABOUT THE KIND OF CONTRACT** (a card headed "Terms for a supply agreement" on a record whose type was read wrong is worse than no card). A field in the fixed grid still prints its em-dash; the second group can never be a row of dashes. A number answers only above zero.
- **THREE NEW TERMS, ALL THE OWNER'S WORD**: `confidentiality` and `disputes` are TEXT (the paper's own words — volumeRebate's reasoning), `assignment` is a select (consent · free · prohibited · unclear). **In the extraction schema too** — a field nothing fills is a dash for ever.
- **ONE CELL BUILDER, TWO POSTURES.** `ktFieldCell(c,k,edit)` decides what a cell draws and is where `ktFactReads` is asked; the grid does not change shape when Edit is pressed — a box is drawn in the value's own place. Nineteen ordinary metadata fields are one generic box driven by META_FIELDS' own type (`data-ktm`, one handler, `change` for everything but free text); the four with a home of their own keep `data-kt` (the value strips its separators, an empty notice DELETES). `term` is derived and is never a box. **Typing over a reading wins** — confidence 'high'.
- **THE RECORD KEEPS ENGLISH**: `metaEnName` derives the audit name from the key, so no second list can drift.
- **A SECTION THIS PANE DRAWS MUST BE NAMED IN `sectionWire`'s ROUTER** or its fold flips and the wrong host repaints — measured the hour `also` was added: the head said "1 term" and drew no field. `Value stream` is deliberately NOT on the deal grid though the artifact drew it: The record already carries it, and it is an admin act with its own confirm.
Tests: f351 (17 claims, **16 red at the parent**; the one that passes is a named CONTROL), overview-as-drawn-verify section 9 (7 checks, **6 red at the parent**, which prints `still on the card: Volume rebate, Rebate tiers, Exclusivity` and `5 boxes against 11 cells`). Re-pointed in place: f325 (1)(2), f178 (**an anchor-and-first-brace slice on ktDealFactsHtml**), overview-as-drawn 4a/4b (4b REVERSED — it asked the grid to give the four fields UP, which was right while the edit posture was a second shape; it asks that no label appears twice now, which is stronger).

## BEFORE SIGNING, THE OVERVIEW MARKS THE FIELDS THAT HOLD IT (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

*"before signing, critical fields in the overview page have to be highlighted before signing so that user can go back and fill them in."*
- **CRITICAL IS NOT A NEW LIST.** `signFieldMarks(c)` (js/signcheck.js) TRANSLATES `signReadiness`'s own open rows into the field each is about — a blocker's `key` through `SIGN_FIELD_OF`, a record row's own `field`. It judges nothing, counts nothing of its own, and cannot print a different number from the list it reads. A HOLD OUTRANKS A NOTE on one field: one cell, one mark.
- **NOT WHILE IT IS A DRAFT.** A draft is a form being filled in; an amber *needed to sign* on every blank of a new agreement is the band rule in a new costume. `live` is "this contract is in the phase where a field can be marked", and the Overview reserves its line off `live` ALONE — so within the whole signing phase a mark appearing or clearing moves no pixel, and a draft or a sealed record draws no line at all and is byte-identical to what it was.
- **THE LINE IS OPT-IN**: `sectionFieldHtml` gained a fifth argument the CALLER builds; passing nothing gets the old markup, so the five screens sharing that builder did not move. `.sec-f-n` reserves its height; the cell is dressed by `:has(.sec-f-n.is-hold)`, so the field builder learns no class. Amber has to mean work — a field nobody is waiting on stays quiet.
- **A HOLD IS A DOOR AND IT IS `focusKeyTerms`** (a real `<button>`, `data-ov-fix`), the same act the signing list's *Fix on Overview* presses — two places, one act. A NOTE (the record disagreeing with the paper) is a sentence, never a door: what to do about it is the signing list's own row.
- **THE COUNT RIDES THE HEAD'S OWN CHIP**, not a new line: a shut section still answers, which is the section grammar's rule, and the alternative was a band across the page. Asked ONCE per card, never per cell.
Tests: f352 (16 claims, **15 red at the parent**; the one that passes is a named CONTROL), overview-as-drawn-verify section 10 (6 checks, **4 red at the parent**, which prints `bg rgba(0, 0, 0, 0) · left rule 0px` and `no chip`; 10d and 10f are named CONTROLS). **10e asks the RELATION** — a marked line and an empty one measured in the SAME render — because two renders also measure the value's own text, an em-dash in the body face against a figure in the mono one, which is two real pixels and nothing to do with the mark.

## THE BRIEF IS THE LAST STEP, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

*"write brief should be the last button clicked to verify that a user reads a brief of everything that has been updated before they sign. It should not be the first button in the signing page but last and mandatory."* Then, of the two questions put back: *"follow the gate, every signature."* **THIS REVERSES that same morning's "THE BRIEF IS THE FIRST BUTTON ON THIS CARD"**, whose reasoning is kept where it stands because it is why the control exists at all.
- **A FOURTH STAGE, `sign`, NOT A REORDER.** Putting `read` after `people` would have moved the standards, obligations, record and risk rows too, and those sit where a separate ruling put them. Only the brief moved; `#sc-brief` keeps its id and the card head now draws no control at all.
- **READING IT HOLDS, AND FOLLOWS THE GATE** (`signCheckRowHolds` → `brief-read` is true; `off` holds nothing, which is what off means). It does NOT wait on `current` — it is not a reading the check makes.
- **IT LAPSES WHEN EITHER HALF MOVES.** `briefReadKey` is *when this brief was written* + *when wording was last proposed* — two facts the record answers exactly. **NO SECOND HASHER**: the brief's own inputHash is the SERVER's, and a browser twin would call every brief stale for ever (the trap `signCheckBriefAt` is already kept out of). Each signer's own; `briefMarkRead` is the ONE writer and never back-dates.
- **`signCheckBriefStands` IS ONE READING WITH TWO ASKERS** (the row, and the button's *Read* / *Write* word) — written twice they drift, and the drift is visible: measured, the card offered to WRITE a brief that was already on the screen. **`stale !== true`, NOT `=== false`**: a contract nobody proposed wording on has no dated change to compare with, and asking `=== false` meant the mandatory rule never applied to a contract that went straight to signature. **THE GUARD AGAINST TRAPPING A SIGNATURE IS THE KEY ITSELF** — a brief with no date of its own cannot be stamped against, so it asks for nothing; missing, stale or cut short each keep their own row and their own way forward.
- **READ, IT SETTLES RATHER THAN VANISHING** (an empty stage draws nothing, so without a settled row the one thing the signer was asked to do would leave no trace on the card they did it on).
- **WHAT MOVED WHILE YOU NEGOTIATED** rides the brief panel: `briefMoved` borrows `negoMemo`'s `agreed` list — deterministic, RAW, no model, no spend — capped at 8 with the remainder COUNTED. The foot's *I have read this* is the only act on that panel that writes. **THE WORDS SAY WHAT WAS PROVED**: opened and pressed, never understood, and the trail line says "Read the brief before signing".
Tests: f353 (22 claims, **16 red at the parent**; six are named CONTROLS), signing-flow-verify section 9 (6 checks, **5 red at the parent**, printing `stages: read → people` and `Write the brief · expected Read the brief`; 9e is the CONTROL). **9c pins the RELATION** (the button equals `br_open`'s own words) not the English. The bench itself now reads the brief, guarded — a bench that throws proves nothing about the checks under it. f319 (4b) re-pointed in place.

## SIGNING WITHOUT THE FACTS — THE THREE QUICK FIXES (Young's go, 23 Sep 2026)

Off a review of what a contract could be signed without; the owner took the recommended answers (must/should split, block with an honest one-press answer, anyone with a reason, blanks at the first send AND at signing) and said go on these three. The rest of that plan is NOT built.
- **A VALUE LEFT EMPTY IS NOT "NO MONEY PASSES."** POST /api/templates/:id/contracts writes `'estimated'` for a typed value, `'none'` ONLY for a standard whose category is `nda` (the template answers the silence, as TEMPLATES.ND does), and NOTHING otherwise; the mailroom writes nothing. **The bulk importer still stamps 'none' on purpose** — its review gate reads 'none' as "value not needed" and most imports are already-signed paper. **Stored records are NOT rewritten** (no backfill); the Overview's tick-box is the way back.
- **`metaEffDateOnto(c, m, opts)` (js/metadata.js) IS THE ONE COPY** of a reading's effective date onto `c.fields.effDate` — the field the Overview prints, the record check compares and Copilot's card reads. Fill only; an ISO day or nothing; `overwrite` for the import queue's confirm alone. Four callers: applyMetadata, applyReviewedMeta, migRerunAi, fillKeyTermsFromDocument. **The importer's build is NOT one** (sealed on arrival — an unconfirmed reading would be frozen for good).
- **AN EMPTY BOX IN HaTi'S OWN PAPER IS A READINESS BLOCK, `blanks`** — so the Send screen asks its tick and signBlockers holds, with no new band. `contractBoxesOpen` (js/blanks.js) is contractBlanksOpen narrowed to kind `field`: never `sync` (the counterparty and value have their own rows), never `upload` (their paper is a different question). The row NAMES the boxes (three, then "and N more"), carries `fields`, and the `effective` warning stands down when the start date box is named. `SIGN_BOX_FIELD` marks the one box the Overview owns (effDate). The card's door is chosen at the DRAW: Draft → `data-sc-blanks="docs"`; past Draft with only Overview-owned boxes → `data-sc-fix`; otherwise `data-sc-blanks="nego"` — its own attribute, because `data-sc-nego` is bound with querySelector. **Limit, said out loud**: once a negotiation has stored the wording, an empty box is a dash in the text and is no longer counted.
Tests: f367 (21 claims, **13 red at the parent**; eight are named CONTROLS and WALLS), signing-without-the-facts-verify (26 checks in a real browser, **21 red at the parent**, which prints `Sign as Amina Otieno` over two empty boxes, the draft SENT without a tick, and `Non-monetary` ticked on a skipped value). **FOUR browser files staged a fixture with empty template boxes and are re-pointed IN PLACE** — sign-links-verify, signing-flow-verify (the boxes filled through `contractBlankSet` BEFORE the wording is hashed, for the brief's own reason: every count would be one higher), signers-and-party-verify (the fixture's `material` box), nda-carries-no-money-verify (its heading count asked as the RELATION) — each red here and green at the parent, and green again after.

## WHO IS ON THIS CONTRACT (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

*"when you draft a new agreement, before you create the draft you should have the option to add the other participants to the contract, roles and their access levels. Should you choose to skip this, there should be another door in the contract page."* And of the one decision: *"narrow only."*
- **`js/participants.js` IS A RECORD, NOT A SECOND PERMISSIONS SYSTEM.** `c.participants` is an ordinary field, absent on every record on file; **no route, no server field, no mail** (f354 greps), and it **does not travel** — `buildSharePayload` is an allow-list by construction and who is on OUR side is not the counterparty's business.
- **EVERY ROLE NAMES THE LIST IT FILLS** (`PARTY_ROLES` → `fills`, printed on the row): the signing order, the approval chain, the standing reviewer, a seat at the desk, the three links. A role is an ANSWER, EARLY, to a question the product already asks late.
- **ACCESS NARROWS AND NEVER GRANTS** — four rungs with nothing above `all`; `participantReach` asks the workspace's OWN question (`canAccessFolder`) so the SCREEN can say so on that person's row. A colleague who cannot open the stream is named and told, never quietly added; not-a-colleague-yet is a fact with a way forward.
- **HELD WHILE THE PAPER IS CHOSEN, CLAIMED WHEN IT IS MINTED.** `participantsHold` is in memory; `contractArrived` claims — **BEFORE its own guards** (a contract too empty to read is still one somebody just named three colleagues on) and never on a bulk import. **THE SCREEN'S OWN DISAPPEARANCE IS WHAT DROPS IT** (a MutationObserver on `#modal-root`): Cancel and ✕ are two of five ways out, and hanging the drop off the buttons left people held after an Escape, for the next contract minted anywhere to claim. Create is exempt and spends the hold.
- **`saveSignerPlan` IS STILL THE ONE AUTHORITY**: an EMPTY plan opens on `participantSignerRows`, an arranged one is never touched. Nothing here writes a plan.
- **ONE BUILDER, TWO DOORS** (`participantsPanelHtml` / `participantsWire`, bound once per element): the drafting screen's shut `<details>` and the Overview's own section. The Overview alone carries *Reached* — borrowed from the signing plan and the share cache, with `unknown` where the page has not asked. **`people` IS NAMED IN THE OVERVIEW'S REPAINT ROUTER**, or the fold flips and the wrong host repaints.
- **A BLANK ROW IS A ROW SOMEBODY STARTED**: filtering blanks out of `participantsOf` was the obvious defensive thing and made *Add someone* do nothing at all — the row was minted and hidden by the reading that drew it.
- The namespace is `ppl_`, not `pt_`: payment terms owns `pt_` and fourteen keys collided.
Tests: f354 (32 claims, **27 red at the parent**; five are named WALLS). A missing module READS AS EMPTY there so the file still runs and reports a claim at a time. overview-as-drawn-verify section 11 (5 checks, **all 5 red**) and new-agreement-verify section 8 (6 checks, **4 red**; 8e and 8f are CONTROLS).

## THE SEND SCREEN TAKES SEVERAL PEOPLE (Young ruled 21 Sep 2026)

*"in Door B, you should be able to add multiple people to send the document to."*
- **ONE SEND, ONE ROUND, SEVERAL LINKS.** Everything that happens once still happens once in `doSend` — the round, the handover, leaving Draft, the version capture — and `shareSendExtras` then posts one share per ticked person, AFTER the primary and only inside the branch that landed (a round nobody received is not one to copy to three more people). It advances nothing, hands nothing over and touches no stage; f355 greps it for all five.
- **THE LIST IS THE CONTRACT'S OWN PEOPLE** (`shareMoreRowsHtml` over `participantSendRows`): the other side's, in the role menu's order, each saying which role they have. The primary recipient is NOT offered to themselves (matched by ADDRESS, folded, because that is what a link binds to). Never on the `history` kind — that one travels to a colleague.
- **ONE AUDIT LINE PER ADDRESS**, because a link is a fact about one address; a refusal is COUNTED and SAID and cannot undo a round that has gone.
- **ONE PURPOSE FOR THE WHOLE SEND.** A purpose per row would mean a signing link and an advise link in one press; it is a different feature and the owner has not ruled on it. Pinned as an absence.
- **THE TWO BUILDERS LIVE IN js/participants.js**, not js/core.js: they are about the people, not the dialog, and that is what makes them drivable on a stage with no core.
Tests: f355 (14 claims, **13 red at the parent**; the one that passes is a named WALL), share-recipient-verify's own section (4 checks, **all 4 red at the parent**, which prints `not drawn`). The bench is guarded so the file still runs on a build without the list.

## THE BOOK IS WALKED ONCE, NOT ONCE PER CONTRACT (the performance audit, 22 Sep 2026)

MEASURED in a real browser at 3,000 contracts before a line moved: drawing Home once touched **108 MILLION items in lists**, 99.5% of them from ONE reading. Home 1,035 ms · Contracts 1,293 · Calendar 1,197 · Obligations 1,025 · Insights 958. After: **88 · 157 · 115 · 83 · 115**.

- **`familyChildren` IS A MAP BUILT ONCE** (js/family.js). It scanned the whole book on every call and `effectiveExpiry` asks it about twelve times per contract. **THE GUARD IS THREE O(1) FACTS** — same array, same length, same stamp — plus `familyIndexDirty()`, which every writer of `parentId` and every in-place swap of a contract object must raise. **A GUARD THAT WALKS THE BOOK IS NOT A GUARD**: the first build verified the index with a per-call checksum over every parentId and made Home *slower* (1,035 → 3,442 ms). The empty answer is one shared FROZEN array. f356 (4) is the net on a future writer.
- **ONE COUNT PER PAINT** (`navCounts`, js/app.js): the rail, Home's tile and the phone's bar each asked `negoNeedsYouTotal` for themselves — one navigation walked the book FOUR times for one figure. The memo is dropped on a **microtask**, so it cannot outlive the turn it was computed in and no caller has to remember to clear it. **THE CYCLE WALL**: the first build asked `approvalsDoorCount`, which draws through `hmDashSlices`, which asks `navCounts` — and the memo was written only at the END, so every level recomputed: **1,132 walks of the book for one paint of Home against 3 before.** `navCounts` now asks nothing that draws a screen, and `_navBusy` refuses a re-entrant call rather than recomputing. Every reader keeps its own fallback — these modules load apart.
- **ONE LOOK-UP PER TICK**: `obligationSurfacesChanged` searched the whole book twice for the same contract before asking any surface whether it was even mounted.
- **THE LIST DECORATES ITSELF FROM ITS OWN IDS**: the contracts route read the WHOLE briefs table and the WHOLE renewal_advice table — parsing the JSON of every renewal row in the workspace — to decorate one page of at most 200. `pageIds` + a bounded `IN (?,?,…)`.
- **THE INSTRUMENT**: `test/chromium/_audit/perf*.js` time a real browser at 3,000 contracts. A REALM MISMATCH is what makes `deepStrictEqual` fail on two identical lists — an array born inside jsdom has that realm's `Array.prototype`; compare joined strings. And **the parent baseline is a worktree** (`git worktree add -f <dir> origin/main`), never an assertion.
Tests: f356 (19 claims, 8 red at the parent), f357 (16 claims, 12 red).

## EIGHT HOLES IN THE LIFECYCLE (the process-flow audit, 22 Sep 2026)

Where the product lost track between one screen and the next. Each is a READING the screens already had the facts for.

- **(1) FOUR PLACES AN ADDRESS CAN BE TYPED** and nothing compared them. `contractAddressBook(c, shares)` (js/participants.js) folds the signing route, the people list, the record and the last link into rows with ONE marked `willUse`; `ADDR_SOURCES` is the order, written down once and the send screen's own. It draws NOTHING under two rows — one address is not a disagreement. `shareModalPrefill` gained the people source between route and record.
- **(2) A ROUND LANDS AND NOTHING RE-READS.** `readingStale(c, kind)` (js/signcheck.js) over brief · playbook · obligations. **THREE ANSWERS, and the third is "we do not know"** (never read is not out of date; nothing proposed is nothing to compare with). **THE PLAYBOOK KEEPS ITS OWN AUTHORITY** — `playbookStale` is asked first, because a hash beats a date. The wording-moved date is BORROWED (`signCheckBriefAt`), never computed again — a second hasher drifts. It re-runs nothing and spends nothing.
- **(3) LANES WAITED FOR SOMEBODY TO OPEN A PAGE.** `intakeLaneSweep` on a beat, armed at sign-in beside `loadIntake`; it reads `intakeLanes()` BEFORE loading anything, so a workspace with no lanes costs nothing. **A RULE THAT FIRES WITHOUT A HUMAN MAY NOT MOVE THE HUMAN**: `createFromTemplate(tid, {quiet:true})` mints without stealing the reader's page or toasting at them.
- **(4) A NOTICE DRAFTED BUT NEVER RECORDED.** `noticeMarkServed` is the ONE writer of `c.notice` (absent on every record on file); a served notice IS the renewal decision, so `renewalDecisionOf` leads with it and the chase stops. **IT CLAIMS NOTHING HaTi SAW** — the trail says "Recorded by a person; HaTi did not send it." **AND `todayStr()` IS NOT AN ISO DAY** — see below.
- **(5) EIGHT SWITCHES AND NO SUMMARY.** `stPersonSays(u)` BORROWS every line from the switch below it and decides nothing; an admin is said ONCE, not six times.
- **(6) FOUR END STATES, NO COMPARISON.** `END_STATES` / `endStateSays` is the one place those three sentences live, printed on the hover by the Contracts row and the contract's own menu. **A CHOOSER WAS NOT BUILT, DELIBERATELY** — Young ruled Hold onto that Contracts row BY NAME on 19 Sep, over a drawing, because reaching it meant opening the contract first; folding the three into one door takes that back. The words were the half that was missing. f358 (6d) pins the absence.
- **(7) THE GUEST HAS ONLY A LINK — ALREADY BUILT.** `share_otp`, `POST /api/shares/:token/otp`, the address is the STORED one, and the server refuses an unverified signature; skippable only where `EMAIL_ON()` is false. The audit had generalised a note about a guest's first SEND to signing. f358 (7e) holds there to exactly one set of code routes.
- **(8) TWO SCREENS, DIFFERENT EDITING RULES.** `docReadOnlyHint` on the Document tab: it ARRIVES WHEN NEEDED and is not a band, asks the product's OWN reading (`docFillable`), stands down on a control, a real box or a highlight, is said once every `DOC_HINT_MS` rather than on every click, and is bound once per canvas.
Tests: f358 (43 claims, 29 red at the parent).

### AND `todayStr()` IS A DISPLAY STRING — PAID FOR THREE TIMES (item 4's net found it; the risk scan had it too)

`todayStr()` reads like "today" and returns **"22 Sep 2026"**. Comparing it with an ISO day compares `"2"` with `"0"`, so `day > today` answers false for every date in the future: the wall refusing a notice served tomorrow **never refused anything**, and the date box opened EMPTY with no maximum, because a date input drops a value it cannot read. **THE CALENDAR PAID FOR THIS EXACT TRAP** and wrote it down beside `calToday`, which is the ONE reading — asked through `window` (this file draws on stages with no calendar) with the same LOCAL arithmetic as its fallback, never UTC. **`todayISO()` (js/core.js, published, written DIRECTLY BELOW `todayStr`) IS THE ONE READING**, and three files ask it: the served-notice wall, the calendar's `calToday`, and the RISK SCAN. Each keeps the same LOCAL arithmetic as its own fallback, because none of them is on every stage; `toISOString()` is never the answer — it puts today on yesterday for every reader west of Greenwich after their afternoon.

**THE RISK SCAN PAID IT THE THIRD TIME (Young asked for it 22 Sep 2026).** `runScan` stamped `c.scan.on` with `todayStr()`, so `ktDayDot` — which correctly refuses anything that is not an ISO day — printed an em-dash in the Overview's *What Copilot read* row **on every record ever scanned**. `on` has ONE writer and ONE reader, and they had never agreed. **THE CHECK THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT WAS ASSERTING THE DEFECT**: f328 pinned the source line `on:(typeof todayStr==='function')?todayStr()` under the message *"and `on` is the ISO day a formatter can read"* — the assertion and its own message contradicted each other. **A CHECK THAT READS THE SOURCE LINE CANNOT SEE THE VALUE**: it is reversed in place and now EVALUATES the `on:` expression and hands the result to `ktDayDot`'s own shape. Its first draft bound only `todayISO`, so `typeof todayStr` answered 'undefined', the expression took its ISO fallback and the claim PASSED at the parent — **the mirror-world trap again; both names are bound with their real bodies now.**

**NO BACKFILL, and the cost is said out loud**: a contract scanned before this keeps a display string in `on` and still prints an em-dash, because `at` beside it is a locale sentence and turning it into a day would be guessing. Scanning again is one press. `todayStr` is right for `c.lastAction`, which is a display field, and that is every other caller. Tests: f358 (4d2, re-pointed to the relation), f328 (three claims, all red at the parent — one prints `the reader refused what the scan wrote: "22 Sept 2026"`).

## WHO THE AGREEMENT IS BETWEEN — THE MULTI-PARTY SYSTEM (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026)

*"Start from the latest main then implement the multi party system accordingly then Merge to main."* Built to the artifact **Comments and Parties, Full Pages**; the record is WORKORDER-multi-party.md.
- **`js/parties.js` IS THE ONE READING AND THE ONE WRITER**, and the design decision everything rests on is MEASURED: `.counterparty` is read **432 times across 40 files**. So **`c.counterparty` IS THE FIRST OUTSIDE PARTY'S NAME, ALWAYS** — `partiesSet` keeps it so in the same breath — and `contractParties` DERIVES the pair every contract has always had where nothing is stored. **NO MIGRATION, and every one of those 432 readings keeps answering what it answered yesterday.** `c.parties` is absent on every record on file.
- **EVERY CONTROL IS DRAWN ONLY WHERE `partiesMulti`**: the register's `+N`, the fact row's `+N`, the send screen's party box, the redline row's who-has-answered line, the signing order's step headings. An ordinary two-party contract is byte-identical on every one of them, and f359's CONTROL is the measurement.
- **PEOPLE ARE NOT PARTIES.** js/participants.js names HUMANS and what each may see; this names LEGAL ENTITIES on the paper. Two namespaces (`ppl_` / `py_`), two builds, neither reads the other. They are joined at one point in one direction: a signer row and a share may carry a `partyId`.
- **THREE INVOLVEMENTS** (`PARTY_INVOLVEMENT`): negotiate · sign · none. **OUR OWN SIDE ALWAYS NEGOTIATES AND SIGNS** whatever a hand-edited record says. A party that does not execute gets **no ruled line on the paper** — a line nobody may sign is a line a reader will wait at.
- **ONE DECISION PER NEGOTIATING PARTY**: `ch.decisions` (absent on file), `negoPartyVerdicts` the ONE reading. **D7 — one refusal is a refusal** and outranks any number of acceptances; accepted means accepted by ALL, so the agreed wording does not move before that (it falls out of `ch.status` staying pending, never a second rule). The deciding party is a fact about the **LINK** (`respPartyId`), never the response, and **never matched by address** — two companies can share a domain.
- **THE ROUTE RUNS IN STEPS**: `signStepOf` derives the step from `order`, so a route with nothing stored is the strict queue it has always been, to the row. Steps are RENUMBERED with no gaps; an EMPTY step is not a complete one. **THE SERVER IS THE WALL** — `srvSignStep` widens `signerTurn` to "an earlier STEP", `releaseNextSignerLink` releases EVERY link in a step, and `srvContractParties` refuses a link for a party the record does not hold or that only signs (D8).
- **REFUSAL 5 WAS LIFTED BY NAME** (the artifact): two doors onto naming a party, ONE writer under both.
- **A FACT MOVED IS A NOTE MOVED** — the lesson, and it is **REVERSED IN PLACE** the next day. For one day the parties block sat inside The record, that card gave up its counterparty and email cells, and the line saying the record and the paper disagree about the name went silently with the cell (f352 (3) caught it). With the block in a section of its own the ruled twelve are whole again and the note is back on the grid's own cell, where `ovFieldMarkOf` already puts it: LESS code, not more. `.py-item` is GONE.
- **AND IT IS ITS OWN NAMED SECTION, OPEN** (Young reported it 22 Sep 2026: *"i do not see the changes in the overview page"*). Built at the top of The record — the artifact's own drawing — and **The record OPENS SHUT, and a shut `sectionHtml` draws no body at all**, a fact this file already records under THIRTEEN OFF A MORNING. So MEASURED on a real page at the merge commit: `#kt-parties` was not hidden, it was **not in the document**, and the only sign of the whole build was the `+1` on the fact row. **MY OWN RENDER OF THE ARTIFACT DREW THAT CARD OPEN**, which is why it looked right. The stack is deal · also · **parties** · record · people; the section carries the name, the count, the lock word and `+ Add a party`, and the block is drawn **`bare`** (`#family-section`'s own idiom) so none of it is said twice. `sectionWire`'s router NAMES `parties`, or the fold is dead. **IT IS THE ONE CONTROL NOT GATED ON `partiesMulti`** — "who is this between" is not a multi-party question, and a two-party contract is what the owner went looking at.
- Not built, said out loud: the guarantor's own page, a purpose per recipient, the evidence pack's party section, a party editor on the phone.
- **AND THE WORD THE PAPER USES IS A CHOICE (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026: *"contract type should be a drop down of choices"*, over the Edit party window)**: that box was labelled **`ov_f_type`** — the Overview's label for `metadata.contractType`, the KIND of agreement — while holding the word THIS paper uses for THIS party, which is what its own placeholder had always said. Both halves are fixed: the label is `py_role` ("What the paper calls them") and the box is a `<select>`. **IT IS THE VALUE STREAM PICKER'S OWN MECHANISM, not a second idea of one** — `PARTY_ROLE_WORDS` are eighteen KEYS (so the list is offered in the READER's language; what is STORED is the paper's own word and is never translated), `partyRoleOptions(current)` keeps a stored word that is off the list and puts it first, and the last option is a sentinel that opens `promptNewName`, whose `make` is the word itself because a paper's word belongs to no store. **`selectMenuSweep` dresses it by construction**, so nothing here builds a dropdown. The sentinel can never reach the record (`roleV` at Save is the wall). One door: `openPartyEditor` is the only place a party's role is typed, and the phone has no party editor. Tests: f359 (11) (8 claims, **7 red at the parent**), overview-as-drawn-verify 13 (6 checks driven with a real pointer, **all 6 red at the parent**, which prints `<INPUT> · "Contract type"`).

Tests: f359 (57 claims, **54 red at the parent**; the three that pass are two named WALLS and one named CONTROL), overview-as-drawn-verify 12 (7 checks in a real browser, **all 7 red at the merge commit**, which prints `no #kt-parties at all`). **THE BENCH MUST RELOAD THE PAGE FIRST**: the fold is per sitting and in memory, so by section 12 The record was already open from section 1 and the first draft of 12a PASSED at the parent — it measured the fault away. And a missing section reports `-1`, never `0`, or 12f passes on a page with no parties on it.

## THE RUNWAY — THE DECISION CARD IS A LINE OF TIME (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026)

**DORMANT SINCE 24 SEP 2026**: the card this rail sat on LEFT HOME, and came back on 25 Sep with two rows and WITHOUT the rail (see THE MAP ON HOME). `js/runway.js` is kept whole and f363 still pins it; nothing draws it.

*"The home page looks boring with mostly words"*, then the question that decided the shape: *"how does the app measure the dates of redline answers that are pending? Redlines usually do not have due dates."* **HE IS RIGHT, AND NOTHING ON THAT CARD WAS EVER DUE.** What HaTi holds for a pending redline is `createdAt` on the other side's change — the day they asked — and `deskCfg().staleDays`, an admin setting (5 WORKING days). The card's "37 days" was elapsed working days sitting in the same column as "in 9 days", which is calendar days REMAINING.
- **`js/runway.js` IS THE READING**: no route, no store, no field, no spend (f363 greps). `rwClock(it)` turns the ONE raw date a row carries into a side and a distance; `rwSplit` is the one pass every figure comes off; `rwX` places it. **ONE UNIT, AND IT IS CALENDAR DAYS** on both sides — the working-day count is the threshold's, never the distance's.
- **THE LEFT-HAND CLOCK STARTS WHERE THE STANDARD RAN OUT**, never where they asked. `rwAddWorkingDays` is the exact inverse of desk.js's own walk and **f363 (3) pins the PAIR, never either number**. The standard is READ (`deskCfg`, already published), never re-invented.
- **A ROW WITH NO DATE IS COUNTED, NEVER PLOTTED** — a signature, an undated review, a contract simply sitting, a join request. They are the column beside the rail; each count is a door through the page's own `data-hm-go` (`rwnone:<kind>` → `regShowOnly`). Every dot is a door too (`data-sel`).
- **THE HOME PAGE HANDS IT RAW FACTS**: six sources, six `rw:{…}` objects (`since` · `due` · `left` · `sat`) and an `rk` kind — never the presented `tag`, which is already through i18t.
- **ONE NUMBER FOR ONE THING**: the quiet desk's row tag is rewritten to the rail's own reading, because the flag's working-day count beside a dot placed in calendar days read 42 against 53 on one card. The desk's flag elsewhere keeps its own word.
- **NOTHING PLOTTED, NOTHING DRAWN** — the card is then exactly what it was. **NO EXPLAINER BAND** (the owner excluded it by name): the end labels carry the two directions and the left one carries the standard; where it is SET rides the hover.
- Not built, said out loud: the phone (it draws `mNeedsYou`).
Tests: f363 (25 claims, **24 red at the parent**; the one pass is a named WALL), runway-and-xray-verify.

## X-RAY — THE SWITCH'S THIRD POSITION (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026)

*"Build the X-ray next to plain English."* The paper, a MAP of it down the margin, and everything already known about the clause in front of you.
- **IT SPENDS NOTHING.** Every section is a reading already paid for: the plain-English entry out of `_readings` (paired by `docReadAnchors`, never a second reading), the open scan findings, the playbook verdicts, and `ladderRungs`. No route, no store, no field (f364 greps).
- **ONE STORE, THREE VALUES.** `DOC_READ_KEY` keeps its key and `'1'` still means Plain English; `docViewMode()` / `docViewSet()` are the readings and **`docReadOn()` keeps its name AND its answer** (`docViewMode()==='plain'`), which is what stops its callers drifting.
- **THE MAP BELONGS IN A MARGIN, AND THE MARGIN IS GREY.** `.doc-xr-spine` is absolutely positioned inside `#doc-paper-col` (which gained `position:relative` and an id, and NOTHING else), so nothing in the layout changes and **the contract does not move by a pixel** — measured inside the document, identical before and after. Mounted only where the measured grey holds it (`DOC_XRAY_SPINE_W` 28 + `DOC_XRAY_SPINE_GAP` 8); otherwise the panel still works and the map is not drawn.
- **PLACEMENT REFUSES RATHER THAN GUESSES**: `docXrayPlace` is containment with `DOC_XRAY_QUOTE_MIN` under it — `rlPbFindClause`'s own rule in a second costume. A verdict that MATCHES is not a mark. The tone is the WORST mark, never the first.
- **ONE WALK**: `docXrayRows` borrows `docReadSheet`, so both positions of the switch see the same clauses. The ladder section is drawn only where the paper carries a stamped clause id — template paper has none, and "never argued" there would be a claim.
- **THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING**: `docXrayPaint` runs AFTER `docReadPaint` at every site that changes the mode or the tab, because that painter hands `#doc-right` back whenever the edition is off. The pick handler is the one exception and f364 (6) names it.
- **THE PANEL IS THE EDITION'S OWN WHITE CARD** (Young ruled it the same day: *"the right hand side of the card should be on a white card just like plain English"*) — which is the **10 Sep 2026 ruling** (*"let the plain english also sit in a white card and not the grey background"*) reaching its second layer. **ONE RULE, `#doc-read,#doc-xray`, never a copy**: the switch draws both into one slot, and five declarations written twice drift the next time either is retuned. The card gives no top padding, so `.doc-xr-head` carries the caption row's own 11px and the two columns start level. f364 (5) pins the shared selector AND the absence of a private one; the browser file asks it as a RELATION — both layers resolved live and required to be equal.
- **NO EXPLAINER BAND** (the owner excluded it): asserted as an absence over the paper.
- Not built: the negotiate page, the counterparty's page, the phone, exports, the Signing tab — Plain English's own walls.
Tests: f364 (30 claims, **30 red at the parent**), runway-and-xray-verify (31 checks in a real browser, **4 of 32 at the parent and all four named CONTROLS**). plain-english-verify 1e/1k/1l re-pointed in place — they pinned "exactly TWO buttons", which the owner's own request made false; the substance of each is untouched.

### FORMAT A — THE MAP IS GRADED, AND THE READING IS HANDED OVER (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026)

Two screenshots off his iPad, three formats drawn, and he picked **A** (the graded map) and **D1** (a hairline between every pair). Both faults were MEASURED in a browser before a line moved, on a contract carrying a real thirteen-clause reading.
- **THE X-RAY NEVER ONCE SHOWED A READING.** `docReadAnchors(c, items)` takes the entries as its SECOND argument and the panel passed none, so it walked `(items||[])`, found nothing, and honestly printed *"No reading of this clause yet"* — on every clause of every contract. MEASURED: the Plain English column paired **13**, this paired **0**. **THE CHECK THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT WAS ASSERTING THE DEFECT** (f364 (4) required `docReadAnchors(c)`), because the browser file opened a contract with no reading and asked whether the panel said "no reading" — it passed either way. Both are reversed in place.
- **A MISSING MEMORY IS "WE DO NOT KNOW", NEVER "IT MOVED".** `c._readSig` has ONE writer, inside `docReadRun`, and is neither stored nor transport — so it is absent on every page load while `c._readings` rides every GET. `c._readSig!==sig` read that absence as a change, so the first press after every load **bought a reading it already had**, and a refusal of that purchase returned BEFORE `docViewSet`: *"nothing happens"*. `const moved=!!(sig&&c._readSig&&c._readSig!==sig)` is `docReadPaint`'s own three-part reading, 250 lines down, which had been right all along — two readings of one fact, disagreeing. A refused re-read now stands the press down ONLY where `!docReadItems(c).length`.
- **THREE GRADES, AND EACH IS A COLOUR THIS PRODUCT ALREADY USES FOR THAT MEANING.** `XR_GRADES` = ruby · amber · steel, **the order IS the rank** and `docXrayTone` walks it, so a clause wears its WORST mark by construction. `XR_SEV_GRADE` maps the scan's own high/med/low and is the ONLY thing that hands out ruby. A bare segment is **not "safe"** — `xr_look_none` names no reader (it listed two while there were three) and says silence is not safety.
- **THE BRIEF REACHES THE X-RAY, PLACED BY ITS OWN QUOTE.** `docXrayBriefWatch` is the ONE reading of `_brief.data.watchouts`, with two askers. **THE `unusual` LIST IS NOT A CLAUSE MARK** (REVERSED for an unusual term that carries a quote, 22 Sep 2026 — see THE UPLOADED CONTRACT, FIXED) and the absence is load-bearing: it carries a sentence and no wording (`required:['point']` on the watchouts, a bare string for unusual), so pinning it would be the product guessing which clause a sentence is about. `docXrayWide(c,rows)` said it about the whole contract instead (kept whole with no caller since 25 Sep 2026), in steel, together with any watchout whose wording did not land — and drops one that DID, so nothing is said twice. `docXrayMarkHtml` is the ONE builder for both lists and **every mark names its source** (`xr_m_scan` · `xr_m_pb` · `xr_m_brief` · `xr_m_odd`).
- **D1**: `.doc-read-seg button + button` takes a 1px left edge in `--accent-ink` at 45% — no new token, (0,1,2) against the base rule's (0,1,1) so it wins on weight. **BETWEEN EVERY PAIR, not only the unlit ones**: dropping it beside the filled half is tidier at rest and MOVES THE FURNITURE as the lit half moves. MEASURED: group still 30px, `--ctl-h` still 30.
- Still no route, no store, no field, no spend; the contract measured unmoved (ink 266.5 / sheet 43 / 735, before and after).
Tests: f364 section 9 (24 claims, **17 of 51 red at the parent**; the four that pass are named WALLS and CONTROLS), runway-and-xray-verify section 7 (12 checks staging a REAL reading, brief and scan — **10 red at the parent**, which prints the owner's screenshot back: `"No reading of this clause yet…"`, `{"asked":1,"mode":"paper","hidden":true,"notes":0}` and `[0,0,0]`). Re-pointed in place: f364 (3) and (4) (the second REVERSED — it pinned the defect), f277's press claim (**its own words already said "MOVED" and the guard it pinned did not**).

## THE UPLOADED CONTRACT, FIXED — A TO L (Young's go, 22 Sep 2026, over "HaTi — The Uploaded Contract, Fixed")

A Maersk SaaS agreement uploaded as Word: clauses drawn as bold headings, Plain English white, the X-ray grey with the scan dropped, an obligations door onto an empty tab, three Overview cards out of line.
- **A HEADING STYLE IS NOT ALWAYS A HEADING.** `docxStyledIsWording(html,text)` (js/docx.js, both hosts) — a bold run-in title then wording, a sentence/list ending (`. ; : ,` or "; and/or") at ≥ `DOCX_HEAD_PUNCT_MIN` 4 words, or > `DOCX_HEAD_MAX_WORDS` 20 words → a numbered `<p>` stepped by the file's OWN outline level (before the title shift). Text byte-identical; `report.demoted` counts. MEASURED on the file: 310 headings → 105. Existing uploads: Re-read document.
- **PLAIN ENGLISH**: a heading-only row that is wording by the same measure is sent WITH its words (`docReadSheet`) — covers every contract uploaded before the reader changed. A bold title split across Word runs is ONE lead-in (`_docReadBoldLeadLoose`), and may start right after the clause's number. A short reading (partial or over) is said in the column HEAD (`.doc-read-short`, the moved line's slot and door), not only at its foot a thousand lines down.
- **THE X-RAY**: a quote is matched against the clause's name AND wording (`docXrayRowText`); all four sources (scan, playbook, brief watchouts, brief unusual) go through one shape each (`_xrScanMark`/`_xrPbMark`/`_xrBriefMark`/`_xrOddMark`); an item that lands nowhere is in About this contract, worst first — **never dropped** (**SUPERSEDED 25 Sep 2026: About this contract left the X-ray — see WHO DOES WHAT below**). A MATCHING verdict ('aligned' from the route, 'ok' older) is no mark. **REVERSES** "the unusual list is not a clause mark": an unusual term WITH a quote places by containment; one without stays contract-level. About this contract was `.doc-xr-sec.is-wide`, a very light red mixed from `--st-ruby-bg` and the surface (the shade is Worth a look's since 25 Sep 2026).
- **EVERY CONCERN SAYS WHY**: the brief schema REQUIRES `why` on watchouts and unusual terms, unusual is `{point,why,quote}` (older briefs: bare strings, read both ways by the X-ray and `renderBriefSection`); `max_tokens` 6000 by the schema's arithmetic. Printed as "Why it matters:" (`xr_why`). A playbook mark's reason is your own standard (`xr_pb_why`). Old briefs need one Rewrite.
- **THE OBLIGATIONS TILE FOLLOWS THE TAB**: `obTileOpensReview(c)` — tab empty and a reading held → `runFindObligations` (the held list, never a second paid scan); otherwise the tab.
- **OVERVIEW**: Who else's body is wrapped in `.sec-body` (sectionHtml leaves the inset to its caller); `.ov-reads` margin-top 14px; `.py-rows > .py-row:first-child{border-top:0}` (`.py-item` is gone, so the old rule reached nothing).
Tests: f366 (26 claims, 24 red at the parent), uploaded-contract-fixed-verify (19 checks, 14 red at the parent); f364 re-pointed in place (the unusual wall REVERSED).

## WHO DOES WHAT — THE X-RAY'S THIRD SECTION, AND ONE LIGHT-RED AREA (Young ruled 25 Sep 2026)

*"…this 'about contract x' portion should be excluded from the x-ray so there is only one red highlighted area which is the worth a look area"*; then, over three drawn options (artifact "X-ray Panel Options"), *"build it using your recommendation of who does what"*.
- **ONE LIGHT-RED AREA**: Worth a look is `.doc-xr-sec.is-look`, and `.has` (About this contract's old shade: ruby wash 55% + surface) only while it HOLDS a mark — an empty list stays white. **About this contract is not drawn**; `docXrayWide` is kept whole with NO caller (two lines put it back); its items stay on the Brief, the Risk scan and the Playbook check. `xr_sec_wide` and `.is-wide` are STALE.
- **`docXrayWho(c, rows, i)` READS, `docXrayWhoHtml` DRAWS AND COMPUTES NOTHING**: one line per SENTENCE of the picked clause that makes a side do something (DOC_DUTY_RE, borrowed as `new RegExp(DOC_DUTY_RE.source,'i')`, never copied), lets it (`XR_RIGHT_RE`: may / is entitled to + an action verb; "may not" is must-not) or caps a liability (`XR_LIMIT_RE`; "not limited to" is not one). "Neither party shall" is must-not; the earliest phrase decides; English drafting only.
- **WHO, IN THIS ORDER**: words naming both sides in the SUBJECT (`XR_BOTH_RE`) → both (the tab records one owner and cannot say both); else an obligation on the tab whose quote sits in the sentence (`xrPlaceObligations`, containment) → its owner; else the party's own name (`xrPartyNames`: contractParties' names and roles, plus the short name the paper defines in brackets after a legal name, stopping at the next party's) → you/them; else UNCLEAR, whole sentence shown. A tab-vouched sentence with no duty verb reads its subject off the modal (`XR_MODAL_RE`).
- Sorted you · them · both · unclear (`XR_WD_SIDES`), paper order inside; the cite is the paragraph's own number (`XR_CITE_RE`); a line is cut at `XR_WD_WORDS` 32 with the whole sentence on the hover; `XR_WD_MAX` 12 lines, then "and N more", the head counting all. A tab item ticks its line with its day; the Obligations-tab door is drawn only then.
- **ONE LISTENER, NO SECOND FINDER**: a line press is `scrollToQuote` (the risk scan's own); the door is `roomGoTab(getContract(c.id),'oblig')`, looked up LIVE — both inside `docXrayWire`'s one delegated listener.
- Chips are literal keys in closures (`XR_WD_CHIPS` / `XR_WD_BAL`), so the both-books net sees every key and the language is read at the draw; **no amber on a chip** (amber means worth a look here); the lines share columns by **subgrid** (MEASURED 30px apart without). Scales with `--doc-scale`. No route, store, field or spend; READING MUST NOT WRITE; the contract does not move.
A reading that throws draws NO section (logged) — never the "found nothing" line, which is a claim.
Tests: f384 (40 claims, 38 red at the parent — two named walls), xray-who-does-what-verify (21 checks on a real Word upload, 16 red at the parent; the stage and 1b/5a/6 are CONTROLS). Re-pointed in place: f364 (three claims), f366 D/G/H, runway-and-xray-verify 7e–7g, uploaded-contract-fixed-verify 2f/2g.

## FOUR FIXES OFF ONE LONG CONTRACT (Young's order, 23 Sep 2026)

- **A READING'S ANSWER IS CHECKED BEFORE IT IS FILED.** `js/metaclean.js` (module AND window, required by the server) — `metaUnleak(m, keys)` cuts a field at the model's own call syntax (`META_SEAM_RE`), moves an answer written after a seam under ANOTHER known field's name into that field only where it is empty, drops anything else, and files an answer that only says the contract is silent (`META_ABSENT_RE`) as silence. Returns a copy. Asked at the route (`/api/ai/extract`, against its own schema's keys — the wall), at `applyMetadata`, and on the way in (`_repairMetadata` in `migrateContract` and after `ensureFull`'s assign — narrow, persisted only on the next ordinary save). The extract ceiling is 3,000 tokens (nineteen fields with spans).
- **PLAIN ENGLISH PAGES ARE SIZED BY THEIR WORDS.** A page closes at `READ_PAGE_CHARS` (14,000) as well as `READ_PAGE` (60) clauses; the whole-edition `aiDocChars` ceiling is GONE (each page is its own call); `READ_MAX_PAGES` 40 is a runaway guard. A page cut short is asked again in two halves, ONCE (`askAll`). An edition that could not finish lands and is held (`docReadHeld` reads `over`), so its head says so instead of "Copilot found nothing to say".
- **THE X-RAY MAP SCROLLS** (**REVERSED IN PART 24 Sep 2026**: blocks SHARE the strip — `--xr-w` is docXraySegH, `XR_SEG_MIN` the floor — so the strand runs to the bottom and scrolls only past the floor; see SIX OFF FIVE IMAGES). A block is a HEIGHT, `docXraySegH(words)` between `XR_SEG_MIN` 16 and `XR_SEG_MAX` 44, never a share of the screen; `.doc-xr-spine` scrolls on its own with the scrollbar hidden; `docXrayFollow` marks the clause at the top of the sheet (`.is-here`, an accent rule) and keeps it in view, bound once on `#doc-scroll`; a repaint keeps the map's place.
- **DRAFT NEW AGREEMENT IS TWO DOORS.** `openNewAgreement()` with NO options (a person pressing the button) opens `openNewDoors` — Draft from HaTi · Upload a contract; every caller with options goes straight to drafting. The drafting door is `openNewAgreement({door:true})`, the upload door `openUploadModal` — one act each. `PAGE_ACTIONS.register` is `['cohort','new']`; the separate Upload button is gone. The doors' attribute is `data-nd-door` (`na-door` is a retired name f360 walls). The drafting screen's own "Upload it" link and its lead "Received a contract instead?" are GONE (Young ruled 23 Sep 2026) — `na_upload` / `na_received` inert in both books; Import stays.
Tests: f367 (42 claims, 16 red at the parent), long-contract-verify (14 checks); f277 (6), f315 (5) and five browser files re-pointed in place.

## NINE OFF THE OWNER'S LIST (Young's go, 23 Sep 2026)

- **PLAIN ENGLISH: THE ECHO IS A SENSE CHECK, NOT A SPELLING TEST** (the third report). Nothing was left unread; the answers were THROWN AWAY because each had to repeat its heading character for character, and past a quarter refused the whole edition went. `readEchoJudge(echo, list, i)` (server) is the ONE judge: `ok` (agrees with the row its key names — `readEchoScore` folds quotes, dashes, numbering and punctuation, then equal, one runs into the other, or `READ_ECHO_AGREE` 0.6 of the words shared), `shift` (agrees better with ANOTHER row of the WHOLE list — refused, the 11 Sep wall), `blind` (describes no row — the key decides; `R<n>` is HaTi's own address). **A CLAUSE LEFT WITHOUT A READING IS ASKED FOR AGAIN BY ITSELF**, once, in fresh pages carrying `idx` (the global index per row); `unmatched` is what is STILL missing; a page cut short whose missing clauses were then read is not `truncated`. The first pass's refusals are what `failed` names.
- **THE DOCUMENT TAB LANDS ON CONTRACT VIEW**: `applyWsTabs` resets the switch on ARRIVAL (`_docViewAt` = contract|tab; `docViewLeave` from `setView` on a change of page); a repaint keeps the reader's pick.
- **THE X-RAY MAP DRAWS THE MARKED CLAUSES ONLY** (`docXraySpineRows`); nothing marked, no map; `docXrayFollow` marks the nearest marked clause at or above the top of the sheet. The panel still lists every clause.
- **THE X-RAY FOLLOWS THE TEXT SIZE**: `applyDocZoom` writes the same `--doc-scale` on `#doc-xray`; five panel sizes are token × scale.
- **"SEND TO COUNTERPARTY" IS GONE** — the head's `share` rung is `noButton` (the guide stays); Share is the one door.
- **"AS AGREED" AND "WITH CHANGES" ARE GONE** from every page that draws the switch (negotiate, clause editor, their page): `rlReadSegsHtml` draws `marks` alone, the baseline line lost its press. The two renderers are kept whole and still pinned (f249, f250); `RL_READS` keeps three.
- **THE DROPDOWN SCROLLS**: the capture-phase scroll listener lets a scroll INSIDE `.hati-selmenu` through; the list is `overscroll-behavior:contain`.
- **PARTIES**: our row's email is the contract OWNER's, looked up off the member list (`partyOurEmail`), never stored or typed; a stored one wins. The town box is gone from the party form and the row (older records keep the field).
- **WHO ELSE FILLS ITSELF** (`participantsAuto`, a READING): colleagues who filed a change on our side (this round and closed ones), edited the wording (audit `Edited`/`Document`/`Corrected`) or approved (`approvalChain`, `c.approval`) — members only, one row per person with every role, a role a hand-named row carries not said twice, marked `added by HaTi`, grants nothing. Taking one off is `c.participantsAutoOff` (member ids, the one write). Drawn only where the caller passes `auto` (the Overview).
Tests: f369, nine-jobs-verify; f300 (2)(4), f84, f172 re-pointed in place.
