Line numbers were verified 2026-08-03. Code moves — treat them as starting points, re-verify with grep, and UPDATE THIS MAP when the layout changes.

## THE NEW AGREEMENT POP-UP IS AN ASK ACROSS THE TOP, A RAIL AND THE QUESTIONS — NO PAPER (proposal C, 22 Sep 2026; the paper came off and the ask moved up, 23 Sep 2026)

*"please redisign this pop up so that it is not so congested"* → proposal C (the picker a RAIL of rows). Then, the morning after D stacked the paper under the questions below 1280, the owner's iPad Pro 12.9" (1366 wide) drew three columns — exactly what the line said — and he ruled: *"Actually remove paper from the pop up entirely and in any type of computer"*, and off four renders, option 1: *"the describe what you need maybe takes the whole top line"*. **C's paper column and D's stack are both REVERSED**; their reasoning is kept in js/wizard.js and MAP-HISTORY because it is what makes this safe.
- **NO PAPER ON THIS SCREEN, AT ANY WIDTH, BY ONE LINE**: `hostOpts.paperHost` is `null`. All three mounted forms (wizardFormMount, openContractEssentials, openTemplateFillModal) ask for a host before they draw an agreement, so **their own dialogs still draw it** beside the questions from `fillPreviewFits()`'s 1000 — whether it goes from those too is the owner's OPEN question. The wide/stacked gates and `NA_PAPER_MIN_W` · `NA_PAPER_W` · `NA_FRAME_W` · `NA_STACK_MIN_W` are GONE, not dormant (a branch that can never fire is a guard that is always false), with `#na-paper`, `#na-right`, `.na-wide`, `.na-stack` and `--na-paper-w`. `na_note_wide` / `na_note_stack` are STALE, inert in both books; `na_note` says only *"Copilot reads the agreement on arrival."* — there is no blank to light.
- **TWO NUMBERS, SAID ONCE**: `NA_RAIL_W` 260 (mirrored as `--na-rail-w`; f345 asserts them EQUAL) and `NA_FRAME_NARROW_W` 900, the frame at every window.
- **THE ASK TAKES THE WHOLE TOP LINE**: `.na-field` and `#dr-out` are direct children of `#na-body`, before `#wz-pick`, spanning both columns; the ask is dressed exactly as `.na-card` (f368 asserts the relation — it is a field, not a band). `#dr-say` went from 182 to ~750px and Find sits beside it; the rail-only `.na-rail .na-row` rules are GONE. `.na-body > #dr-out:empty{display:none}`, or an empty answer adds the body's 20px gap.
- **ONE ROW BUILDER FOR THREE SHELVES** (`pickRow`; `.na-door` / `.na-chip` GONE) and **IT IS THE TEMPLATES PAGE'S OWN RAIL** — unchanged from C.
- **THE LIST SCROLLS INSIDE THE RAIL ON A DERIVED CAP**: `max(140px, calc(88vh - 350px))`, RE-DERIVED when the ask left the rail and AGAIN at the merge, when main took "Upload it" off the row under the list (51 → 28, Import alone). Every part is listed beside the rule (edge 2 · head 69 · foot 55 · padding 36 · ask 126 · gap 20 · rail gap 12 · Import row 28 · margin 2), the same at 1100/1280/1440/1920 and both iPads, English and Swedish, Copilot on or off — **and f368 checks the listed parts ADD UP to the number the rule uses**. Each shelf's heading PINS.
- **THE ASK GROWS WITH THE SENTENCE** (`naSayFit`, bounded at `NA_SAY_MAX_LINES` 7) — at 750px a one-line request has nothing to grow into, so new-agreement-verify 3a2 types three lines.
- **A SHORT SCREEN SCROLLS THE BODY, MEASURED AND SAID**: the ask above both columns takes 146px off the questions card's room, so the body scrolls by 47px at 1440×900, 105 at 1194×834, 135 at 800 tall; 0 at 1920×1080 and at the owner's 1366×1024. Create is outside the scroller and the Import row is on screen on a fresh open.
Tests: f368 (was f365, renamed and reversed in place; 30 claims, **21 red at the parent**, the 9 that pass are named walls and controls), f360 and f345 reversed in place, new-agreement-verify (42 checks, **12 red at the parent** — at 1366 it prints `3 cols · 1180px`, the owner's report). paper-beside-questions-verify 7c/7g red on BOTH sides (pre-existing).

## THE CONTRACT TYPE IS A CHOICE, AND THE WORDS ARE ENGLISH (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026)

*"make the contract type on the overview a dropdown too"* — the morning after the same ask for a party's role word. **THE TWO ARE NOT THE SAME FIELD AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE RULING.**
- **THE FIELD STAYS FREE TEXT ON THE RECORD** (`type:'text'`), because `openMetaReview` and the upload confirm both branch on `type` and a closed list there would DROP whatever Copilot read off the paper. What it gains is **`picks`** — "free text, and here are words to offer" — so the extraction review and the upload confirm are byte-identical.
- **THE WORDS ARE ENGLISH, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE PARTY ROLE.** A party's role word is what THE PAPER calls them, so offering Swedish is right there. This one is a MATCHING KEY: `playbookKeyFor` lowercases it, `copilotPlaybookKey` mirrors it, and f133 requires both hosts to answer the same key — a translated word matches none of the patterns and takes the baseline book instead of the right one, in silence. The RECORD-keeps-English rule, in its third costume.
- **THE TWELVE ARE READ OFF `TEMPLATES`, NEVER TYPED** (`contractTypeKinds`, with `CONTRACT_TYPE_FALLBACK` as the literal for a stage without js/templates.js — `docLibWording`'s own idiom, pinned as a no-drift wall). They are the same words `cKind` writes onto every drafted contract, so an uploaded contract and a drafted one spell one type one way. **EVERY ONE OF THEM RESOLVES TO A REAL PLAYBOOK** and none falls to the baseline by accident — f361 (2) is that measurement, and it is what the English choice rests on.
- **IT OFFERS AND NEVER REFUSES**: the blank leads (clearing a term is an answer), whatever is on the record LEADS the words where it is not one of them (folded, so a reading is never quietly replaced), and the last row types the document's own words through `promptNewName`. `metaPickOptions` is the ONE reading behind all three, so a second `picks` field cannot drift.
- **A SENTINEL NEVER REACHES THE RECORD, AND THE WALL IS IN THE HANDLER** — there is no Save on this grid, every box writes as it is answered, so a cancelled name box returns before anything is written and puts the value back, as `bindFolderSelect` puts a stream back. **THE EVENT IS THE ELEMENT'S**: a `picks` field is text on the record and a SELECT on the screen, and a select answers on `change`.
Tests: f361 (20 claims, **18 red at the parent**; the two that pass are named `[wall]`), overview-as-drawn-verify 14 (7 checks driven with a real pointer, **all 7 red at the parent**, which prints `<INPUT>`). f351 (5) re-pointed in place — it pinned the ternary's own spelling, and **`wireKeyTerms` carries TWO `const evt=` lines**, so the region is the metadata sweep's own. That run's *noticed, not fixed* — `Edit these details` dead on a first paint — was **FIXED the same day**; see THE OVERVIEW'S ACTS WORK ON A FIRST PAINT below.

## THE OVERVIEW'S ACTS WORK ON A FIRST PAINT (Young ruled 22 Sep 2026)

*"fix the edit button bug too."* MEASURED in a real browser on an ordinary contract: open the Overview, press `Edit these details`, **ZERO boxes**; fold any section and press again, **24**. The button was drawn, visible and hit-testable the whole time.
- **ONE STRUCTURAL ODDITY, AND IT IS THE WHOLE DEFECT.** The stack has three hosts and two of them — `#kt-ov-lead`, `#kt-side` — are EMPTY SLOTS their painter fills when the tab is shown. `#kt-ov-terms` interpolated `ktOverviewTermsHtml` straight into the room's template instead, so its markup arrived **without `renderKeyTerms` ever running** — and that function is where every door on the card is wired: Edit these details, Move to another stream, a marked field's Fix, the signers row, the people list, and `wireKeyTerms`' own boxes. A fold calls it through `sectionWire`'s router, which is why a fold woke it and why this had never been seen.
- **THE FIX IS THE PRODUCT'S OWN IDIOM, TWELVE LINES ABOVE IT**: `#kt-triage-slot` is already noted as *"A SLOT, NOT THE STRIP ITSELF"*, with a painter, for exactly this shape of fault. The host becomes a slot, `applyWsTabs`' terms branch paints it beside the sibling painter it already called, and that host has **ONE WRITER** rather than two. No flash — `applyWsTabs` runs synchronously at the end of `wireWsTabs`, in the same tick as the template.
- **THE STANDALONE `wireKtRows` CALL IS GONE, NOT MOVED.** It was the hand-patched symptom — somebody had noticed the rows were dead and armed that one by name. `renderKeyTerms` calls it itself, and `wireKtRows` adds a listener per row **with no bound-once flag**, so leaving both would put two handlers on every row (a blur that writes twice). **ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING**: the side is painted first, so the painter's document-wide row sweep covers what the side drew.
- **`wireKtParties` IS SAFE ACROSS THE REPAINT** because it is delegated on the HOST and carries its own `dataset.pyBound` — `renderKeyTerms` writes the host's `innerHTML` and never replaces the element. Driven both ways to be sure.
- Two orphaned template locals (`ktEditable` / `ktReadable` at the room's own scope) went with it; the lint warning set is byte-identical.
Tests: f362 (12 claims, **7 red at the parent**; the two `[wall]`s pass on both sides by design), overview-as-drawn-verify **section 0** — which is FIRST in that file on purpose, because every other section folds something before it presses and a fold is exactly what used to hide this. `0a` prints `0 boxes before, 0 after` at the parent. `0b` is GATED on something having opened (ungated it passed there, on the fault). overview-as-drawn-verify 14's note re-pointed in place — it recorded this defect as reported-not-fixed, and a false note is worse than none.

## SEVEN FIXES OFF ONE REVIEW (Young's go on the before-and-after preview, 23 Sep 2026)

Diagnosed off a Warehousing agreement and a Maersk SaaS upload; the owner approved the preview and said *"fix all from 1 through 7 and merge to main."* Binding decisions: a highlight across two clauses never edits; the Document tab's highlight menu is untouched; every standard on Our standards is checked; a contract already negotiated keeps its clause split; Plain English starts the first time it is opened; governing law option A.

### 1 · WORD'S NUMBERS AND SPACES, EXACTLY AS WORD SHOWS THEM
- `docxNumbering(xml, stylesXml)` follows `w:numStyleLink`/`w:styleLink`; `docxStyleTable` / `docxStyleChain` walk `w:basedOn` (bounded at `DOCX_STYLE_HOPS` 20) for a style's numbering and outline level; `docxLevelForStyle` takes the level a list links to a style that names none; numbered paragraphs inside tables count in the body's own sequence (`docxTableHtml(tbl, report, numFn)`).
- A list HaTi cannot count all the way through draws NO numbers rather than shifted ones, counted on the file strip. Outline level 9 is body text.
- A space alone in its own bold/underlined run is KEPT (`RICH_KEEP_SPACE`, js/richdoc.js: the dress goes, the space stays — "Order.Unless" was the tell). Tests: f370, upload-structure-verify 8.
- PROVED ON THE OWNER'S REAL MAERSK FILE once it arrived: its "Ikke med" style is `basedOn` Heading 1 and overrides nothing, so Word numbers it in Heading 1's list — main read "48.2 Completion of performance of Project Phase", Word (and now HaTi) reads 57.2.

### 2 · A PAGE BANNER OVER NUMBERED CLAUSES IS A SECTION TITLE
- `_clBanners(blocks, headings)` (js/clausemodel.js): unnumbered headings ABOVE numbered clauses whose numbers run on across them are banners, and a banner rides with the clause after it (`sectionHtml`); `rlSectionHtml(cl)` draws it as a quiet `.rl-sect` title with no pencil on all three negotiate papers.
- ONCE STAMPED, THE IDS DECIDE: a stamped body is read by which headings carry ids, so a rename or insertion cannot flip it and a contract stamped the old way KEEPS its split. `clauseCarryIds` carries only onto headings the fresh read stamped, so Re-read on an un-negotiated contract adopts the new split. Tests: f371, upload-structure-verify.

### 3 · PREPARE REDLINES CHECKS EVERY STANDARD, AND SAYS WHAT IT CHECKED
- `pbStandardsFor(c)` (js/playbook.js) is the ONE list: every standard on Our standards (library positions folded with the book's; a range folds into its standard; the book decides how hard each is held). `pbStandardsSent` is what the route is sent, `pbAlignVerdicts` pairs answers back; both review branches stamp `std.label`.
- FOUR answers — aligned · deviation · missing · `na` (never a departure; `PB_SETTLED_STATUSES`). `pbReviewTokens` sizes the answer and the route says `truncated`; a cut-short or empty answer is REFUSED and never saved. "Every standard is met" only over a FINISHED check (`pbReviewComplete`); a saved check is reused only where finished and current (`pbReviewUsable`); every result names the count checked (`rlPbCheckedLine`, `rlPbOutcomeSay`). Tests: f372, prepare-redlines-verify 9.

### 4 · EDIT WITH COPILOT FROM ANY HIGHLIGHT ON THE NEGOTIATE PAGE
- `rlSelInFront(sel)`: a highlight with both ends in the paper's head or recital is the FRONT REGION's own words → Ask · Edit · Comment, through the editor's door.
- Across two clauses: Ask · Comment only, never Edit. **Ask opens the EDITING Copilot on the first clause** (`askAt`, `passageMode:'ask'`); `rlAskCopilotPanel` remains only where the editor cannot take the words. The Document tab is untouched. Tests: one-copilot-verify, f96, f304.

### 5 · ONE COPILOT LOOK IN THE CLAUSE EDITOR
- `ceRenderScope` draws ONE card, ALWAYS, in four states: the whole clause (`.is-clause`, the pencil), your words (Edit), a question (`.is-asking`), the whole contract (`.is-whole`, after the ✕ — `_ceWhole`, `ceSetWhole`). A probe for "the passage card" asks `.ce-scope:not(.is-clause):not(.is-whole)`. The greeting SENTENCE is gone, its rows stay.
- Whole-contract mode: `context.wholeDoc` lifts the chat routes' per-turn cap to `aiDocChars()` for the LAST user message only; answers name clauses and change no wording. Tests: one-copilot-verify; clause-editor-verify's card probes and redline-verify 3 re-pointed.

### 6 · PLAIN ENGLISH READS IN THE BACKGROUND, A CLAUSE AT A TIME
- `clause_reading_rows` keeps each clause the moment it is read, keyed `readRowHash(lang,row)` (kind · num · heading · text — never the page key); `readRowsKeep` is the ONE writer (INSERT OR IGNORE); deleted with the contract. A cut-short answer and a page more than a quarter misfiled are SHOWN, never kept. The whole-edition cache is unchanged, except one with `unmatched>0` is not served whole: the next press asks for the hole alone.
- THE READING IS A JOB (`_readJobs`, contract+lang): outlives the request; a second press on the same wording JOINS; different wording waits. A failed page is asked again ONCE; only a press where no page answered is refused (502); a tool block of any other name is no answer.
- `GET /api/contracts/:id/reading-progress?run=&from=` — NOT under /api/ai/, no limiter, no spend, scope-checked, about ONE press (`run`).
- THE PAGE: the switch is set FIRST and the column fills (`_docReadJobs`; `docReadPoll` only while `docReadWatching`); "Reading N of M" (`ct_read_progress`) in the head's moved slot; `.doc-read-wait` "Reading…" beside each clause still to come — its own class, placed in the entries' pass. A dropped connection waits on the server (`DOC_READ_WAIT_MS`), then asks once more. Tests: f373, reading-in-the-background-verify.

### 7 · THE CONTRACT'S OWN GOVERNING LAW UNDER THE TITLE
- The negotiate paper's sub-line read "Jurisdiction:" off the WORKSPACE market (`RL_REGION`, deleted). `contractGoverningLaw(c)` (js/metadata.js, published) is the ONE reading — `metadata.governingLaw`, what the Overview prints; nothing read → the line names the kind only, never a guess. `ng_paper_law` in both books; the law is the record's and is never translated. Tests: f374.

## THE PROTOTYPE'S FACES ACROSS THE PLATFORM (Young ruled 24 Sep 2026)

*"change the font in HaTi to match the prototype approach across the platform."* Three decisions, one token each: screens (`--font-heading`/`--font-body`) read **Geist**; figures (`--font-mono`) read Geist too, lined up by tabular digits, the typewriter look retired; the contract paper (`--font-doc`) reads **Source Serif 4**. `--font-code` keeps a true monospace (the fingerprint and the ⌘K chip).
- **IBM PLEX SANS IS NAMED SECOND AND fonts.css STAYS LINKED**: Geist carries no Greek; the browser falls back per glyph.
- **GEIST IS INLINED** (`fonts/geist.css`, ~90 KB, no flash); **SOURCE SERIF 4 IS FILES** (`fonts/source-serif-4.css` + one woff2 per subset and style, ~1 MB in all, fetched only as unicode-range asks). The /fonts route caches 30 days immutably: a changed face is a NEW FILE NAME.
- **EVERY HEADING ON THE PAPER READS THE PAPER'S FACE**: `.rl-paper :is(h1…h6)` and `.rl-paper-title` name `--font-doc` (a clause heading is an h4 and took the shell's heading face); the kicker is a label and reads `--font-body`. A contract's own design still wins (those rules use `!important` and a wider scope).
- The edition (Plain English) still measures its face off the sheet (11 Sep ruling), so it reads the serif too. Emails and the cohort pack name Geist first and fall back to the system sans (they cannot load a face).
- MEASURED: first ink unmoved on the Document tab (271.9) and up 1px on the negotiate page (287.7 → 286.7).
Tests: f377 (9 of 10 red at the parent; the monospace is the WALL), type-and-symbols-verify section 1 reversed in place.

## SIX OFF FIVE IMAGES (Young ruled 24 Sep 2026)

*"Remove the value stream column from both pages as well but not from the filter … make the DNA strand to cover until the bottom of the screen. It should not extend past the length of the screen … the why it matters should be in bold font … the exit focus esc should not move with you to other pages. It should stay where the focus mode is."*
- **HOME WAS REVERTED THE SAME DAY** (Young: *"Revert just the home page to how it was before"*). Images 2 and 3 — the decision card as the graph alone, grown to fill the screen — were built, merged and undone: Home's code is byte-identical to the day before, and the rows, `hmFitDecisions`, the four-row floor, "sorted by what closes first", the rail standing down where nothing is dated and the quiet desk's one number are all back. **Do not rebuild the runway-only card without asking.** (A different Home, the Map, was built on the owner's own word the same day — see THE MAP ON HOME.)
- **NO STREAM COLUMN, EITHER SEAT**: `stream` is off `REG_COL_KEYS` / `REG_COL_KEYS_NEGO`; `CELL.stream`, its head and `.reg-tick` are deleted; the 13 points went to the give column (counterparty 26 → 39) so the five shared columns still agree. **The Stream FILTER (`#reg-type-sel`) and the sort by stream (the dropdown; `REG_CMP.stream`) stay.** The Negotiations band row spans `REG_COL_KEYS_NEGO.length`, never a literal. A stored width array one entry too long is ignored (`regColWidths`), so nobody's table shifts.
- **THE X-RAY STRAND ENDS WHERE THE COLUMN ENDS**: a block is a SHARE of the strip, `flex:var(--xr-w) 0 0px` with `--xr-w` = `docXraySegH(words)`; the 23 Sep floor (`XR_SEG_MIN`, inline `min-height`) stays, so only a contract with more marked clauses than fit at 16px (about 29 on a laptop) still scrolls — the one place the two rulings meet, said to the owner.
- **"WHY IT MATTERS:" IS BOLD** in both homes — `.doc-xr-why b` and `#brief-section .br-why b` read `--w-strong`.
- **THE ROOM'S FOCUS CHIP BELONGS TO THE ROOM**: `wsFocusChip` mounts `#ws-focus-out` in `#content` (replaced by every navigation), never `document.body`, and draws only while `wsFocusHere()` (view `workspace` or `doc`); the room's Escape answers only there too. The MODE stays with its contract (`_wsFocusFor`), so coming back finds it on, chip and all. The negotiate page's own exit (`.rl-focus-exit`) was always inside its page.
- **ONE COLOUR NET MOVED, OWNED, NOT RE-RECORDED**: theme-tokens-verify's register screens lose the stream bars; no colour was changed and no baseline re-recorded (BUGLOG). The dashboard findings went with the Home revert.
Tests: f379 (10 claims, 7 red at the parent; two walls and a control), six-off-five-images-verify (17 checks, 7 red at the parent — it prints the chip on the Contracts page); re-pointed in place: f97, f184, f240, f281, f350, f367 (3), contracts-page-verify 3a–3c/11a/11d/18a/18c2, counterparty-leads-verify 2a/2f, negotiations-door-verify 9, signed-and-columns-verify, register-foot-verify 1h. The Home re-points (f3, f252, f363, f56, f66, home-page-verify, runway-and-xray-verify, auto-triage-verify) are back as they were.

## THE MAP ON HOME — THE TILES LEFT, AND NEEDS YOUR DECISION CAME BACK WITH TWO ROWS (Young ruled 24–25 Sep 2026)

Picked by name off three drawn options ("Executive Home Options"), then *"make it so you have a toggle for review in cash or in quantity"*, *"instead of needs your decision, delete it and replace with prepared for you"*, *"Build it and merge to main"*. The next day: *"reduce the where your contracts stand card by 25% … The color code of the drafting, review and executed should match the color coding in the contracts list page. And below prepared for you card, add bring back the needs your attention card but only have 2 lines and nothing more."*
- **THE DESKTOP HOME IS**: the greeting, the Map (`#hm-map`), Prepared for you when something is prepared, and **Needs your decision** (back since 25 Sep — the approved reference's own name; "Needs your attention" is the Settings page's block). The four tiles and *Choose tiles* (`#kpi-customize`, `openKpiCustomizer`, `kpiApply` — gone) stay gone. **THE PHONE IS UNTOUCHED**: its tiles and sheet still read `KPI_CATALOG`, `currentKpiSel`, `KPI_MAX` — the one picker left.

Only AIR and CHART HEIGHT came out (paddings, gaps, stage bar 38 → 22, plot 168 → 102, the legend's figure and unit on one line `.hm-map-lf`, which WRAPS rather than cutting); every figure, door and the switch are still drawn and the figures keep their type sizes. home-page-verify 3b measures against the parent's own number.

- **NEEDS YOUR DECISION, TWO ROWS AND NOTHING MORE (25 Sep)**: `hmDecisionItems(S, deskRows)` is the ONE reading (the rows, the head's count, the See all door); `HM_DD_ROWS = 2` on every screen — a number, not a measurement (`hmFitDecisions` stays gone); the head says "N items · sorted by what closes first" and "See all N" only where more wait than show (`regShowOnly` onto the list's contracts); a row opens its contract; empty is one line (`.hm-card .hm-empty` draws no frame of its own); NO RAIL. The desk's eviction is back (`deskCids(shown)` — the renewal source only, the rows the desk DRAWS). The two bell rows stay: the bell says everything owed, the card the first two.
- **COUNTING IS NOT DRAWING**: `hmMapData(sl)` counts, `hmMapInnerHtml(d, mode)` draws, `hmMapWire` binds once per element. Stages are `hmDashSlices`' own; a month is where a term ENDS by `renewalWindow`, its pile the renewal card's decision (made · due · later); the box is `RENEWAL_WINDOW_DAYS`; the side facts borrow `exposureData`'s lead, `hmOwed` (shared with the phone's owed tile) and the turnaround's own `cycleIds`.
- **COUNT AT REST; VALUE IS A PERSON'S CHOICE** (`hmMeasure` / `hmSetMeasure`, `hati.v1.homeMeasure.<uid>`, in this browser). No `canViewValues`, no switch. Money goes through `fxHome` and what has no rate is counted (`fx_left_out`). The switch repaints the card's INSIDE only and gives the pressed half back its focus.

- **TWO REMINDERS SAID NOWHERE ELSE RIDE THE BELL**: `desk-quiet` (a negotiation past the standard, its age `dk_stale_tag` leading the sub-line) and `desk-join`, off `myStaleDesks` / `myJoinAsks` — the card's own two sources.
- **DORMANT, KEPT**: `js/runway.js` (the card came back WITHOUT its rail), `rowsThatFit` on Home (the list is two rows, not fitted). No band was added.
- **ONE COLOUR NET MOVED, OWNED, NOT RE-RECORDED**: theme-tokens-verify's two dashboard lines (the Map's shades in, a removed tile's ruby out — audited as a set, stable over two recordings); the baseline edit was refused in this session, so it is listed in BUGLOG. Every other browser file fails exactly as it does on main.

## TWO COPIES OF ONE CONTRACT — THE WORKING COPY AND THE SIGNING COPY (Young ruled 25 Sep 2026, picks 1A 2B 3A 4A 5A 6A)

*"When a contract goes to the document page, the negotiation page as well, it should look like its on Microsoft Word waiting to be edited. But when you go to the signing page it should look exactly like how it was designed."*
- **`js/pages.js` IS THE ONE PAGE-MAKER UNDER BOTH COPIES.** The sheet stays ONE element with exactly the markup it held: a block that would cross a page is pushed down by `margin-top` (a batch-measured correction pass beats margin collapse), and a layer (`[data-pg-layer]`, aria-hidden, its words generated content through `attr()`) paints the grey gap, Word's corner marks and each page's head and foot. Nothing is inserted into the wording, so every reading that walks the painted page (Plain English, X-ray, sign spots, note marks, the field link, a highlight) finds what it found. `pagesPlan` is PURE; a heading keeps with the WHOLE next block; **a block taller than a page GROWS its page — wording is never cut**. `pagesWatch` re-breaks on a resize (`PG_WATCH_MS`); `.pg-sheet::after{height:var(--pg-extra)}` makes the last page whole (never the inline padding — a `padding` shorthand holding a var() answers '' for its longhand).
- **THE WORKING COPY** (Document tab; Negotiate page through `rlPaginate` from `negoAfterPaint`; the clause editor is NOT paged): white pages, `PG_GAP` 20, corners, the letterhead's name FADED in the top margin (`pagesLetterheadName`, 2B), "Page k of n" faded in the foot (3A). Always ONE column; the design lends its typeface only.
- **THE SIGNING COPY** (the Signing tab, every tab once Signed — `docCopyOf`; their signing link; the printed PDF): a fixed 794px A4 page (`PG_SIGN_W/H`, `PG_SIGN_PAD`), the design at full strength — the letterhead on page one, `.pg-run` on pages 2+, the design's footer carrying the number on EVERY page (`pagesSignFoot`); two-column dropped; fields as words and an empty one a ruled line (`docSignBodyHtml`); always white (light tokens pinned on `.pg-sheet.pg-sign`). The size control ZOOMS by transform (`scApplyZoom`, `SC_ZOOMS`, `hati.v1.signZoom`, default fit) and never re-breaks (4A). `docSheetHtml` is the ONE builder per copy; `docRepaintSheet` swaps them on the tab change and re-arms the canvas's listeners.
- **THE PLACES TO SIGN ARE ON THE SIGNING COPY ONLY** (5A): `wireDocCanvas` paints spots and `pagesSignFlags` ("Sign here"; our line index 0, theirs 1) only where `docCopyOf(c)==='sign'`.
- **THEIR PAPER NEVER WEARS OURS** (`signCopyTheirs`): an unedited PDF or scan → their file in the browser's viewer plus an UNNUMBERED signature page (`data-pg-sigpage`); a Word file → `report.layout` (`docxLayoutOf` / `docxReadLayout`: page size, margins, face, size, header, footer — nothing guessed); an edited PDF → the agreed words on a plain page, said in the control row (`sc_src_rebuilt`, 6A).
- **PRINT**: `pagesPrintPages` rebuilds one `.pp-page` box per screen page with the same furniture, plus `@page{size:A4;margin:0}` for the print only (`pagesPrintPageRule`); `fillPrintRoot` and the portal's `exportPDF` use it for the signing copy.
- **REFUSAL 3, SAID OUT LOUD**: the Document tab's first line is unmoved (271.9 — and with a design it moved UP 80px, 352.1 → 272.9); the Negotiate page's is unmoved; **the Signing tab's sits 30px lower** (271.9 → 301.9), the signing page's own 64px top margin against the working copy's 34px. No chrome grew (signing-on-paper-verify 1c re-pointed to that relation). The owner's to rule on.
Tests: f383 (42 claims, 38 red at the parent; the four that pass are named walls), two-copies-verify (28 checks in a real browser; `HATI_SHOT_DIR` for its pictures). **REVERSED IN PLACE by this ruling**: F89 (2)'s flat, warm sheet and redline-verify 2's "it is FLAT" (the Negotiate page's paper is the working copy — white, its own edge, the page shadow, asserted as the three tokens); redline-verify 2b counts gaps between clauses on ONE page (a page break is told apart by the page-maker's own record, never by its size). **Re-pointed**: signing-on-paper-verify 1c (see refusal 3), f225's region (the canvas lives in `docSheetHtml` now — anchor on `#doc-sheet-host`), f278 (1) (a third scroll listener, the signing copy's page number, named). The new Word names sit at the END of docx.js's publish lists (f366 pins `{docxStyledIsWording,` as the first).
